34.09   Sexual harassment
   
34.10   Retirement
   
34.11   Health and life insurance
   
34.12   Employment at will
   
34.13   Uniform allowance
   
34.14   Additional expenses
   
34.15   Personal days
   
34.16   Longevity pay
   
34.17   Pay in lieu of excess vacation
   
34.18   Retirees insurance
§ 34.01 INTRODUCTION; DEFINITION.
   (A)   (1)   This personnel policy is applicable to all full-time City
employees and elected officials who are not covered by a collective bargaining
agreement, unless otherwise noted.
      (2)   With regard to police and fire employees, the provision of the
state law regarding insurance, discipline and dismissal are incorporated by
reference herein as are all operating regulations of the Board of Public Works
and Safety as they relate to the conduct of those two departments.
   (B)   All employees to whom the policy applies, working an average of thirty
(30) hours per week for twenty-six (26) consecutive weeks shall be deemed full-
time employees and receive all benefits provided in this policy. All other
employees shall be deemed part-time employees and shall not receive benefits
hereunder.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2013-10, passed 10-3-13)
§ 34.02 WAGES; PAY DAY.
   (A)   The wage rates which the City shall be obligated to pay non-fire and
non-police employees are as determined by the Mayor and Board of Public Works
and Safety, not to exceed the salary ordinance caps as set by the Common
Council. The wage rate which the City shall be obligated to pay police and fire
employees shall be as set by the Common Council in the salary ordinance.
   (B)   The City of Boonville shall have a bi-weekly pay period. Pay shall be
made to employees not later than 4:00 p.m., on Friday. If an employee's
paycheck is short by twenty-five dollars ($25.00) or more on regular, non-
overtime wages, or seventy-five dollars ($75.00) or more on overtime wages,
such employee shall be issued a supplemental check making up the shortfall, by
the close of business of the 3rd working day after the shortfall is identified,
and brought to the attention of the Department Head. In all other instances,
the shortfall shall be included in the next regular wage paycheck.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord.
2001-10, passed 7-12-01; Am. Ord. 2003-9, approved 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2013-1,
passed 2-7-13)
§ 34.03 HOURS AND OVERTIME.
   (A)   (1)   The work week for employees, other than police, fire, elected
officials and supervisory employees deemed exempt under the Fair Labor
Standards Act shall begin at 12:01 a.m. Monday and end Sunday night, midnight.
Employees shall receive a fifteen-minute break in the morning and a fifteen-
minute break in the afternoon.
      (2)   The words "work week," except for police, fire, elected officials
and supervisory employees deemed exempt under the Fair Labor Standards Act, as
used in this policy shall be the period of seven (7) days commencing at the
starting time of the employee's first scheduled work shift after 12:00 midnight
Sunday. The work week shall consist of five (5) consecutive work days of eight
(8) hours each, or forty (40) hours per week.
      (3)   The phrase "work day," except for police, fire, elected officials
and supervisory employees deemed exempt under the Fair Labor Standards Act, as
used herein shall be defined as a twenty-four (24) hour period starting with
the employee's scheduled starting time and ending twenty-four (24) hours
thereafter. The basic work day shall consist of eight (8) hours of work,
exclusive of lunch periods. Lunch periods shall be scheduled by the City and
shall consist of sixty (60) minutes. Provided, further, the basic work day
shall consist of eight (8) consecutive hours for those employees who remain on
duty constantly during such eight (8) hour period.
      (4)   For non-police and non-fire employees, the work day begins at 7:00
or 8:00 a.m. and ends at 4:00 or 5:00 p.m. as directed by the Mayor. Police and
fire employee shifts are as determined by their respective department.
      (5)   All hours worked in excess of forty (40) hours in any work week,
except police, fire, elected officials and supervisory employees deemed exempt
under the Fair Labor Standards Act, shall be paid at the rate of one and one-
half (1½) times the regular hourly rate. For the purpose of this provision
only, time not worked in any work week because of a holiday or the observance
of a holiday which otherwise would have been a scheduled work day, shall be
counted as hours worked in computing said forty (40) hours. Absences for which
an employee is authorized by the employer and for which the employee is
compensated and which otherwise would have been a scheduled work day shall be
counted as time worked for the purposes of this provision only.
      (6)   Overtime shall not be paid twice for the same hours worked. There
shall be no pyramiding of overtime.
   (B)   Firefighters shall receive overtime for all time worked in excess of
twenty-four (24) hours per workday. When a firefighter is required to work
overtime, firefighter shall be paid for time worked at an hourly rate equal to
one and one-half (1½) times the employee's regular rate of pay. The regular
hourly rate shall be the firefighter's base pay. Compensatory time shall not be
available, nor shall overtime be carried from year to year. The Fire Chief is
required to develop a policy ensuring all firefighters will receive equal
opportunities to work available overtime. The policy shall be in writing and
available to all eligible employees. Overtime section shall be based upon
staffing requirements, as determined by the Fire Chief.
   (C)   All payments for overtime compensation together with other fringe
benefits shall be in addition to the annual compensation and shall not be
considered as part of the compensation set out in the salary ordinance.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord.
2001-10, passed 7-12-01; Am. Ord. 2002-7, passed 9-3-02; Am. Ord. 2003-9,
passed 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2013-1, passed 2-7-13)
§ 34.04 HOLIDAYS.
   (A)   (1)   The City shall observe the following holidays: New Year's Day,
Martin Luther King, Jr.'s Birthday, Presidents Day, Memorial Day, Independence
Day, Labor Day, Veterans Day, Thanksgiving Day, Friday after Thanksgiving,
Christmas Eve day, Good Friday, Christmas Day, election days. In those years
where no elections occur, election day holidays shall be observed on the Monday
before the Tuesday when such election would have occurred if elections were
held.
      (2)   When any of the above holidays fall on a Sunday, the holiday shall
be observed on the Monday following; and when on a Saturday, the holiday shall
be observed on the Friday prior thereto.
      (3)   The employees covered by this division shall be allowed eight (8)
hours pay for each of the above holidays, or the day on which said holiday is
observed by the City as set out above.
      (4)   Employees covered by this division who are required to work on any
of the aforesaid holidays or observance days shall receive a substitute day off
for the holiday.
   (B)   Full-time firefighters shall receive, in lieu of the holiday observed
by other employees, payment in the sum of one hundred dollars ($100.00) gross,
per holiday. Such sum shall be paid as the holiday occurs within the current
pay period.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord.
2001-10, passed 7-12-01; Am. Ord. 2002-7, passed 9-3-02; Am. Ord. 2003-9,
passed 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2013-1, passed 2-7-13)
§ 34.05 VACATIONS.
   (A)   (1)   All full-time employees on the active payroll of the City, other
than elected officials and police officers, who are covered by a collective
bargaining agreement, are covered by this policy, and who have been actively at
work sixty percent (60%) or more of the total working days during the calendar
year, shall be entitled to earn paid vacation which will be credited on January
1st of the following year of full-time employment with the City of Boonville.
For example, an employee who begins work after January 1st of a given year
shall not be credited with a week of paid vacation, until January 1st of the
following year; an employee who begins work on January 1st will be credited
with a week of vacation effective January 1st of the following year. The earned
paid vacation, following, does not apply to elected officials, full-time police
(who are covered by a collective bargaining agreement) and full-time
firefighters:
         (a)   After one (1) year of continuous service - 1 week (40 hours);
         (b)   After two (2) years of continuous service - 2 weeks (80 hours);
         (c)   After five (5) years of continuous service - 3 weeks (120
hours); and
         (d)   After eight (8) years of continuous service - 4 weeks (160
hours).
      (2)   The earned paid vacation is as follows for all full-time
firefighters, with exception of the Fire Chief, who shall earn the paid
vacation as stated above, due to the recognition of the City of his/her duties
and work schedule:
         (a)   After one (1) year of continuous service - 3 working shifts;
         (b)   After two (2) years of continuous service - 6 working shifts;
         (c)   After five (5) years of continuous service - 9 working shifts;
and
         (d)   After eight (8) years of continuous service - 12 working shifts.
      (3)   All full-time employees on the active payroll of the City, other
than elected officials and police officers shall receive one (1) additional
working shift per year for each subsequent year after their 10th year of
completed service, not retroactive.
      (4)   All vacation shall be taken in blocks of time of not less than four
(4) hours. Vacation not used within the calendar year it is accrued, shall be
forfeited. Pursuant to division (E), the employee may elect to be paid for
vacation within the calendar year, not previously used by the employee. Any
employee, who is unable to work due to a job related injury, shall continue to
accrue vacation until employment is terminated.
   (B)   An employee who leaves the employ of the City for any reason prior to
using earned vacation shall receive payment in lieu thereof at the straight
time rate for the unused but earned vacation on a pro-rata basis of one-twelfth
(1/12) earned for each month worked for which no vacation has been taken.
   (C)   The City shall schedule the number of employees in each Department who
shall receive vacations at a particular time. The vacation period of each
employee shall be set with due regard to the desire, and preference of the
employees, consistent with the efficient operation of the City's business.
Vacations shall be scheduled on a year-round basis. Under no circumstances will
the City allow more than fifty percent (50%) of the employees of a department
to be on vacation at any one time.
   (D)   An employee, upon giving a reasonable notice of not less than two (2)
weeks to the City, shall be given his vacation pay before starting his
vacation. It is understood that during the first year of employment, the
employee must work sixty percent (60%) of the total working days in order to
obtain his vacation and must have been employed for the full year. During the
second and subsequent years, the employee must have worked sixty percent (60%)
of the total working days of the year, but need not be employed for the full
year to be eligible for the vacation. No more than one vacation will be earned
in any twelve (12) month period.
   (E)   Any full-time employee of the City of Boonville, who has accumulated
up to eighty (80) hours of vacation time, shall be required to take up to
eighty (80) hours of vacation time during his or her twelve (12) month calendar
year employment period. Any vacation time accrued, in excess of eighty (80)
hours, may be taken in hourly pay, payable at straight time rate, in lieu of
time off.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord.
2003-9, passed 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2009-6, passed 3-5-09; Am. Ord. 2013-1, passed
2-7-13)
§ 34.06 LEAVES OF ABSENCE.
   (A)   Sick leave.
      (1)   An employee covered by this policy, except elected officials, shall
be compensated for time lost due to illness or injury pursuant to this section.
The employees covered by this agreement shall be entitled to receive
compensation for time lost due to illness or injury incurred while on duty for
a total of six (6) months per calendar year. For injury or illness incurred off
duty each employee shall be entitled to three (3) months sick leave in any
calendar year, provided, however, that the Board of Public Works and Safety may
grant up to an additional three (3) months sick leave for those employees under
its jurisdiction for a nonwork related injury or illness.
      (2)   Any employee who, at the end of the sick leave period, is still
unable to return to work shall not continue on the payroll of the City, subject
to family and medical leave requirements set out in this chapter, and shall be
transferred to a non-paid disabled employee list. No employee placed on
disability shall return to active employment, with the City until fully
released to do so by an appropriate health care provider with such release to
be in writing. The City shall not be required to preserve a job for an employee
who is transferred to the disabled list, except as provided under the family
and medical leave provision.
      (3)   To be eligible for sick leave, an employee must report an illness
or injury to the City prior to the time scheduled to work. If an employee is
absent for more than three (3) consecutive days due to illness or injury, the
employee shall furnish a doctor's certificate stating that the employee has
been ill or injured, was under the doctor's treatment and was unable to work
because of said illness or injury. Where advance notice is not possible, the
employee will be considered to have provided proper notice by informing the
City of the anticipated date of return. Otherwise, the employee will be
expected to notify the City daily of the absence. Any employee off work on
extended sick leave shall, when directed by the appropriate authority, submit
necessary medical information, including physician opinions, justifying the
employee's absence.
      (4)   Misuse of sick leave benefits will result in the loss of pay for
those days for which sick leave was misused and may result in termination of
the employee.
   (B)   Family and medical leave.
      (1)   Definitions.
      ELIGIBLE EMPLOYEE. An employee who has been employed:
         (a)   For at least twelve (12) months by the City with respect to whom
leave is requested under §
34.03 (A)(2); and
         (b)   For at least one thousand two hundred fifty (1,250) hours of
service with the City during the previous twelve (12)-month period.
      EMPLOYMENT BENEFITS. All benefits provided or made available to employees
by the City, including group life insurance, group health insurance, disability
insurance, sick leave, annual leave, educational benefits and pensions,
regardless of whether such benefits are provided by a practice or written
policy of the City or through an “employee benefit plan,” as defined in Section
3 of the Employee Retirement Income Security Act of 1974 [29 USC 1002 (3)].
      HEALTH CARE PROVIDER.
         (a)   A doctor of medicine or osteopathy who is authorized to practice
medicine or surgery by the State of Indiana; or
         (b)   Any person determined by the United States Secretary of Labor to
be capable of providing health care services.
      PARENT. The biological parent of any employee or an individual who stood
in loco parentis to an employee when the employee was a son or daughter.
      REDUCED LEAVE SCHEDULE. A leave schedule that reduces the usual number of
hours per work week, or hours per workday of an employee.
      SERIOUS HEALTH CONDITION. An illness, injury, impairment of physical or
mental condition that involves:
         (a)   Inpatient care in a hospital, hospice or residential medical
care facility; or
         (b)   Continuing treatment by a health care provider.
      SON or DAUGHTER. A biological, adopted or foster child, stepchild, a
legal ward or a child of a person standing in loco parentis, who is:
         (a)   Under 18 years of age; or
         (b)   18 years of age or older and incapable of self care because of a
mental or physical disability.
      SPOUSE. A husband or wife, as the case may be.
      (2)   Leave requirement.
         (a)   In general.
            1.   Entitlement to leave. Subject to §
34.03 (A)(3), an eligible employee shall be entitled to a total of 12 work
weeks of leave during any 12-month period for one or more of the following:
               a.   Because of the birth of a son or daughter of the employee
and to care for such son or daughter.
               b.   Because of the placement of a son or daughter with the
employee for adoption or foster care.
               c.   In order to care for the spouse, or a son, daughter or
parent, of the employee, if such spouse, son, daughter or parent has a serious
health condition.
               d.   Because of a serious health condition that makes the
employee unable to perform the functions of the position of such employee.
            2.   Expiration of entitlement. The entitlement to leave under
divisions c. and d. above for a birth or placement of a son or daughter shall
expire at the end of the 12-month period beginning on the date of such birth or
placement.
         (b)   Leave taken intermittently or on reduced leave schedule.
            1.   In general. Leave under division (B)(2)(a)1., divisions a. and
b., shall not be taken by an employee intermittently or on a reduced leave
schedule unless the employee and the City agree otherwise. Subject to divisions
(B)(2)(e)2. and (B)(3)(b)5., leave under (B)(2)(a)1., divisions c. and d., may
be taken intermittently or on a reduced leave schedule when medically
necessary. The taking of leave intermittently or on a reduced leave schedule
pursuant to this paragraph shall not result in a reduction in the total amount
of leave to which the employee is entitled under division(a) above beyond the
amount of leave actually taken.
            2.   Alternative position. If an employee requests intermittent
leave or leave on a reduced leave schedule under division (B)(2)(a)1.,
divisions c. and d., that is foreseeable based on planned medical treatment,
the City may require such employee to transfer temporarily to an available
alternative position offered by the City for which the employee is qualified
and that:
               a.   Has equivalent pay and benefits; and
               b.   Better accommodates recurring periods of leave than the
regular employment position of the employee.
      (c)   Unpaid leave permitted. Except as provided in division (d) below,
leave granted under division (a) above shall consist of unpaid leave.
         (d)   Relationship to paid leave.
            1.   Unpaid leave. Since the City provides paid leave for fewer
than 12 work weeks per year, the additional weeks of leave necessary to attain
the 12 work weeks of leave authorized under this contract shall be provided
without compensation.
            2.   Substitution of paid leave.
               a.   An eligible employee may elect, or the City may require the
employee to substitute any accrued paid vacation leave or sick leave of the
employee for the leave provided under division (a)1. for any part of the 12-
week period of such leave under such subsection.
               b.   Nothing in this contract shall require the City to provide
paid sick leave or paid medical leave in any situation in which the City would
not normally provide any such paid leave.
      (e)   Foreseeable leave.
            1.   Requirement of notice. In any case in which the necessity for
leave under division (B)(1)(a), divisions 1. or 2., is foreseeable based on an
expected birth or placement, the employee shall provide the City with not less
than 30 days written notice before the day the leave is to begin of the
employee's intention to take leave under such subparagraph, except that if the
date of the birth or placement requires leave to begin in less than 30 days,
the employee shall provide such notice as is practicable.
            2.   Duties of employee. In any case in which the necessity for
leave under divisions (c) or (d) above is foreseeable based on planned medical
treatment, the eligible employee:
               a.   Shall make a reasonable effort to schedule the treatment so
as not to disrupt unduly the operation of the City, subject to the approval of
the health care provider of the employee or the health care provider of the
son, daughter, spouse or parent of the employee as appropriate, and
               b.   Shall provide the City with not less than 30 days notice
before the date the leave is to begin of the employee's intention to take leave
under such subparagraph, except that if the date of the treatment requires
leave to begin in less than 30 days, the employee shall provide such notice as
is practicable.
         (f)   Spouses employed by the same employer. In any case in which a
husband and wife entitled to leave under division (B)(1)(a) are employed by the
City, the aggregate number of work weeks to which both may be entitled, shall
be limited to 12 work weeks during any 12-month period if such leave is taken:
            1.   Under division (B)(1)(a)1.a. or (B)(1)(a)1.b.; or
            2.   To care for a sick parent under (B)(1)(a)1.c.
      (3)   Certification.
         (a)   In general. The City may require that a request for leave under
divisions § 34.03 (A) be supported by a certificate issued by the health care
provider of the eligible employee or of the son, daughter, spouse or parent of
the employee as appropriate. The employee shall provide, in a timely manner, a
copy of such certification to the City.
         (b)   Sufficient certification. Certification provided under division
(a) shall be sufficient if it states:
            1.   The date on which the serious health condition commenced.
            2.   The probably duration of the condition.
            3.   The appropriate medical facts within the knowledge of the
health care provider regarding the condition.
            4.   For purpose of leave under division (a) above, a statement
that the eligible employee is needed to care for the son, daughter, spouse or
parent and an estimate of amount of time that employee is needed to care for
the son, daughter, spouse or parent, and a statement that the employee is
unable to perform the functions of the position of the employee.
            5.   In the case of certification for intermittent leave or leave
on a reduced leave schedule f or planned medical treatment, the dates on which
such treatment is expected to be given and the duration of such treatment.
            6.   In the case of certification for intermittent leave, or leave
on a reduced leave schedule a statement of the medical necessity for the
intermittent leave or leave on a reduced leave schedule and the expected
duration of the intermittent leave or reduced leave schedule.
            7.   In the case of certification for intermittent leave, or leave
in a reduced leave, a statement that the employee is intermittent leave, or
leave on a reduced leave schedule is necessary f or the care of the son,
daughter, parent or spouse who has a serious health condition or will assist in
their recovery and the expected duration and schedule of the intermittent leave
or reduced leave schedule.
         (c)   Second opinion.
            1.   In general. In any case in which the City has reason to
question the validity of the certification provided for leave under division
(a)1.c. or d., the City may require at the expense of the City that the
employee obtain the opinion of a second health care provider designated or
approved by the City concerning any information certified under subsection (b)
for such leave.
            2.   Limitation. A health care provider designated or approved
under paragraph (3) shall not be employed on a regular basis by the City.
         (d)   Resolution of conflicting opinions.
            1.   In general. In any case in which the second opinion described
in division (c) differs from the opinion in the original certification provided
under division (a), the City may require at the expense of the City that the
employee obtain the opinion of a third health care provider designated or
approved jointly by the City and the employee concerning the information
certified under division (b).
            2.   Finality. The opinion of the third health care provider
concerning the information certified under subsection (2) shall be considered
to be final and shall be binding on the City and the employee.
         (e)   Subsequent recertification. The City may require that the
eligible employee obtain subsequent recertification on a reasonable basis.
      (4)   Employment and benefit protection.
         (a)   Restoration to position.
            1.   In general. Except as provided hereunder in division (b)
below, any eligible employee who takes leave for the intended purpose of the
leave shall be entitled to return from such leave:
               a.   To be restored by the City to the position of employment
held by the employee when the leave commenced; or
               b.   To be restored to an equivalent position with equivalent
employment benefits, pay and other terms and conditions of employment.
            2.   Loss of benefits. The taking of leave hereunder shall not
result in the loss of any employment benefit accrued prior to the date on which
the leave commenced.
            3.   Limitations. Nothing in this section shall be construed to
entitle any restored employee to:
               a.   The accrual of any seniority or employment benefits during
any period of leave; or
               b.   Any right, benefit or position of employment other than the
right, benefit or position to which the employee would have been entitled had
the employee not taken the leave.
            4.   Certification. As a condition of restoration under f or an
employee who has taken a leave under division (B)(2)(a)1.d., the City does
require that such employee receive certification from the health care provider
of the employee that the employee is able to resume work, except that nothing
in this paragraph shall supersede any valid law of the State of Indiana or
collective bargaining agreement that governs the return to work of such
employees.
            5.   Construction. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to
prohibit the City from requiring an employee on leave hereunder to report
periodically to the City on the status and intention of the employee to return
to work.
         (b)   Exemption concerning certain highly compensated employees.
            1.   Denial of restoration. The City may deny restoration under
division (B)(4)(a) to any eligible employee described in division (4)(b) if:
               a.   Such denial is necessary to prevent substantial and
grievous economic injury to the operations of the City;
               b.   The City notifies the employee of the intent of the City to
deny restoration on such basis at the time the City determines that such injury
would occur; and
               c.   In any case in which the leave was commenced, the employee
elects not to return to employment after receiving such notice.
            2.   Affected employees. An eligible employee described in division
(B)(4)(b) is a salaried eligible employee who is among the highest paid 10% of
the employees employed by the city.
         (c)   Maintenance of health benefits.
            1.   Coverage. Except as provided in division (B) above, during any
period that an eligible employee takes leave hereunder, the City shall maintain
coverage under any “group health plan” [as defined in section 5000(b)(1) of the
Internal Revenue Code of 1986] for the duration of such twelve (12) weeks leave
at the level and under the condition coverage would have been provided if the
employee had continued in employment continuously for the duration of such
leave.
            2.   Failure to return from leave. The City may recover the premium
that the City paid for maintaining coverage for the employee under such group
health plan during any period of unpaid leave hereunder if:
               a.   The employee fails to return from leave after the period of
leave to which the employee is entitled has expired; and
               b.   The employee fails to return from leave for reason other
than:
                  i.   The continuation, recurrence or onset of a serious
health condition that entitles the employee to leave under (B)(3)(b)1.c. or d.;
or
                  ii.   Other circumstances beyond the control of the employee.
            3.   Certification.
               a.   Issuance. The City shall require that a claim that an
employee is unable to return to work because of the continuation, recurrence or
onset of the serious health condition described be supported by:
                  i.   A certification issued by the health care provider of
the son, daughter, spouse or parent of the employee, as appropriate, in the
case of an employee unable to return to work because of a condition specified
in (B)(2)(c)1.c.; or
                  ii.   A certification issued by the health care provider of
the eligible employee in the case of an employee unable to return to work
because of a condition specified in (B)(2)(c)1.d.
               b.   Copy. The employee shall provide, in a timely manner, a
copy of such certification to the City.
               c.   Sufficiency of certification.
                  i.   Leave due to serious health condition of employee. The
certification described in division 3.a.ii., shall be sufficient if the
certification states that a serious health condition prevented the employee
from being able to perform the functions of the position of the employee on the
date that the leave of the employee expired.
                  ii.   Leave due to serious health condition of family member.
The certification described in division 3.a.i., shall be sufficient if the
certification states that the employee is needed to care for the son, daughter,
spouse or parent who has a serious health condition on the date that the leave
of the employee expired.
   (C)   Funeral leave. Funeral leave shall be defined as leave granted without
loss of pay to an employee upon the death of an immediate family member. In any
event, the day of the funeral shall be one of the days of such leave and in no
event shall the last day of the leave be longer than two (2) days after the
funeral. During such absence, the employee shall be compensated at the regular
rate not in excess of eight (8) hours per day of leave.
      (1)   In the event of the death of a child, step-child or spouse of an
employee, the employee shall be granted up to five (5) days of funeral leave
with pay.
      (2)   In the event of a death of an employee’s mother, father, mother-in-
law, father-in-law, brother, sister, grandmother, grandfather, grandchild,
brother-in-law or sister-in-law, the employee shall be granted up to four (4)
days of funeral leave with pay.
      (3)   In the event of a death of an aunt, uncle, niece, nephew or cousin,
the employee shall be granted up to two (2) days of funeral leave with pay.
   (D)   Military reserve. All employees who are members of the Military
Reserve components shall be entitled to a leave of absence, not to exceed
fifteen (15) calendar days in any one calendar year, to attend active duty
training or inactive duty training with full pay and without affecting their
vacation or vacation pay.
   (E)   Jury duty pay plan.
      (1)   An employee called for jury duty, and who must serve on the jury at
a time which causes him to miss work, will be paid by the City in the amount of
the difference between what he is paid by the Court and what his daily base
rate of pay is.
      (2)   If an employee is called for jury duty, he must bring to the Clerk-
Treasurer, upon his return from Court, a statement as to the actual time he
served and the amount that he will be paid by the Court. If an employee serves
on a jury only a portion of a day, he will be expected to report for work the
balance of that day.
      (3)   The maximum amount paid in any one day shall not exceed eight (8)
hours pay at the employee's basic rate less that amount paid by the Court for
jury duty.
   (F)   Training leave.
      (1)   Employees may, upon agreement with the City, obtain training leaves
without loss of pay for the purpose of participating in training and seminars
that will increase the knowledge or efficiency of said employee in his position
with the City.
      (2)   A leave of absence without pay or benefits may be granted to an
employee upon request of the employee and approval by the Board of Works. An
employee must return to work or have been granted additional, leave time by the
agreed upon date of return, or such employee will be considered to have
resigned, not in good standing.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2004-27, passed 12-22-04; Am. Ord.
2013-1, passed 2-7-13)
§ 34.07 PENSION.
   The City will participate in the Public Employees Retirement Fund of Indiana
established by Acts of 1945, chapter 340, and all other acts amendatory thereof
and supplemental thereto. Contributions, allocation of contributions and
benefits will be established in accordance with state law governing the Public
Employees Retirement Fund. The employer shall pay the employees contribution to
PERF for non-police and non-fire employees and shall pay, for police and fire,
an amount of their contribution equal to that of non-police and non-fire
employees.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96)
§ 34.08 DRUG AND ALCOHOL POLICY.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this Drug and Alcohol Policy, hereinafter
referred to as “this Policy,” is to implement drug and alcohol testing
procedures for City of Boonville, Indiana, hereinafter referred to as “City,”
employees and employment applicants, and to otherwise comply with state and
federal statutes, rules and regulations, including the Drug Free Work Place
Act, governing drug and alcohol abuse programs for municipal employees. This
Policy is also designed to achieve the objectives outlined hereinafter in
division (C).
   (B)   Persons affected by testing. All potential new hires will be subject
to mandatory pre-employment assessment screening and drug testing. All
employees of the City who are not members of the bargaining unit shall be
subject to probable cause testing as spelled out in this section. Only drivers
or operators of CDL required equipment, members of the police and fire
department and supervisory personnel shall be subject to the random testing
portions of this policy.
   (C)   Objectives.
      (1)   To implement scientifically valid, fairly administered and properly
documented procedures for prohibited drug and alcohol testing of City
employees.
      (2)   To define circumstances when the City may require its employees to
submit to prohibited drug and alcohol testing.
      (3)   To define the disciplinary sanctions that may be imposed against
employees who test positive for illegal drug and alcohol use, or who are
otherwise found to have illegally used, consumed, possessed, transferred or
been under the influence of prohibited drugs or alcohol while on duty or at
their workplace.
      (4)   To prevent accidents and injuries resulting from the misuse of
prohibited drugs and alcohol.
   (D)   Definitions.
   ALCOHOL. The intoxicating agent in a beverage, including ethyl alcohol,
methyl alcohol and isopropyl alcohol.
   CONFIRMATION TEST. A second analytical procedure required after a screen
test reveals the presence of an impermissible level of alcohol or a prohibited
drug in a person's system. A CONFIRMATION TEST shall employ a procedure
independent from the initial screen test and utilize a more advanced and
reliable scientific method for determining the presence of alcohol or
prohibited drugs in a person's system. Unless otherwise directed, CONFIRMATION
TESTING for the presence of prohibited drugs shall employ the gas
chromatography/mass spectrometry method.
   POST-ACCIDENT SCREEN TEST. A screen test taken when an employee operating a
motor vehicle in the course and scope of his or her employment is involved in
an accident resulting in death, personal injury.
   PROBABLE CAUSE SCREEN TEST. A screen test taken when facts and circumstances
cause an employee's supervisor, and a member of the Board of Works, based upon
reasonable suspicion, to believe that the employee is using or is under the
influence of alcohol or prohibited drugs.
   PROHIBITED DRUG. A “controlled substance,” as defined in Schedules I-V of 21
USC § 812, including marijuana, cocaine, opiates, amphetamines and
phencyclidine. PROHIBITED DRUG shall also include any controlled substance used
contrary to or without a valid prescription issued by a health care provider.
   SCREEN TEST. An initial, scientifically valid, procedure for examining human
fluids to detect the presence of alcohol or prohibited drugs in a person's
system. This policy also authorizes the City to employ alkasensor and
breathalizer devices for alcohol screen testing, provided they are operated by
persons properly trained in that use.
   RANDOM SCREEN TEST. A screen test taken on an unannounced and non-
predictable basis. The only City employees subject to RANDOM SCREEN TESTING
shall be employees holding Commercial Drivers' Licenses (hereinafter referred
to as “CDLs,” who are required to undergo such testing pursuant to 49 C.F.R.
Part 382 and members of the police and fire departments as well as supervisory
personnel.
   RETURN TO DUTY SCREEN TEST. A screen test required of an employee who has
been determined to have illegally used alcohol or prohibited drugs, for which
the results must be verified as negative before the employee is allowed to
return to work.
   (E)   Procedures.
      (1)   General.
         (a)   In administering its Drug and Alcohol Policy, the City shall
only utilize the services of testing facilities certified by the United States
Department of Transportation as qualified to gather, examine, test, maintain
and report the results of the testing of specimens taken from City employees.
Before allowing a testing facility to report the results of any screen testing,
the testing facility shall certify to the City that it meets or exceeds the
requirements set out in 49 C.F.R. Part 40, as amended or otherwise modified,
concerning quality control procedures for workplace drug testing programs.
         (b)   All City employees shall, sign appropriate authorizations at the
testing facility to submit to all prohibited drug and alcohol testing required
by the Policy.
         (c)   All records concerning the City's implementation of this policy
shall be maintained at City Hall in a secure location with controlled access.
Such records shall be maintained by the City in the applicable employee's or
applicant's medical file for a period of not less than five (5) years following
their generation.
         (d)   This Policy shall be administered by the City in a strictly
confidential manner. Only those persons with a legitimate need to know shall be
given access to information or records generated as the result of the City's
implementation of this policy. The Mayor shall, upon the written request from
an employee, release the employee's records generated under this policy to the
employee, their legal counsel, a subsequent employer or any other person
specifically authorized by the employee. The Mayor may also release records
generated under this policy pursuant to a valid subpoena, court order or other
appropriate legal process.
         (e)   No finding of “positive” on a screen test shall be acted upon
until the results of the screen test are validated by confirmation testing.
         (f)   City managerial and supervisory personnel charged with the
responsibility for implementing and administering this Policy shall receive at
least four (4) hours of training on alcohol misuse and four (4) hours of
training on drug misuse from a knowledgeable person prior to assuming such
responsibilities. Thereafter, refresher training shall be conducted no less
frequently than annually. Records showing that such training has occurred will
be maintained at City Hall.
         (g)   All employees shall be provided with a copy of this policy and a
representative of the City shall be made available to those persons to answer
any questions concerning this policy. The City shall also make available to its
employees information concerning the effects of alcohol and drug abuse on the
employee's health, work and personal life; the signs and symptoms of alcohol
and drug abuse; and available methods or assistance programs for dealing with
alcohol and drug abuse.
      (2)   Probable cause screen testing.
         (a)   The City may require an employee to submit to probable cause
screen testing when it is determined, based upon reasonable suspicion, that the
employee is using or is under the influence of alcohol or a prohibited drug.
“Reasonable suspicion” shall be based upon specific, contemporaneous,
articularly observations concerning the appearance, behavior, speech or body
odors of the employee, or some other equally reliable indicator
         (b)   Those persons eligible to determine that probable cause exists
shall be appropriately trained to make such determinations before ordering an
employee to submit to screen testing. The required training shall, at a
minimum, include the subject matter described in 49 C.F.R. 382.601 and 382.603,
as amended or otherwise modified. Persons making probable cause determinations
shall make a written record of their observations and the basis for their
findings within twenty-four (24) hours of the observed behavior, or prior to
the release of the results of the testing, whichever is earlier.
         (c)   An employee subject to probable cause testing shall be
immediately escorted to the appropriate testing facility by a supervisor
         (d)   After completing probable cause testing, the employee may be
suspended from work until the City receives the test results. If the employee
is so suspended, the City will make arrangements to transport the employee home
or to some other appropriate place, such suspension period will be with pay.
         (e)   Those persons making probable cause determinations shall not
participate in any manner in the specimen gathering, testing, analysis or
reporting process.
         (f)   The probable cause determination must be made by both a
supervisor and one member of the Board of Works.
      (3)   Post-accident screen testing. Post-accident screen testing shall be
conducted as soon as practicable following the accident. If possible, testing
for alcohol shall be conducted within two (2) hours and for prohibited drugs
within thirty-two (32) hours following the accident.
      (4)   Random screen testing. Random screen testing for City employees
subject to random testing shall be conducted in accordance with the provisions
of 49 C.F.R. 382.305, as amended or otherwise modified. The minimum annual
percentage rate for tested employees shall be fifty percent (50%) of the
average number of positions.
      (5)   Return to duty screen testing. If an employee is eligible to return
to work after committing an alcohol related act prohibited by this policy, the
employee shall undergo alcohol screen testing before returning to work, the
results of which must indicate an alcohol concentration of 0% in the employee's
system. If an employee has committed a drug related act prohibited by this
policy, the employee shall undergo drug screen testing before returning to
work, the results of which must be negative.
      (6)   Follow-up testing. Employees who have returned to work following
the commission of alcohol or drug related acts prohibited by this policy, shall
be subject to unannounced screen testing for a period of sixty (60) months
following the date of the employee's return to duty.
   (F)   Prohibitions.
      (1)   Pursuant to the Drug Free Work Place Act, 41 USC § 701, each
employee is hereby notified that the unlawful manufacture, distribution,
dispensation, possession or use of a controlled substance is prohibited in the
workplace and punishment, up to and including discharge, for violation of this
policy will be imposed.
      (2)   No employee shall report for duty, remain on duty, or otherwise
engage in any safety- sensitive work function while on duty, with a blood or
breath level of alcohol concentration greater than 0.02%. Any employee who
reports to work with a breath or blood alcohol concentration between 0.02% and
.039% shall be sent home, without pay, for the workday and shall be subject to
discipline under the appropriate discipline policy applicable to the employee.
      (3)   No employee, while on duty, shall operate a motor vehicle while
possessing alcohol.
      (4)   No employee shall use alcohol for eight (8) hours following an
accident which would subject the employee to post-accident screen testing,
unless such testing has already occurred.
      (5)   No employee or applicant for employment shall test positive for
alcohol or drug use as the result of screen testing and confirmation testing
conducted under this policy.
   (G)   Sanctions.
      (1)   Employees who commit any of the acts prohibited by this policy
shall be subject to disciplinary action under the policy applicable to the
employee which may include discharge.
      (2)   Employees who refuse to execute required authorizations will be
deemed insubordinate and shall be subject to disciplinary action which may
include discharge.
      (3)   Employees who refuse to submit to screen testing will be deemed
insubordinate and shall be subject to disciplinary action which may include
discharge.
      (4)   Employees who falsify or adulterate their specimens, or who allow
their specimens to be tested knowing they have been adulterated, will be
immediately discharged.
   (H)   Applicability of state and federal rules and regulations.
      (1)   In implementing this policy, the City is attempting in good faith
to comply with all appropriate state and federal statutes, rules and
regulations governing alcohol and drug abuse programs in the workplace. To the
extent that any portion of this policy is or becomes invalid or unenforceable
because it is in conflict with any such statute, rule or regulation, the
offending portion shall be deemed to have been amended so as to comply with the
applicable statute, rule or regulation.
      (2)   The City also recognizes that the rules and regulations governing
alcohol and drug testing for employees holding CDLs, presently set out in 49
C.F.R. Part 382, are somewhat more comprehensive than those set forth in this
policy. Accordingly, employees with CDLs are hereby advised that they are
subject to the program set forth in 49 C.F.R. Part 382, in addition to the
terms and conditions of this policy. The City shall maintain a copy of 49
C.F.R. Part 382, as amended or otherwise modified, for inspection by City
employees with questions concerning that regulation.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96; Am. Ord. 2017-8, passed 5-24-17)
§ 34.09 SEXUAL HARASSMENT POLICY.
   (A)   Policy.
      (1)   It is the City's policy to prohibit harassment of employees and
prospective employees on the basis of sex. All members of management are
responsible for the effective administration of this policy.
      (2)   Unwelcome sexual advances, requests for sexual favors and other
verbal or physical conduct of a sexual nature constitute sexual harassment
when:
         (a)   Submission to such conduct is made either explicitly or
implicitly a term or condition of an individual's employment;
         (b)   Submission to or rejection of such conduct by an individual is
used as a basis for employment decisions affecting the individual; or
         (c)   Such conduct has the purpose or effect of interfering with an
individual's work performance or creating an intimidating, hostile or offensive
working environment.
      (3)   All forms of sexual harassment are prohibited whether verbal,
nonverbal or physical.
      (4)   Sexual harassment includes, but is not limited to repeated
offensive sexual flirtations, advances or propositions; continued or repeated
commentaries about an individual's body; offensive sexual language; and the
display in the work place of sexually suggestive pictures or objects.
      (5)   Employees who believe they have been sexually harassed should
follow the steps outlined in the problem solving procedure described in
division (B).
   (B)   Problem solving procedure.
      (1)   A goal of the City of Boonville is to treat employees fairly and
equally. We encourage employees to make to make management aware of their
concerns. Complaints and disagreements should be dealt with promptly and as
near the point of origin as possible. Under no circumstances will an employee
be penalized for presenting a complaint to the City. It is the City of
Boonville's policy that all employees have a right to work in an environment
free of discrimination, which includes freedom from sexual or any other form of
harassment of an employee feels discriminated against or sexually harassed
(both of which are against the City of Boonville's policy), the City urges the
employee to report it immediately to his or her supervisor. If for some reason
the supervisor is not available, or the employee does not feel comfortable
talking to the supervisor, the employee should talk to his or her Department
Head.
      (2)   If the employee likewise feels uncomfortable talking to the
Department Head, the City urges the employee to contact the Mayor or the Law
Department. The City will make every effort to resolve the complaint and
protect the confidence of the employee. No employee will suffer retaliation for
initiating investigations or lodging complaints.
      (3)   If wrongdoing is established, appropriate corrective action will be
taken. This corrective action can include discipline, up to and including
termination, of an employee whose actions have been contrary to the City's
policies. If the matter is not resolved to the employee's satisfaction by the
employee's supervisor, or department head, the employee should contact the
Mayor
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96)
§ 34.10 RETIREMENT.
   All employees shall be protected by the rights and privileges granted them
by the Age Discrimination in Employment Act of 1967, as last amended.
(Ord. 1996-14, passed 12-27-96)

at will. All police and fire employees are employees at will in the first year
of employment unless the Board of Public Works and Safety sets a shorter
period.

§ 34.13 UNIFORM ALLOWANCE.

of Public Works and Safety consistent with the City's annual budget.

§ 34.14 ADDITIONAL EXPENSES.
   (A)   The City will allow City employees to use the City credit card for
necessary travel expenses that have been pre-approved by the Mayor, Clerk-
Treasurer or his/her designee for air fare, lodging, car rental, tolls,
parking, and meals along with tips. All expenses shall be subject to
documentation by receipt, and any other documentation requested to substantiate
said expense.
   (B)   The City will reimburse mileage incurred by employees in driving
personal vehicles for City business, other than for travel to and from work and
home daily, as authorized by the Mayor or his/her designee, at the federal
reimbursement rate, as periodically amended.
(Ord. 1989-2, passed 2-2-89; Am. Ord. 2004-30, passed 12-22-04; Am. Ord. 2016-
2, passed 4-7-16; Am. Ord. 2023-7, passed 8-8-23)
§ 34.15 PERSONAL DAYS.
   All full-time employees of the City of Boonville, with the exception of any
department covered by a collective bargaining agreement, firefighters and
elected officials, shall be entitled to two (2) eight (8)-hour personal days
each calendar year. Personal days must be used within the calendar year in
which they accrue, and cannot be carried over to the next calendar year.
Personal days shall be paid in hourly wages, at straight time pay rate.
Personal days shall accrue on January 1st of each year. Employees who have not
worked on January lst of a given calendar year, shall not accrue personal days
until January 1st of the following year.
(Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord. 2003-9, passed 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2013-1,
passed 2-7-13)
§ 34.16 LONGEVITY PAY.
   All current full-time employees of the City of Boonville, except elected
officials and any employee covered by a collective bargaining agreement with
one (1) year of continuous service, shall be entitled to longevity pay. These
employees shall be paid fifty dollars ($50.00) gross, for each calendar year of
continuous employment, after the first year of employment. Longevity pay shall
be paid to the employee in January, in the first payroll of the month,
following the first year of employment.
(Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01; Am. Ord. 2001-10, passed 7-12-01; Am. Ord. 2002-
7, passed 9-3-02; Am. Ord. 2003-9, passed 9-5-03; Am. Ord. 2013-1, passed 2-7-
13)
§ 34.17 PAY IN LIEU OF EXCESS VACATION.
   Any full-time employee of the City of Boonville, who has accumulated up to
eighty (80) hours of vacation time, shall be required to take up to eighty (80)
hours of vacation time during his or her twelve (12) month calendar year
employment period. Any vacation time accrued, in excess of eighty (80) hours,
may be taken in hourly pay, payable at straight time rate, in lieu of time off.
(Ord. 2000-25, passed 2-12-01)
§ 34.18 RETIREES INSURANCE.
   (A)   Any person who has qualified for and obtained retirement status for
full-time employment status rendered to the City of Boonville, Indiana, for a
minimum of twenty (20) years within INPRS (Indiana Public Retirement System-
PERF), ten of the twenty (20) years must be with the City of Boonville, shall
be entitled to obtain and extend coverage from medical insurance policies which
are in effect between the City of Boonville, Indiana, and third-party insurers
for the retiree and their spouse, so long as the retiree and/or spouse shall
pay the percentage of cost set forth in division (B) of this section for the
coverage of the retiree and/or spouse, and provided the third-party insurer
will cover the retiree and/or spouse, at the time of application to the third
party insurer for coverage.
   (B)   (1)   After twenty (20) years full-time employment within INPRS
(Indiana Public Retirement System-PERF), ten (10) of which shall be with the
City of Boonville, the retiree and/or spouse shall pay fifteen percent (15%) of
the insurance premium paid by the City of Boonville for the coverage of the
retiree and/or spouse.
      (2)   After twenty-five (25) years full-time employment within INPRS
(Indiana Public Retirement System-PERF), ten (10) of which shall be with the
City of Boonville, the retiree and/or spouse shall pay five percent (5%) of the
insurance premium paid by the City of Boonville for the coverage of the retiree
and/or spouse.
      (3)   After thirty (30) years full-time employment within INPRS (Indiana
Public Retirement System-PERF), ten (10) of which shall be with the City of
Boonville, the City of Boonville shall pay one hundred percent (100%) of the
insurance premium paid for the coverage of the retiree and/or spouse.
   (C)   When the retiree and/or spouse reaches the age of sixty-five (65), the
medical insurance coverage shall be converted to a Medicare Supplement Plan,
which is in effect between the City of Boonville and a third-party insurer that
the City of Boonville chooses. The retiree and/or spouse must have Medicare
Part A and B to qualify. The portion of the premium paid by the retiree and/or
spouse, and the City of Boonville, shall be the same as outlined in division
(B) of this section.
   (D)   In the event the retiree divorces, the former spouse will no longer be
eligible for benefits provided by this section.
   (E)   In the event the retiree marries or remarries, after retirement, the
spouse shall be eligible for benefits provided by this section.
   (F)   In the event the retiree dies, the surviving spouse shall be eligible
to retain the benefits provided by this section so long as the spouse does not
remarry, and the income of the surviving spouse does not exceed three (3) times
the poverty level guideline of the United States Office of Management and
Budget.
   (G)   Retirement status shall be provided to those former full-time
employees who have qualified through the Indiana Public Employees Retirement
Fund, Policemen's or Firemen's Fund, or any other Indiana retirement program
based solely upon the retiree's former full-time employment with the City of
Boonville.
   (H)   In the event a retiree chooses not to go immediately on the City of
Boonville's retiree insurance benefit, upon retirement, the retiree and/or
spouse will still be eligible for the Medicare Supplement Plan provided by this
section, provided that the coverage in effect at the time of application
provides coverage to retirees and/or spouses, and that there are no-exclusions
which prevent coverage, as determined by the insurer. The retiree must be
covered for their spouse to receive benefits provided by this section. In the
event the retiree and/or spouse is required to pay a higher rate than that
which is required by the City of Boonville employees, the retiree and/or
spouse, will be required to pay that amount which is above said amount. In
addition to the percentage outlined in division (B).
   (I)   The Clerk-Treasurer shall provide information to retirees upon
retirement regarding benefits provided by this section. It shall be the duty of
the retiree and/or spouse to communicate with the insurer regarding
application, benefits and coverage questions. Application for coverage and
applicable administration of benefit questions shall be communicated by the
retiree and/or spouse directly to the insurer or their designated agent.
   (J)   This section shall apply to and be effective for all persons retiring
from full-time employment with the City of Boonville, and their spouses,
subject to the conditions and qualifications outlined herein.
(Ord. 2023-3, passed 5-9-23)
CHAPTER 35: FINANCE AND TAXES
Section
   
35.01   Municipal motor vehicle license excise wheel surtax
   
35.02   Municipal wheel tax
§ 35.01 MUNICIPAL MOTOR VEHICLE LICENSE EXCISE WHEEL SURTAX.
   (A)   Creation of fund. There is hereby created a municipal surtax fund,
which shall be a non-reverting fund. Revenues received from the Department of
Motor Vehicles from taxes imposed under this section shall be deposited into
the fund.
   (B)   Tax rates. Effective January 1, 2018 and in accordance with I.C. 6-
3.5-10-2(c), the surtax shall be impose at the following rates:
      (1)   Passenger vehicles: $25 per year.
      (2)   Motorcycles: $15 per year.
      (3)   Trucks with a declared gross weight that does not exceed 11,000
pounds: $25 per year.
      (4)   Motor driven cycles: $7.50 per year.
   (C)   Vehicle definitions. The definitions set forth in the Act shall apply
to this section.
   (D)   Uses of fund. Funds in the municipal surtax fund may be used for one
or more of the following purposes:
      (1)   To construct, reconstruct, repair, or maintain streets and roads
under the city's jurisdiction;
      (2)   For the city's contribution to obtain a grant from the local road
and bridge matching grant fund pursuant to I.C. 8-23-30; and
      (3)   Any other lawful purposes as may be permitted by the Act.
   (E)   Transportation asset management plan. The City Engineer shall prepare
a transportation asset management plan, approved by the Indiana Department of
Transportation, as required by I.C. 6-3.5-10-2(e) and 6-3.5-11-2(c), and submit
a copy of such plan to the Common Council on or before August 1 of each year.
   (F)   Accounting of fund. On or before August 1 of each year, the Clerk-
Treasurer shall provide the Common Council an estimate of the surtax revenues
to be received by the city under this section during the next calendar year.
The city shall include the estimated surtax revenues in the city's budget
estimate for the calendar year.
(Ord. 2017-9, passed 8-9-17)
§ 35.02 MUNICIPAL WHEEL TAX.
   (A)   Creation of fund. There is hereby created a municipal wheel tax fund,
which shall be a non-reverting fund. Revenues received from the Department of
Motor Vehicles from taxes imposed under this section shall be deposited into
the fund.
   (B)   Tax rates. Effective January 1, 2018 and in accordance with I.C. 6-
3.5-11-2(d), the wheel tax shall be imposed on the following non-exempt
vehicles at the following rates:
      (1)   Buses: $25 per year.
      (2)   Recreational Vehicles: $7.50 per year.
      (3)   Semitrailers: $7.50 per year.
      (4)   Tractors: $25 per year.
      (5)   Trailers: $7.50 per year.
      (6)   Trucks: $25 per year.
   (C)   Vehicle definitions. The definitions set forth in the Act shall apply
to this section.
   (D)   Uses of fund. Funds in the municipal wheel tax fund may be used for
one or more of the following purposes:
      (1)   To construct, reconstruct, repair, or maintain streets and roads
under the city's jurisdiction;
      (2)   As a contribution to an authority established under I.C. 36-7-23;
      (3)   For the city's contribution to obtain a grant from the local road
and bridge matching grant fund under I.C. 8-23-30; and
      (4)   Any other lawful purposes as may be permitted by the Act.
   (E)   Transportation asset management plan. The City Engineer shall prepare
a transportation asset management plan as required in Sec.
54.01(5).
   (F)   Accounting of fund. On or before August 1 of each year, the Clerk-
Treasurer shall provide the Common Council an estimate of the wheel tax
revenues to be received by the city under this section during the next calendar
year. The city shall include the estimated wheel tax revenues in the city's
budget estimate for the calendar year.
(Ord. 2017-9, passed 8-9-17)

§ 50.01 CONTROL OF UTILITIES.
   All Boonville owned utilities shall be controlled by the Board of Works of
the City of Boonville until such time the Boonville Common Council shall act
upon the recommendation of the utility advisory committee established by the
Common Council.
(Ord. 1993-21, passed 9-23-93)
§ 50.02 PLANS REQUIRED FOR CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   Prior to the commencement of any public works program listed below in
any unincorporated area written approval must be obtained from the Warrick
County Area Plan Commission. In order to secure such approval, the Engineer,
Surveyor, Consulting Engineer or the owner shall submit to the said Warrick
County Area Planning Commission complete plans and specifications for the
construction of:
      (1)   Any public water system;
      (2)   Any sanitary storm sewer system;
      (3)   Any gas distribution system; and/or
      (4)   Any street or highway system.
   (B)   In any unincorporated area in which the construction of the above is
contemplated and by this construction it is necessary to connect to or extend
any existing or proposed system lying outside the corporate limits of all towns
and cities the Clerk-Treasurer of said town or city must file the above
construction plans with the Warrick County Area Plan Commission.
(Ord. 1966, passed 9-6-66)
§ 50.03 UTILITY MARKING.
   (A)   The City of Boonville's Utility Marking policy is hereby established
in the City.
   (B)   This policy shall apply to any public utility company, a utility
company operated by a government agency, or to any other entity, whether public
or private, conducting work, such as excavation or demolition that would
require the marking of utility systems, on any roadway, pavement, sidewalk, or
any public right-of-way located within the boundaries of the City of Boonville.
   (C)   Any designated employee of the City of Boonville shall enforce the
provisions of this section.
   (D)   The application and removal of utility markings shall be as follows:
      (1)   Any person marking a utility system on any impervious or hard
surfaces shall only use temporary, removable, water-based spray paints or
temporary, removable marking chalk.
      (2)   The size of any area of excavation that requires marking of
underground utilities shall be observed in accordance with Indiana 811 and I.C.
8-1-26.
      (3)   The removal of such utility markings shall be the responsibility of
the utility company that made or directed the markings. The utility markings
shall be removed within 30 days of the original utility marking or upon
completion of the project.
   (E)   The City of Boonville shall notify the utility company of any
remaining utility markings on any roadway, pavement, sidewalk, or any public
right-sof-way following the completion of the project or within thirty (30)
days of the original utility marking for which the marks were made.
   (F)   The notification to the utility company shall require the removal of
the utility markings within thirty (30) working days. The thirty (30) working
days shall run from the date of notice. Upon the expiration of this period, the
City of Boonville is hereby authorized to remove the utility markings without
further notice to the utility company and assess to the utility company the
full cost incurred by the City of Boonville to affect such removal. The day
before the scheduled removal of the utility markings, the City of Boonville
shall contact Indiana 811 to confirm that no notifications to excavate are
pending for the subject area.
   (G)   Whoever violates the provisions of this section shall be fined one
thousand dollars ($1,000) per violation. Payment of all penalties or additional
costs under any of the provisions of this section shall be made to the City of
Boonville.
(Ord. 2021-2, passed 2-23-21)
