**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 51.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   BULKY WASTE. Bicycle parts, lawn mower parts, vehicle parts and farm
implements. Household wastes such as stoves, refrigerators, water tanks,
washing machines, furniture, toilets and any other matter of this nature.
   BUNDLE. Tree, shrub and brush trimmings, newspapers and/or magazines
securely tied together forming an easily handled package, not to exceed three
feet in length or forty pounds in weight.
   COMMERCIAL REFUSE. The miscellaneous waste material, such as garbage,
rubbish and ashes, resulting from the operation of business enterprises and
institutions. Such term includes construction waste, but excludes industrial
trade waste.
   CONTAINER. A receptacle with a capacity of twenty gallons or greater,
constructed of plastic, metal or fiberglass, having a tight-fitting lid, being
water tight and vermin-proof. No CONTAINER shall have jagged or sharp edges or
any other defect liable to hamper or injure any person depositing refuse in or
collecting the contents thereof.
   CURBSIDE. That portion of a right-of-way adjacent to paved or traveled City
roadways.
   CURBSIDE UNLIMITED. A residential premises refuse collection service option
which allows the customer to place an unlimited amount of residential refuse at
the curb for pick-up once a week.
   GARBAGE. Rejected food wastes, including waste accumulation of animal, fruit
or vegetable matter used or intended for food or that attends the preparation,
use, cooking, dealing in or storing of meat, fish, fowl, fruit or vegetables.
   HAZARDOUS WASTE. Waste, or a combination of waste and other discarded
material, including solid, liquid, semisolid or contained gaseous material,
which, because of its quality, concentration or physical, chemical or
infectious characteristics, may cause or significantly contribute to an
increase in mortality or an increase in serious irreversible illness or serious
incapacitating, but reversible illness, or pose a substantial present or
potential hazard to human health or the environment, if improperly treated,
stored, transported, disposed of or otherwise managed. HAZARDOUS WASTE, for
purposes of this chapter, does not include material which is sold for recycling
or treatment and stored for one year or less, solid or dissolved material in
domestic sewer discharge, solid or dissolved material in an irrigation return
flow discharge, authorized industrial discharge to a Municipal treatment system
or industrial discharge which is a point source subject to permits under
Section 402 of the Clean Water Act of 1977, 33 USC § 1342, or is a source,
special nuclear or byproduct material, as defined by the Atomic Energy Act of
1954, 42 USC §§ 2011 to 2282.
   INDUSTRIAL WASTE. All trade wastes peculiar to industrial manufacturing or
processing plants, including hazardous refuse, but not including commercial
refuse.
   LICENSEE. A person who has been issued a license by the City to collect,
transport or dispose of residential refuse within the City.
   MULTIPLE RESIDENTIAL PREMISES. A parcel of land containing five or more
residential units.
   NONRESIDENTIAL PREMISES. Commercial, industrial or other institutional
premises.
   OPEN BURNING. Any fire wherein the products of combustion are emitted into
the open air and are not directed thereto through a stack, chimney or approved
incinerator
   PLASTIC BAG. A polyethylene or similar PLASTIC BAG of not less than one and
one-half mils thickness, designed to store refuse and secured in a manner to
prevent spillage, leakage or other release of its contents by the use of wire,
string or ties appropriate for thus purpose. The total weight of a bag, and its
contents, shall not exceed forty pounds.
   PREMISES. A parcel of land including any building, improvement or
appurtenance thereon, which, by legal construction or by actuality, forms one
enclosure with it.
   RECYCLABLE MATERIAL. Glass (brown, green or clear), plastic No.2 (milk jugs
etc.), tin (soup cans, etc.), and aluminum, and such other material capable of
being recycled as may be specified.
   REFUSE. Putrescible material and non-putrescible solid wastes, except body
wastes, including garbage, rubbish, ashes, incinerator ash, incinerator
residue, small dead animals, bulky waste and solid construction, hazardous,
industrial and market wastes.
   RESIDENTIAL PREMISES. A parcel of land containing four or fewer residential
units.
   RESIDENTIAL REFUSE. Refuse generated from normal household activity.
RESIDENTIAL REFUSE does not include commercial refuse, construction waste,
hazardous waste, industrial waste, small dead animals exceeding ten pounds in
weight or bulky items.
   RUBBISH. Nonputrescible solid waste, excluding ashes, consisting of both
combustible and noncombustible waste, including paper, cardboard, metal
containers, yard clippings, tree trimmings, wood, glass, bedding, crockery,
demolished building materials or litter of any kind that may be a detriment to
the public health and safety.
   SMALL DEAD ANIMALS. Carcasses of small animals, fish and fowl, not to exceed
ten pounds in weight.
   STORAGE. The accumulation of materials which are awaiting collection,
transportation and disposal.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97; Am. Ord. 2006-8, passed 8-23-06)
§ 51.02 RESPONSIBILITIES OF OWNERS AND OCCUPANTS.
   (A)   Every owner or person in possession of a residential or nonresidential
premises in the City of Boonville is required to have accumulations of refuse
removed and disposed of in accordance with this chapter and any and all other
applicable rules and regulations of the City.
   (B)   No owner, occupant, tenant or lessee of any building, structure,
property or premises in the City shall store, collect, transport or dispose of
any refuse, garbage, rubbish or other rejected, unwanted or discharged waste
materials, except in compliance with this chapter and applicable State, Federal
and local laws, rules and regulations.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.03 BOARD OF PUBLIC WORKS; AUTHORIZATION.
   The Board of Public Works is hereby authorized to make such rules and
regulations as from time to time are deemed reasonably necessary to carry out
the intent of this chapter, provided that such rules are not in conflict with
this chapter or any other law of the city or other appropriate governmental
unit. Failure to observe any approved rule or regulation shall be a violation
of this chapter. Such rules and regulations shall be published and available
for distribution at the City Clerk-Treasurer's office.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.04 PREPARATION OF WASTE.
   All garbage intended for collection shall be drained of all free liquid. The
garbage shall be wrapped or placed in plastic garbage bags.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.05 TYPES OF WASTE.
   (A)   The owner, contractor, occupant or other person responsible for
construction work shall remove from the land, within a reasonable time after
the completion of such construction work, all construction waste. Such material
shall be removed to an appropriate disposal area.
   (B)   Hazardous waste disposal shall be the responsibility of the person who
produced, used or possessed the same, and it shall not be disposed of within
the City.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.06 CONTAINERS; NUMBER AND PLACEMENT.
   (A)   (1)   The owner and/or occupant of all premises within the City shall
provide containers of sufficient number and size for the storage of refuse
between collections. All containers shall be kept clean and in good repair.
      (2)   Arrangements may be made for the rental or purchase of trash
containers from any company operating under a license issued pursuant to this
chapter.
   (B)   All owners or persons in control of construction and demolition sites
shall provide adequate numbers of containers at the site for loose debris,
paper, building material waste and other trash produced by those working on the
site. All loose refuse shall be containerized at the end of each day, and the
site shall be kept in a reasonably clean and litter-free condition.
   (C)   (1)   All owners or persons in control of parking lots, including
drive-in restaurants, taverns, shopping centers, supermarkets and grocery
stores, shall have an adequate number of refuse containers. All persons using
such parking areas shall use such refuse receptacles or containers for the
purpose intended, and no person shall dump, scatter or throw upon any such
parking lot any refuse, garbage or trash of any kind.
      (2)   Owners or persons in control of properties being used for
commercial purposes shall not use areas plainly visible to the public as
storage areas for inventory, stored or discarded items. Such items shall either
be properly disposed of, or removed to storage areas not plainly visible to the
public.
   (D)   (1)   Garbage shall be wrapped or placed in appropriate containers.
Rubbish shall be placed in a container so as to prevent scattering or
littering, or may be cut, baled, tied, bundled or packaged so as not to exceed
40 pounds in weight or three feet in length. Residential refuse that is larger
than that which can be stored according to this section shall be stored in
accordance with rules and regulations promulgated pursuant to this chapter.
      (2)   Only recyclable materials shall be placed in bins used for
recyclable pickup.
   (E)   (1)   Every owner, occupant or person in possession of residential
premises in the City shall provide for his or her residential unit adequate
numbers of proper containers or receptacles, as provided in this chapter.
Containers must be located in such a manner as to prevent them from being
overturned, and be kept in a clean and sanitary condition, free from any
substance which will attract or breed flies, mosquitoes or other insects.
      (2)   Every owner, occupant or person in possession of residential
premises in the City shall keep recyclable material bins and garbage containers
in a clean and sanitary condition and free from any substance which will
attract or breed flies, mosquitoes, or other insects. The bins and containers
must be located in such a manner so as to prevent them from being overturned.
      (3)   Rubbish and yard waste which cannot be secured in a container may
be secured in bundles or may be deposited in plastic bags.
      (4)   For bulky waste, containerization is not required. However, such
bulky items shall be secured so as to prevent scattering.
      (5)   No person shall use plastic bags for unprotected outdoor storage of
refuse, other than for leaves or other lawn debris. Plastic bags containing all
forms of refuse may be set out for scheduled collection in accordance with the
rules set forth in this chapter. Storage of plastic bags containing refuse
shall be considered unprotected when not used as liners for approved containers
or not placed in an enclosure affording reasonable protection from damage by
animals.
      (6)   No person shall use the refuse container of another.
      (7)   No person shall store refuse or recyclable material in the front of
their premises except in accordance with this chapter.
   (F)   For curbside unlimited collection, all containers, bags, bundles and
bulky wastes shall be placed at the curbside in a manner to prevent scattering.
The licensee may decline to collect any container, bag, bundle and bulky waste
not so placed. Doors from refrigerators and freezers must be removed before
being stored for collection. Freon must be removed by a State certified
technician and the item appropriately tagged prior to pick-up.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.07 AVAILABLE SERVICES; DEPOSIT AND REMOVAL.
   (A)   (1)   For residential premises, and for mobile home parks, licensees
shall:
         (a)   Include, with every option for solid waste collection, the
pickup of recyclable materials at least twice a month.
         (b)   Provide the option of unlimited curbside collection service (to
include pickup of garbage, refuse, leaves, lawn debris and twice a month
recyclable pickup.)
      (2)   Licensee may offer other options for different types of collection
service to owners and/or occupants of dwelling units, but licensee must include
the pickup of recyclable materials at least twice a month with any other option
of collection service offered to owners and/or occupants at residential
premises, and to owners and/or occupants of individual dwelling units in mobile
home parks.
   (B)   For residential premises, recyclable material, garbage or refuse shall
not be placed for collection earlier than two hours prior to sundown on the day
preceding the day of scheduled pickup. After the collection of refuse and/or
recyclable material has been made, empty bins and containers shall be removed
from the curb area no later than 9:00 a.m. on the day following collection.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.08 ACCUMULATIONS; NUISANCES.
   (A)   Accumulations or deposits of refuse on any premises, or commercial
inventory, or stored or discarded items that are visible to the public, are
hereby declared to be a nuisance. No owner or occupant of any premises or
property within the City shall permit the accumulation of refuse upon the
premises or property for a period of more than seven (7) days. No person shall
permit accumulations or deposits of refuse, or commercial inventory, or stored
or discarded items that are visible to the public, to remain upon premises
occupied or owned by him or her, except as permitted by this chapter.
   (B)   (1)   The City shall first investigate the existence of an alleged
nuisance to determine whether or not a nuisance exists and to further determine
the person who has created or is committing or maintaining such nuisance.
      (2)   The City shall then give written notice to the person responsible
for the creation, commission or maintenance of such nuisance, specifying in
particular the nature thereof, the corrective action to be taken to abate the
same and the time limit for the abatement of such nuisance, which shall be not
less than three (3) days nor more than seven (7) days, the determination to be
made by the City Code Enforcement Officer.
   (C)   If at the expiration of the time limit such notice, either the person
responsible for the commission, creation or maintenance of the nuisance, or the
owner of the property, has not complied, the City may initiate such cleanup as
identified in the notice. The cost of such cleanup shall be a debt owed to the
City by the person responsible for the commission, creation or maintenance of
such nuisance, and the owner of the property, such costs, including any
reasonable attorney fees, and other costs incurred to enforce compliance with
the ordinance.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97; Am. Ord. 2002-9, passed 8-8-02)
§ 51.09 INTERFERENCE PROHIBITED.
   (A)   (1)   No person, other than the owner or person lawfully in control of
any premises, or any authorized employee of a person licensed by the City for
the removal of refuse, shall interfere in any manner with a container or
plastic bags used for the accumulation or storage of refuse or recyclable
material or remove any such container from the location where it shall have
been placed by the owner or person lawfully in control of the premises; nor
shall any such person disturb or remove refuse or recyclable material placed
for collection.
      (2)   No person shall permit an animal owned by them to tip-over, molest
or otherwise disturb trash containers placed for collection.
   (B)   No person shall scavenge or rifle refuse left for disposal, or damage
or destroy bags or containers of refuse placed for storage or collection.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 51.10 UNLAWFUL DUMPING.
   No person shall dump, deposit, place or scatter any refuse, ashes or yard
wastes within the City, except at an approved disposal area and in compliance
with applicable local, State and Federal laws, rules and regulations. Any
person illegally dumping such refuse within the City shall pay all applicable
penalties, as well as the costs of cleaning-up the illegal dumping, and any
other expense related to enforcing this section, including reasonable attorney
fees.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 51.11 LITTERING; SCATTERING OF REFUSE.
   No person shall cast, spill, place or sweep or deposit anywhere within the
City any refuse in such manner that it may be carried or deposited by the
elements upon any street, sidewalk, alley, sewer, parkway or other public
place, or onto any other premises within the City.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 51.12 CLEANING VACANT LOTS.
   Any person owning or having a possessory interest in any lot or parcel of
land within the City which is vacant is responsible for securing, collecting
and disposing of refuse which may accumulate thereon by contracting with a
person licensed in accordance with this chapter or by self-removal through
proper collection and storage of refuse, transporting the refuse in a manner to
prevent littering or any other nuisance and depositing the same at an approved
disposal area, in accordance with applicable local, State and Federal laws,
rules and regulations.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.13 OPEN BURNING PROHIBITED.
   No person shall kindle or maintain any open fire or authorize any such fire
to be kindled or maintained on any premises without having obtained a permit or
other proper authorization from the Board of Public Works. This restriction
shall not apply to residential fireplaces or to the use of charcoal and similar
materials for the purpose of food preparation. Burning of garbage, small dead
animals, any material made of, or coated with rubber, plastic, leather,
petroleum-based materials or flammable or combustible liquids is prohibited.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 51.14 HOME DISPOSALS.
   (A)   It shall be the duty of every owner of property within the City of
Boonville, Indiana, to cause to be installed, placed and used after January 1,
1969, in each dwelling unit with kitchen facilities, a mechanical home use
garbage disposal unit, connected to the kitchen drains and sanitary drains of
said dwelling unit.
   (B)   The Board of Public Works and Safety shall establish procedures which
provide for the issuance of variance permits for the installation of garbage
disposal to those persons who, upon petition, establish a special hardship or
poverty as a reason for not complying with the terms of this chapter, and which
variance may be granted for a term of one (1) year and may be renewed from time
to time while reasons for non-compliance shall continue.
(Ord. 1968-9, passed 8-1-68)
**** SOLID WASTE COLLECTION ****
§ 51.25 LICENSE REQUIRED; COMPLIANCE.
   (A)   No person shall engage in the business of collection, transportation
or disposal of refuse in the City without first obtaining a license as provided
pursuant to this chapter.
   (B)   The licensee shall at all times comply with all requirements of this
chapter and all rules and regulations issued pursuant to this chapter.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 51.26 APPLICATION PROCEDURE.
   (A)   Application for a license under this chapter shall be made in writing
to the Board of Public Works upon forms furnished by the City. The Board of
Works may issue no more than three (3) annual licenses at any given time.
   (B)   Each applicant shall provide the following information on the
application:
      (1)   Name of individual or corporation completing application.
      (2)   Residence or business address.
      (3)   The number, year and style of vehicles to be used and owned by the
applicant, and whether they are owned or leased.
      (4)   Place or places where the vehicles are to be parked overnight.
      (5)   Whether applicant has ever been denied a license similar to the one
being requested, by any other municipality or governmental entity.
      (6)   Whether the applicant has ever had a similar license revoked by any
other municipality or governmental entity.
      (7)    How many years the applicant has been in business as a solid waste
hauler under the present business name.
      (8)   Supply a current financial statement of the company.
      (9)   Two municipal references.
      (10)   Customer fee schedule.
   (C)   Such application shall also contain a commitment by the applicant to
comply with the provisions of this chapter and all other laws, ordinances,
rules and regulations applicable to the conduct of such business.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.27 INDEMNIFICATION.
   A licensee shall indemnify the City from any and all liability, loss or
damage, including court costs and attorney fees, which the City may suffer from
as a result of claims, demands, settlements or judgments against it arising
from activities relating to refuse collection and disposal by licensee, for
which it is found legally liable.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.28 VEHICLE REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   All vehicles of any licensee carrying refuse under this chapter shall
have on both sides thereof a sign containing the name of the licensee, the
telephone number and number of the vehicle, all of which shall be painted in
plainly visible letters in a size required by D.O.T. regulations, or not less
than four (4) inches in height. The number of the vehicle shall be registered
in the office of the City Clerk- Treasurer.
   (B)   Vehicles used in the transportation of refuse shall be kept clean and
in good repair. The hauling body shall be watertight and completely enclosed on
all sides. Vehicles shall be maintained and operated so that no portion of
their contents is spilled or allowed to remain on any public highway. All
vehicles shall be cleaned at intervals frequent enough to maintain the unit in
a sanitary condition and as free from disagreeable odor as possible to prevent
nuisance or vermin attraction. A vehicle that fails to meet the requirements of
this chapter shall be removed from service until it complies or its use shall
be discontinued permanently.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.29 FEES, BONDS AND INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   Every person or company which engages in the business of collection,
transportation or disposal of refuse in the City shall meet the bonding and
reasonable insurance requirements established by the Board of Public Works of
the City. The license year shall be from January 1st to and including December
31st, and the fee shall be payable in advance.
   (B)   The licensing fee shall be $100.00 per year, plus $5.00 for each and
every collection vehicle to be used within the City. Said fee shall not be
prorated if the license was granted after January 1st of a given year.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.30 REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   (A)   The Board of Public Works may consider the revocation of any license
issued pursuant to this chapter. The licensee shall be given an opportunity to
appear before the Board and present evidence as to why the license should not
be revoked.
   (B)   Grounds for revocation of a license shall include, but not be limited
to the following:
      (1)   The making of any false or misleading statements in the application
for a license.
      (2)   Operating the licensed vehicles in a manner causing or likely to
cause damage to persons or property.
      (3)   Conviction of a licensee or any employee of any licensee of any
criminal offense reasonably related to the issue of whether the licensee is
suitable to continue as a licensee.
      (4)   Violations by the licensee, any employees of the licensee, or any
agents or assigns of the licensee of any of the terms and provisions of this
chapter.
      (5)   The licensee, any employees of any licensee, or any agents or
assigns of the licensee using abusive language to City residents or employees,
creating a disturbance during the performance of work pursuant to this license,
damaging property during the performance of work pursuant to this license, or
creating or maintaining a traffic hazard during the performance of work
pursuant to this license.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
§ 51.31 CONDITIONS OF OPERATION.
   (A)   The openings of each vehicle shall be closed and doors or covers shall
be secured by an adequate latch or restraining mechanism to keep them closed
while transporting refuse. The driver and licensee shall see that the cover is
in the proper position. A special covering shall be used where conditions
require control of odor, vermin, liquids, dust or smoke.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the licensee to transfer the contents of all
containers for which they have contracted for collection with a customer into
vehicles licensed under this chapter, using care to avoid spilling. It shall be
the duty of the licensee or his or her designated agent to clean up all garbage
or rubbish spilled during collection, completely empty the containers, replace
the lids thereon, and return the container to its curbside collection spot.
   (C)   Each vehicle shall be loaded in a manner which minimizes the spilling
of materials.
   (D)   The operator of a refuse transporting unit shall be responsible for
immediately cleaning up any spillage that may occur.
   (E)   No vehicle shall be parked in a residential area longer than necessary
to collect refuse unless it is parked not less than 500 feet from adjacent
residences. Vehicles shall not be parked or stored at any location so as to
cause a hazard to health, or a nuisance.
   (F)   All licensees shall provide a local or toll-free telephone number to
all customers to receive complaints through normal daytime business hours and
provide someone to answer and dispose of such complaints within twenty-four
(24) hours of receipt.
   (G)   The licensee shall not damage cans which are owned by customers or
other property while making collections, and shall return cans, upright, to
their designated place of collection.
   (H)   A complaint log shall be maintained by the licensee indicating the
customer, the time and date complaints are received, and the time and date the
responsive action was taken. A copy of this log shall be made available to the
City upon request.
   (I)   No refuse shall be collected from residential premises in the City
except between the hours of 6:00 a.m. and 6:00 p.m., Monday through Friday.
   (J)   Licensee shall not raise the rates or charges for the work performed
under this license during the duration of such license without prior
notification to the Board of Public Works.
   (K)   Licensees serving residential premises and mobile home parks shall
provide a semi-annual report to the City which lists the number and percentage
(%) of customers participating in recycling and which describes how and where
the recyclable materials are disbursed. The report shall include such
documentation, records, receipts and other objective information as shall be
reasonably necessary for the City to determine that no materials collected in
the recycling program were sent to a landfill, incinerator or otherwise not
properly recycled.
   (L)   Weight limits on trash collection vehicles shall not exceed those
permitted by the State of Indiana, 66,000 lb. gvw plate.
   (M)   Licensee shall provide, quarterly, a report to the City which, in
appropriate detail, describes the volume of refuse being hauled and disposed
of, the time it takes to make said disposal, and any other pertinent
information deemed necessary by the City.
(Ord. 1997-15, passed 12-18-97)
CHAPTER 52: GENERAL WATER AND SEWER PROVISIONS
Section
   
52.01   Water and sewer adjustments
   
Appendix A: Self Repairs Affidavit
   
Appendix B: Plumber's Affidavit
   
Appendix C: Swimming Pool Fill Affidavit
§ 52.01 WATER AND SEWER ADJUSTMENTS.
   (A)   (1)   Any consumer shall have the right to demand that the meter
through which water is being furnished be examined and tested by the Boonville
Water Department for the purpose of ascertaining whether or not it is
registering correctly the amount of water which is being deliver through it by
the Boonville Water Department to such consumer.
      (2)   Upon such application being made, it shall be the duty of the
Boonville Water Department to cause the meter to be examined and tested in the
presence of the consumer, if possible, during normal business hours for the
purpose of ascertaining whether or not it is registering correctly the water
being delivered through it.
      (3)   Prior to such examination, a meter testing deposit shall be
collected from the consumer. The deposit based on meter size shall be
established, and from time to time amended, by resolution of the Common
Council.
      (4)   For any size meter, if the test proves the meter is not within plus
or minus three percent of the American Water Works Standards, the meter testing
fee will be refunded to the consumer and adjustments will be made to the water
and sewer charges on the consumer's most current utilities bills.
      (5)   If the consumer is not satisfied with tests performed by the
Boonville Water Department, the meter shall be tested by the manufacturer. The
consumer must agree to pay all costs of such further tests if the
manufacturer's results verify the utility's results. If the manufacturer's
results do not verify the Boonville Water Department's results, the Boonville
Water Department will bear all costs of said meter testing.
   (B)   Sewer adjustments will be given for first time filling of new swimming
pools one time only within the first year of purchase. An affidavit must be
filled out stating that it is a new swimming pool and accompanied by a receipt
of purchase. It must include the height, width and depth to calculate water
usage. Sewer adjustments will be given for a one-time refill adjustment due to
a repair on an existing pool. The repair is subject to the same time line as
new pool fill adjustments and must have an affidavit of repair along with the
receipt of repair made and will be subject to the Board of Works approval.
   (C)   (1)   Sewer abatement for leaks will be given for underground or
hidden leaks (like in walls). The consumer must file for abatement in writing
within fifteenth (15) days of receipt of the bill in dispute. No claimant or
consumer shall in any event be allowed an abatement or reduction of a bill that
is less than fifty dollars ($50.00) nor more than two (2) consecutive billing
periods. There will only be one sewer abatement allowed in one calendar year.
The consumer or the consumer’s plumber making repairs must file an affidavit
regarding the leakage with the application for adjustment. The consumer’s bill
in dispute will be adjusted to their average sewer usage if determined to be a
hidden or underground leak. This section does not apply to instances where a
leak is caused by the City or its agent, nor to instances of negligence of the
consumer, property owner or its agent. The disputed bill will be adjusted to
the average sewer usage of the previous six (6) months if determined to be a
hidden or underground leak.
      (2)   An adjustment for such a leak will be made to the consumer for half
of the excess water used over the yearly monthly average.
      (3)   If it can be shown that the excess water above the monthly average
did not go into the sewer, then no sewer charges will be assessed for excess
water over the average. If any part of the excess water went into the sewer,
the adjustment for the leak will be made to the consumer for half of the excess
water used over the yearly monthly average.
(Ord. 1998-16, passed 9-24-98; Am. Ord. 1999-2, passed 6-10-99; Am. Ord. 2000-
2, passed 3-9-00; Am. Ord. 2008-5, passed 6-5-08; Am. Ord. 2018-12, passed 8-2-
18)

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 53.01 TAMPERING PROHIBITED.
   It shall be unlawful for any person, not authorized by the City of Boonville
or its agents, to tamper with, alter or otherwise injure any part of the City's
waterworks, supply system or water meter.
(Ord. 1997-6, passed 6-3-97)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 53.02 CONSERVATION AND RATIONING.
   (A)   This section shall apply to all persons, firms, partnerships, company,
associations, corporations or organizations of any kind connected to the public
water system or using water therefrom (“users”).
   (B)   Upon determining that the City of Boonville's public water system is
in imminent danger of a shortage of water or is experiencing a shortage of
water, the Board of Works shall declare a water conservation emergency and
establish the appropriate conservation measures and the duration thereof.
   (C)   The Board of Works of the City of Boonville reserves the right to
establish alternative rationing requirements for the following:
      (1)   Health care providers.
      (2)   A reasonable use of water to maintain adequate health and sanitary
standards.
      (3)   Those industrial and agricultural activities declared to be
necessary for the public health and well being.
   (D)   (1)   In accordance with division (G), users shall be requested to
reduce water consumption by practicing voluntary conservation techniques.
      (2)   The Board of Works suggest reasonable and meaningful actions which
will alleviate existing or potential water shortage.
   (E)   In accordance with division (G), users shall be prohibited from the
water uses listed below, subject to reasonable terms, times and conditions as
the Board of Works shall determine.
      (1)   Sprinkling, watering or irrigating of shrubbery, trees, grass,
ground covers, plants, vines, gardens, vegetables or any other vegetation.
      (2)   Washing of automobiles, trucks, trailers, mobile homes, railroad
cars or any other type or mobile equipment.
      (3)   Cleaning or spraying of sidewalks, driveways, paved areas or other
outdoor surfaces.
      (4)   Washing and cleaning of any business equipment or machinery.
      (5)   The filling of swimming pools, wading pools and ornamental
fountains.
      (6)   Knowingly allowing leakage through defective plumbing.
      (7)   Bulk sales of water.
   (F)   In addition to the mandatory conservation measures of division (E)
above and in accordance with division (G) below, users shall be limited to
water use per the following.
      (1)   Residential use shall be limited to 30 gallons per person per day.
      (2)   Business, commercial and industrial users shall be limited to 60
percent of the volume of water used during the corresponding month of the
preceding year. Business, commercial or industrial users that were not in
business and operating in the area served by the Boonville public water system
more than one year prior to the declaration of need shall be restricted to 60
percent of the average monthly volume of water used during the number of months
such business, commercial or industrial user was in business and operating in
the public water supply area.
   (G)   Notice of voluntary conservation measures shall be by publication in a
local newspaper of general circulation or other means as deemed appropriate by
the Board of Works. Notice of voluntary conservation shall be effective upon
publication. An urgent necessity requiring the immediate effectiveness of the
ordinance can be proclaimed by the Mayor pursuant to I.C. 36-4-6-14 (b).
Mandatory conservation and rationing shall be effective upon passage and the
proclamation of an urgent necessity requiring the immediate effectiveness of
this section.
(Ord. 1993-19, passed 8-4-93)
§ 53.03 RATES AND CHARGES.
   (A)   Water rates shall be as established by the Common Council from time to
time. Copies of the most current water rate ordinance shall be on file in the
City Clerk-Treasurer’s office, and shall be available for public inspection
during regular city office hours.
   (B)   Water, furnished to temporary users, such as contractors, etc., shall
be charged on the basis of the above gallonage rates as estimated by the
Waterworks Superintendent. When a meter is installed for such purposes, the
regular water rats and minimum charges shall apply and the temporary user shall
pay for the installation and removal of the meter.
   (C)   All bills for water services not paid within fifteen (15) days from
the due date thereof, as stated in such bills, shall be subject to a collection
or deferred payment charge of 10% on the first $3.00 and on the excess over
$3.00.
(Ord. 1987-3, passed 2-19-87)
§ 53.04 RESALE PROHIBITED.
   No water shall be resold or distributed by the recipient thereof from the
utility to any premises or other persons other than that for which application
and contract has been made and the meter installed, except in case of
emergency.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 53.05 MUNICIPAL WATER UTILITY.
   (A)   Pursuant to I.C. 8-1.5-3-9.1, the City's municipal water utility is
hereby removed from the jurisdiction of the IURC for the approval of rates and
charges and the issuance of stocks, bonds, notes, or other evidences of
indebtedness, and any other applicable matters.
   (B)   Except as described in division (C) hereof, this section shall take
effect sixty (60) days after the date of its adoption.
   (C)   If, within sixty (60) days of the adoption of this section, the
Council receives a petition: (i) that is signed by at least the number of the
registered voters of the City required under I.C. 3-8-6-3 to place a candidate
on the ballot; and (ii) that requests the Council to submit the question of
removal from the IURC jurisdiction to the registered voters of the City at the
next election, the Council will certify the public question to the county
election board in accordance with I.C. 8-1.5-3-9.1.
   (D)   Within thirty (30) days after this section becomes effective, the
Mayor and/or Clerk-Treasurer shall mail to the IURC written notice of the
withdrawal of the City's municipal water utility from the IURC's jurisdiction.
(Ord. 2020-2, passed 6-16-20)
**** USE REGULATIONS ****
§ 53.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   CUSTOMER. Designates a person, partnership, association, firm, public or
private corporation or governmental agency applying for or using water service
supplied by utility.
   UTILITY or COMPANY. Designates the Boonville Municipal Water System, which
furnishes water service under these rules and regulations.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.16 APPLICATION PROCEDURE.
   (A)   Each applicant for water service will be required to sign a form
provided by the utility setting forth:
      (1)   The date of application.
      (2)   The location of premises to be served.
      (3)   The date on which the applicant will be ready for service.
      (4)   Whether the premises have ever before been supplied by the utility.
      (5)   The purpose for which the service is to be used.
      (6)   The size of service.
      (7)   The address to which the bills are to be mailed or delivered.
      (8)   Whether the applicant is an owner or tenant of, or an agent for the
owner of the premises. If the applicant is an agent, the utility may require
proof of his authority.
      (9)   An agreement to abide by all rules and regulations of the utility.
   (B)   The rates, rules and regulations, ordinances and conditions applying
to water service, hereinafter named shall constitute and be considered a part
of the contract with every person, firm, organization or corporation who is
supplied with water from the system of the utility, and every person, firm,
organization or corporation, hereinafter called the customer, who accepts and
uses water service shall be held to have consented to be bound hereby.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.17 SECURITY DEPOSIT.
   (A)   (1)   A cash deposit equal to two (2) times the estimated average
monthly charge shall be made by the customer as security for the payment of
water service bills, but in no case shall said deposit be less than twenty-five
dollars ($25.00). Deposits prescribed herein less the amount of any unpaid
service bills, will be refunded. Deposits cannot be sold or transferred by one
customer to another except by consent of the utility. Deposits may be refunded
after a satisfactory credit has been established over no less than two (2)
years period and upon proof of home ownership.
      (2)   Deposits will be applied to unpaid balances if service has been
discontinued by the utility because of nonpayment of bills and the full amount
shall be re-established before service will be resumed. Service so discontinued
shall not be restored until all sums then due and owing, shall be paid, plus, a
turn-on charge of five dollars ($5.00).
      (3)   The utility reserves the right to refuse to render service to any
applicant indebted to it for service rendered at any location in the community
served by the utility within three (3) years prior to such application, until
such indebtedness is settled to the satisfaction of the utility.
   (B)   In case the character or volume of a customer's service should change
at any time so that a customer's deposit is inadequate to insure payment of
bills, the utility reserves the right to require the customer to increase his
deposit to an amount satisfactory to utility.
   (C)   Deposit shall be refunded by utility to customer upon termination of
use of all services, provided that customer has paid all bills due to utility,
has given the utility an opportunity to remove its meters and equipment in an
undamaged condition and surrenders the deposit receipt. Utility may require the
identification of the person surrendering deposit receipt. In case the customer
shall have lost the deposit receipt, utility will require that customer sign
its standard release form acknowledging return of the deposit.
   (D)   (1)   The utility may require a present customer to make a reasonable
cash deposit when the customer has been mailed disconnect notices for two (2)
consecutive months or any three (3) months within the preceding twelve (12)
month period, or when the service has been disconnected pursuant to the rules
for non-payment.
      (2)   The amount of such deposit may not exceed an amount equal to 1/6 of
the expected annual billings for the customer at the address at which service
is rendered.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.18 DISCONNECTION; NOTICE.
   (A)   (1)   The customer shall notify the utility at least three (3) days in
advance of the day disconnection is desired. The customer shall remain
responsible for all service used and the billings therefore until service is
disconnected pursuant to such notice.
      (2)   Upon request by a customer of a utility to disconnect service, the
utility shall disconnect the service within three (3) working days of the
requested disconnection date. The customer shall not be liable for any service
rendered to such address or location after the expiration of these three (3)
days.
   (B)   The utility may disconnect service without request by the customer and
without prior notice only:
      (1)   If a condition dangerous or hazardous to life, physical safety or
property exists; or
      (2)   Upon order by any court, or other duly authorized public authority;
or
      (3)   If fraudulent or unauthorized use of water is detected and the
utility has reasonable ground to believe the affected customer is responsible
for such use; or
      (4)   If the utility's regulating or measuring equipment has been
tampered with, the utility has reasonable grounds to believe that the affected
customer is responsible for such tampering.
   (C)   (1)   Except as otherwise provided, herein, service to any residential
customer shall not be disconnected for a violation of any rule or regulation of
the utility or for the non-payment of a bill, except after five (5) days prior
written notice to customer by either:
         (a)   Mailing the notice to such residential customer at the address
shown on the records of the utility;
         (b)   Personal delivery of the notice to the residential customer or a
responsible member of his household at the address shown on the records of the
utility;
         (c)   No disconnect notice for non-payment may be rendered prior to
the date on which the account becomes delinquent.
      (2)   The notice must be in language which is clear, concise and easily
understandable to a layman and shall state in separately numbered large typed
or printed paragraphs:
         (a)   The date of proposed disconnection;
         (b)   The specific actual basis and reason for the proposed
disconnection;
         (c)   The telephone number of the utility office at which the customer
may call during regular business hours in order to question the proposed
disconnection or seek information concerning his or her rights; and
         (d)   A reference to the amount that is past due on their account.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.19 DELINQUENCIES.
   (A)   A utility service bill which has remained unpaid for a period of more
than fifteen (15) days following the mailing of the bill shall be a delinquent
bill.
   (B)   The utility service bill shall be rendered as a net bill. If the net
bill is not paid within fifteen (15) days after the bill is mailed, it shall
become a delinquent bill and a late payment charge may be added in the amount
of ten (10) percent of the first three (3) dollars and three (3) percent of the
excess of three (3) dollars.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.20 ESTIMATES AND ADJUSTMENTS OF BILLS.
   (A)   The water utility may estimate the bill of any customer only for good
cause. Good cause includes, but is not limited to inaccessibility of a
customer's meter, if the utility has made a reasonable attempt to read it; and
other circumstances beyond the control of the utility, its agents and
employees.
   (B)   Errors in meter readings, billing errors, including incorrect tariff
applications, may be adjusted to the known date of error or for a period of one
year, whichever period is shorter.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.21 COPIES OF RULES PROVIDED.
   The utility shall supply free of charge a copy of the rules and regulations
and a copy of the rate schedules applicable to the types of service available
to new applicants and existing customers upon request by the applicant or
customer.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.22 WATER AND SERVICE QUALITY.
   (A)   Water furnished by the utility for human consumption, or for domestic
use shall be of such quality as will have the approval, or comply with the
requirements, of the Indiana State Board of Health.
   (B)   Public water supplies within the distribution pipe systems shall be
adequately protected against pollution from back siphonage or cross connections
with other systems on customer's property by proper installation of protective
devices in accordance with the rules of the Indiana State Board of Health. Such
protective devices shall be installed and maintained at the cost and expense of
the customer, subject however, to the inspection test and approval of the
utility before being placed in service, and at such times thereafter as
recommended by the Indiana State Board of Health.
   (C)   Public water supplies shall also be adequately protected against any
unnecessary and avoidable pollution at their own sources, and at all times
after being taken therefrom, until delivery to the customer, to conform in all
cases to the requirements of the Indiana State Board of Health.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.23 MODIFICATION AND CONSTRUCTION.
   (A)   If a customer requests for his convenience or by his actions requires
that utility facilities be redesigned, reengineered, relocated, removed,
modified or reinstalled, the utility may require the customer to make payment
to it of the full cost of performing such service.
   (B)   (1)   The utility shall, upon written request for service, make free
of charge an extension necessary to give service when the estimated total
revenue, for a period of three (3) years, from the prospective customer or
customers, is at least equal to the estimated cost of such extension.
      (2)   If the estimated cost of the extension required to furnish service
is greater than the total estimated revenue from such extension specified
herein, such an extension shall be made under the following conditions:
         (a)   Upon proper application for such extension:
            1.   Receipt by the water utility of a cash deposit in an amount
equal to the cost of such extension in excess of the amount which would have
qualified as provided in division (1) above; or
            2.   Receipt by the water utility of adequate provision, mutually
acceptable to the parties, for payment of such cash deposit upon completion of
the construction of the extension, the water utility shall proceed with such
extension.
         (b)   For each customer, exclusive of the initial applicants
considered in the making of an extension, connected to such an extension within
the period of six (6) years from the completion of such extension, the water
utility shall refund to such initial applicants, in proportion to their
respective contributions toward the cost of such extension, an amount equal to
three (3) times the estimated annual revenue from such new customer, less the
cost to service such new customer, but the total of all refunds to any such
applicant shall in no event exceed the aforesaid contribution of such
applicant.
   (C)   (1)   The utility shall estimate the total cost of extension from its
existing main or mains which is to serve the extension to the end of the lot or
frontage of the most remote applicant to be served; provided, however, that if
this be a corner lot abutting an intersecting street in which an existing main
is located, the terminal point of the extension shall be located so that the
main constructed hereunder ties in with the existing main located in such
intersecting street.
      (2)   In estimating the cost of an extension, the estimate shall be based
on the diameter of the pipe to be used, provided, however, the estimated cost
to the customer or customers shall not be based on a pipe diameter in excess of
eight (8) inches, unless actual consumption estimated for the proposed customer
(s) requires a larger pipe.
   (D)   (1)   The water utility shall not be required to make extensions as
provided in division (B) above unless the customers to be initially served by
such extension upon its installation have entered into an agreement with the
water utility setting forth the obligations and commitments of the parties,
which may require the customer to provide a satisfactory guaranty to the water
utility of the performance of the customer's obligations thereunder.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.24 TURNING WATER ON.
   (A)   No water shall be turned on for service into any premises by any
person but the Superintendent of Utilities or some person authorized by him to
perform this service.
   (B)   No water shall be turned on for service in premises in which the
plumbing does not comply with the ordinances of the City of Boonville, Indiana;
provided, that water may be turned on for construction work in unfinished
buildings.
(Ord. 1978-1, passed 2-2-78)
§ 53.25 CONNECTION INSPECTION FEES.
   The connection inspection fee for water service shall be one hundred dollars
($100). All applications for water service shall be on forms provided by the
City. In the event a reinspection is needed to complete the connection, a
reinspection fee of fifty dollars ($50) will be assessed for each subsequent
reinspection.
(Ord. 2017-6, passed 5-4-17)
§ 53.26 PROTECTING PUBLIC WATER SYSTEM FROM BACKFLOW CONTAMINATION.
   (A)   A cross connection shall be defined as any physical connection or
arrangement between two otherwise separate systems, one of which contains
potable water from the City of Boonville water system, and the other, water
from a private source, water of unknown or questionable safety, or steam,
gases, or chemicals, whereby there may be a flow from one system to the other,
the direction of flow depending on the pressure differential between the two
systems.
   (B)   No person, firm, or corporation shall establish or permit to be
established or maintain or permit to be maintained any cross connection. No
interconnection shall be established whereby potable water from a private,
auxiliary, or emergency water supply other than the regular public water supply
of the City of Boonville may enter the supply or distribution system of said
municipality, unless such private, auxiliary, or emergency water supply and the
method of connection and use of such supply shall have been approved by the
City of Boonville and by the Indiana Department of Environmental Management in
accordance with 327 IAC 8-10.
   (C)   It shall be the duty of the City of Boonville to cause inspections to
be made of all properties served by the public water system where cross
connections with the public water system is deemed possible. The frequency of
inspections and re-inspections based on potential health hazards involved shall
be established by the City of Boonville.
   (D)   Upon presentation of credentials, the representative of the City of
Boonville shall have the right to request entry at any reasonable time to
examine the property served by a connection to the public water system of the
City of Boonville for cross connections. On request, the owner, lessee, or
occupant of any property so served shall furnish to the inspection agency any
pertinent information regarding the piping system or systems on such property.
The refusal of access or refusal of requested pertinent information shall be
deemed evidence of the presence of cross connections.
   (E)   The City of Boonville is hereby authorized and directed to discontinue
water service to any property wherein any connection in violation of this
section exists, and to take such other precautionary measures deemed necessary
to eliminate any danger of contamination of the public water system. Water
service shall be discontinued only after reasonable notice is served on the
owner, lessee, or occupants of the property or premises where a violation is
found or suspected to exist. Water service to such property shall not be
restored until the cross connection(s) has been eliminated in compliance with
the provisions of this section.
   (F)   If it is deemed by the City of Boonville that a cross connection or an
emergency endangers public health, safety, or welfare and requires immediate
action, and a written finding to that effect is filed with the clerk of the
City of Boonville and delivered to the consumer's premises, service may be
immediately discontinued. The consumer shall have an opportunity for hearing
within ten days of such emergency discontinuance.
   (G)   All consumers using toxic or hazardous liquids, all hospitals,
mortuaries, wastewater treatment plants, laboratories, and all other hazardous
users install and maintain a reduced pressure principal backflow preventer in
the main water line serving each building on the premises. The backflow
preventer must be installed in an easily accessible location not subject to
flooding or freezing.
   (H)   The reduced pressure principle backflow preventers shall not be
installed below ground level.
   (I)   This section does not supersede the Indiana Plumbing Code, the IDEM
Rule 327 IAC 8-10 or any applicable City of Boonville ordinances, but is
supplementary to them.
   (J)   If, in the judgment of the City of Boonville, an approved backflow
prevention device is necessary for the safety of the public water system; the
City of Boonville will give notice to the water consumer to install such an
approved device immediately. The water consumer shall, at his own expenses,
install such an approved device at a location and in a manner approved by the
City of Boonville and shall have inspections and tests made of such approved
devices as required by the City of Boonville and in accordance with the IDEM
Rule 327 IAC 8-10.
(Ord. 2020-4, passed 7-30-20)
