**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 55.01 ELECTRICITY CURTAILMENT.
   The electric curtailment rules for the municipal electric utility of the
City of Boonville, Indiana, as set forth in Appendix I of Ordinance 1978-3, is
adopted and approved. The said electric curtailment rules herein adopted and
approved are hereby authorized to be filed with the Public Service Commission
of Indiana for information purposes only and so identified.
(Ord. 1978-3, passed 2-16-78)
**** TERMS AND CONDITIONS OF SERVICE ****
§ 55.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   COMMISSION. The Public Service Commission of Indiana, or any successor of
such commission having jurisdiction of the subject matter hereof.
   CUSTOMER. A person, partnership, association, firm, public or private
corporation or governmental agency applying for or using electric service
supplied by utility.
   UTILITY or COMPANY. The Boonville Municipal Electric Utility, which
furnishes electric service under these rules and regulations.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.16 APPLICATION FOR SERVICE.
   Each applicant for electric service will be required to sign a form provided
by the utility, setting forth:
   (A)   The date of application;
   (B)   The location of premises to be served;
   (C)   The date on which the applicant will be ready for service;
   (D)   Whether the premises have ever before been supplied by the utility;
   (E)   The purpose for which the service is to be used;
   (F)   The size of service;
   (G)   The address to which the bills are to be mailed or delivered;
   (H)   Whether the applicant is an owner or tenant of, or an agent for the
owner of the premises. If applicant is an agent, the utility may require proof
of his authority; and
   (I)   An agreement to abide by all rules and regulations of the utility. The
rates, rules and regulations, ordinances, and conditions applying to electric
service hereinafter named shall constitute and be considered a part of the
contract with every person, firm, organization or corporation who is supplied
with electricity from the system of the utility, and every person, firm,
organization or corporation, hereinafter called the customer, who accepts and
uses electric service shall be held to have consented to be bound hereby.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.17 SECURITY DEPOSIT.
   (A)   (1)   A cash deposit equal to two (2) times the estimated average
monthly charge shall be made by the customer as security for the payment of
electric service bills, but in no case shall said deposit be less than twenty
($20.00) dollars. Deposits prescribed herein, less the amount of any unpaid
service bills, will be refunded. Deposits cannot be sold or transferred by one
customer to another except by consent of the utility. Deposits may be refunded
after a satisfactory credit has been established over no less than a two (2)
year period or upon proof of home ownership.
      (2)   Deposits will be applied to unpaid balances if service has been
discontinued by the utility because of nonpayment of bills and the full amount
shall be re-established before service will be resumed. Service so discontinued
shall not be restored until all sums then due and owing, shall be paid, plus a
turn-on charge of five ($5.00) dollars.
      (3)   The utility reserves the right to refuse to render service to any
applicant indebted to it for service rendered at any location in the community
served by the utility within three (3) years prior to such application, until
such indebtedness is settled to the satisfaction of the utility.
   (B)   In case the character or volume of customer's service should change at
any time so that customer's deposit is inadequate to insure payment of bills,
the utility reserves the right to require customer to increase his deposit to
an amount satisfactory to utility.
   (C)   Deposit shall be refunded by utility to customer upon termination of
use of all services, provided that customer has paid all bills due to utility;
has given utility an opportunity to remove its meters and equipment in an
undamaged condition and surrenders the deposit receipt. Utility may require the
identification of the person surrendering deposit receipt. In case customer
shall have lost the deposit receipt, utility will require that customer sign
its standard release form acknowledging return of the deposit.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.18 NOTICES.
   (A)   Notice to customers. Notices from the utility to a customer will
normally be given in writing, and either delivered or mailed to him at his last
known address. Where conditions warrant, the utility may resort to notification
either by telephone or messenger.
   (B)   Notices from customers. Notice from the customer to utility may be
given by him or his authorized representative orally or in writing at the
utility's office.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.19 BILLING PROCEDURES.
   (A)   Opening and closing bills.
      (1)   Opening bills, closing bills, and special bills rendered for
periods smaller than the normal monthly billing period will be prorated.
      (2)   For metered service, if actual use is greater than the monthly
minimum, the charges shall be based on actual consumption. In case electric
service is rendered for a period less than the regular billing period of one
(1) month, the monthly service charge shall be prorated on the basis of the
number of days served. In no case shall a bill be rendered for service for less
than one-half the monthly service charge.
   (B)   Meter readings and bills.
      (1)   Meters will be read at regular intervals for preparation of regular
bills, and as required for the preparation of opening bills, closing bills and
special bills.
      (2)   Charges for electrical service furnished by utility shall be billed
and collected monthly. All bills for electrical service are due and payable on
or before the date specified thereon. When payments are made by mail, bills
will be considered as having been paid on date of mailing as shown by postmark.
      (3)   If for any reason whatsoever, the utility's meter reader cannot
gain access to the meter, an average bill may be rendered and the amount
thereof shall be considered as due and payable provided no objection is made to
the amount thereof at the office of the utility by the customer in person or in
writing on or before the date specified for payment. Should the customer object
to such a bill, the service charge allowed in the rate schedule shall be due
and payable on the same date. All meters for electrical service shall be read
and billed separately. No submetering will be permitted.
      (4)   Utility reserves the right to adopt the plan of dividing the city
or territory into districts and of reading meters in each district at a
selected time.
   (C)   Delinquent bills and collection charges. A deferred payment charge of
ten (10%) percent of the first three ($3.00) dollars of current bill and three
(3%) percent of amount of current bill in excess of three ($3.00) dollars will
be added unless amount due is paid on or before delinquent date as stated on
bill. Delinquent date will not be less than ten (10) days from date of issue.
Remittances sent by mail on or previous to the fifteenth day, as evidenced by
the U.S. Post Office mark on the envelope in which they are received, will be
accepted by the Utility as a tender of payment within such fifteen day period.
When the fifteenth day falls on Sunday or any other legal holiday, the first
business day thereafter shall be considered as being within the fifteen day
period.
   (D)   Payment after net bill period. Once in each half calendar year, but
not more often, customer will be permitted to make payment of a single bill at
the net amount after expiration of the prompt payment period; upon submission
by customer of evidence to the utility that payment was not made within the
prompt payment period due to absence from the city, illness or other sufficient
reason.
   (E)   Adjustment of bills for meter error
      (1)   Whenever any test by the utility or by the Commission of a watt-
hour meter while in service or on its removal from service shall show such
meter to have an average error of more than four (4) percent, the following
provisions for the adjustment of bills shall be observed:
      (2)   The error found shall be considered for the purpose of this rule to
have existed for the six (6) months preceding the test or for the time the
meter has been in service at that location if less than six (6) months. If the
meter be found faster than allowable, utility shall refund to customer
concerned any overcharge caused thereby during the period of inaccuracy of the
meter as above defined. The actual error of the meter and not the difference
between the allowable error and the error as found shall be used as the basis
for calculating the refund.
      (3)   If the meter be found to under-register, the utility shall render a
bill to customer concerned for the estimated consumption not covered by bills
previously rendered during the period of-inaccuracy as defined in the preceding
paragraph. Such action shall be taken, however, only in cases where the bill
for estimated inaccuracy amounts to fifty (50) cents or more and all such bills
shall be conditional upon the utility not being at fault for allowing the
inaccurate meter to remain in service.
      (4)   The accuracy and testing of utility's meters shall be in accordance
with the rules of the commission applying thereto.
   (F)   Nonregistering meters. The utility may bill the customer for
electricity used while the meter was not registering. At the option of the
utility the bill will be either at the minimum monthly meter rate or will be
computed upon an estimate of consumption based upon the customer's prior use
during the same season of the year.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.20 ACCESS TO PREMISES.
   The Utility's authorized representative shall have free access to the
premises of the customer at all reasonable hours for the purpose of reading the
meter or inspecting the metering equipment and all other equipment relating to
utility's service or for making the necessary repairs or tests to its
equipment, or for removing its meter and equipment.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.21 CONNECTIONS.
   (A)   Company will designate the point at which the service lines will be
connected to the Customer's facilities. The Customer's wires, at the point of
connection with the Company's lines, if service is overhead, shall extend at
least three feet (3) beyond the outer end of any conduit, weatherproof fitting,
or insulator in order to facilitate this connection.
   (B)   A residential Customer desiring an underground service which is to be
connected to an overhead distribution system of the Company shall at his own
expense provide a conduit riser of a size designated by the Company below the
meter base to a depth below grade as required by the Company. In addition, the
Customer shall make a non-refundable contribution in aid of construction to the
Company in an amount equal to the product of the route of the proposed line
distance in feet from the base of the Company's riser pole to a point directly
below the Customer's meter multiplied by one dollar and twenty cents ($1.20).
Such amount shall be paid to the Company by the customer prior to actual
construction of the service.
   (C)   A residential Customer desiring an underground service which is to be
connected to an underground distribution system of the Company shall at his own
expense provide a conduit riser of a size designated by the Company below the
meter base to a depth below grade as required by the Company. In addition, the
Customer shall make a non-refundable contribution in aid of construction to the
Company in an amount equal to the product of the route of the proposed line
distance in feet from the termination of the Company's facilities at the front
or rear property line to a point directly below the Customer's meter base
multiplied at seventy five cents ($.75). Such amount shall be paid to the
Company by the Customer prior to actual construction of the service.
   (D)   Underground service lines to a Commercial Customer(s), Apartment
House, Apartment House Complexes, or group of adjacent such Customers whether
served from the overhead distribution system of the Company or an adjacent
under ground distribution system of the Company will be available at an
additional charge to the Customer. A non-refundable contribution in aid of
construction to the Company by the Customer(s) shall be made in an amount equal
to the estimated difference in installed cost between underground and overhead
construction. Such amount shall be paid to the Company by the Customer(s) prior
to actual construction of any underground service lines.
   (E)   The Company shall install, own and maintain the service lines from its
facilities to the Customer's meter on underground service.
   (F)   Any changes made in service connections at the Customer's request,
after the original installation, shall be at the Customer's expense.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.22 INTERRUPTIONS AND DISCONNECTIONS.
   (A)   Interruptions in service. The utility will endeavor to furnish
continuous service but does not guarantee uninterrupted service, and shall not
be liable for any damages which the customer may sustain by reason of the
failure of the energy, or failure or reversal of phases, whether caused by
accident, repair or other causes; nor shall the utility be liable for damages
that may be incurred by the use of electrical appliances, or the presence of
the utility's property on customer's premises, or to third persons from the use
of electricity, unless due to fault or neglect on the part of the utility. In
case the customer requires three-phase service, he shall install and maintain
adequate relays with circuit breakers to protect against phase reversal and
single-phase conditions; and his failure to do so shall absolve the utility
from responsibility for loss or damage so resulting. Neither party shall be
liable to the other for any failure by strikes, the acts of God, or unavoidable
accidents or contingencies beyond its control, and is not due to fault, neglect
or culpability on its part.
   (B)   Disconnections of service by utility for purpose of repair. The
utility reserves the right to disconnect its service for the purpose of making
repairs, betterments or extensions on any part of its lines, machinery, plant
or system, without notice to customer. However, the utility will strive to
restrict disconnection of its service to times it will cause the least
inconvenience to the customer; and insofar as possible will notify the customer
in advance of such disconnection. Repairs or improvements will be prosecuted as
rapidly as is practicable. The utility will not be held responsible or liable
for any injury or damage suffered directly or indirectly by customer, or any
agent or employee of the customer, on account of the disconnection of the
service without notice to the customer for the making of such, repairs,
betterments or extensions.
   (C)   Discontinuance of service. A customer may have his electrical service
discontinued by notifying the utility reasonably well in advance of the desired
date of discontinuance. He will be required to pay all electrical service
charges until the date of such discontinuance notice.
   (D)   Discontinuance of service for vacation purposes. Whenever a customer
desires discontinuance of service for vacation purposes, he shall notify the
utility of such desire and of the date of which service is to be discontinued,
sufficiently in advance of date to provide a reasonable time for the utility to
obtain the final meter reading. The customer may have the option of paying the
minimum charge for service during such period, or paying the reconnection
charge of five ($5.00) dollars. This rule shall not apply to any case where a
customer has entered into a contract with the utility to take service for a
definite period of time specified or provided for in such contract.
   (E)   Discontinuance of service for non-payment. Where bills for service
have not been paid by the delinquent date, as stated on bill, which will not be
less than fifteen (15) days from date of issue, service shall be discontinued
and a reconnection charge of five ($5.00) dollars shall be required before
service is restored. Reconnection will be made only after the customer has
established his credit.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.23 FRAUDULENT USAGE.
   (A)   Resale prohibited. The service furnished is for the sole use of the
customer; customer shall not sell or re-deliver electric service to any other
person without the written consent of utility. In case electric energy supplied
by the utility to the customer is resold without the consent of the utility,
service shall be discontinued after two (2) days written notice. If service is
required to be discontinued for this cause, a reconnection of five ($5.00)
dollars shall be required to be paid before service is restored.
   (B)   Fraudulent use of electricity. In the event that evidence is found
indicating fraudulent use of electricity or tampering with utility's meter or
equipment, the utility may discontinue service without notice. Though the
customer may be ignorant of such fraudulent use or tampering, the utility shall
not be required to restore service in such cases, until proper restitution is
made, including payment for any unmetered energy use, damage to utility's
property, and until the proper type meter assembly and wiring shall have been
installed by the customer, and payment of five ($5.00) dollars reconnect charge
is made to the utility.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 55.24 EQUIPMENT AND CONSTRUCTION; MAINTENANCE.
   (A)   Changes in customer's equipment. Customers making any material change
in the size, character or extent of the equipment or operations utilizing
electric service, or whose change in operations results in a large increase in
the use of electricity, shall immediately give the utility written notice of
the nature of the change and, if necessary, amend their application.
   (B)   Unsafe apparatus. The utility may refuse to furnish electricity
service to any premises where apparatus, appliances or equipment using
electricity is dangerous, unsafe or affects matters, of health and safety or is
not in conformity with any applicable laws or ordinances. In any case where
utility has received notice that a dangerous condition exists on customer's
premises, utility may, without any advance notice, shut off the service, and
same shall not be resumed until such dangerous condition shall have been
eliminated. The utility assumes no responsibility for defects in customer's
appliances or equipment or for the escape or damage caused by escape of
electric energy on the customer's premises. The utility does reserve the right
of inspection if there is reason to believe that unsafe apparatus is in use.
   (C)   Meter seals. Seals will be placed by utility on all meters or meter
enclosures, and such seals shall not be broken or disturbed by anyone other
than an authorized representative of the utility.
   (D)   Liability for leakage and damage. The utility will not be liable for
any loss, damage or injury whatsoever caused by escape or loss of electricity
after same has passed through the utility's meter herein defined as “point of
delivery,” nor for defects in the customer's wiring, equipment or appliances.
   (E)   Charges for work done on customer's premises. The utility shall charge
for all materials furnished and for all work done on customer's premises, and
for trouble calls not occasioned by default or negligence on the part of the
utility. Such charges shall be based upon costs as determined herein. The
utility shall not charge for any costs of replacements or repairs to equipment
furnished and owned by the utility on customer's premises, except when damage
is due to negligence or misuse by customer or his agents.
   (F)   Basis of determining costs. The term cost or actual cost as used
herein will be the actual cost of materials used and labor required, plus
twenty (20) percent for use of tools, equipment and appliances, storeroom and
accounting expense, insurance and supervision.
   (G)   Liability. The customer shall save utility harmless from all claims
for trespass, injury to persons, or damages to lawn, trees, shrubs, buildings
or other property that may be caused by reason of the installation or
replacement of the service wires and other necessary appurtenances to serve
customer, unless it shall affirmatively appear that the injury to persons or
damage to property complained of, has been caused by wilful default or
negligence on the part of the utility or its accredited personnel.
   (H)   Wiring requirements. All wiring and equipment connected with the
Utility's service owned or controlled by the customer, shall be suitable for
the purpose intended and shall be installed in accordance with the requirements
of the latest edition of the National Electrical Code, and the National
Electrical Safety Code, with all regulations of the utility. Such customer's
wiring shall be so maintained by the customer at all times. The utility
reserves the right to refuse to connect its service to any wiring or apparatus
if it discovers that same does not meet these requirements. However, connection
of the utility's service to customer's wiring shall not imply that all such
requirements have been met, and the utility is under no obligation to inspect
such wiring either before or after making connection therewith. Also, the
utility may discontinue service to any customer, without notice, when a
defective condition of wiring or equipment upon the customer's premises
results, or is likely to result, in the interference with proper service.
   (I)   Customer's wiring installation. All service entrance wires hereafter
installed shall not be less than number 4 kwg, and shall be identified in
accordance with the latest edition of the National Electrical Code. They shall
be so located that the point of attachment for utility's service shall be not
less than ten (10) feet or more than twenty-five (25) feet above the ground
line, except that in special cases, the utility may designate a particular
point of attachment. Service entrance wires at end of conduit shall be
protected with weatherproof head so installed that the extended wires will
slope downward to prevent the entrance of moisture, and shall be so located
that the, and utility's service wires will be at least two (2) feet from other
wires, downspouts, eaves troughs, etc., and will not be within reach of
windows, porches, porch roofs, balconies or other easily accessible parts of
the building. Such service entrance wires shall extend at least three (3) feet
beyond the weather head on conduit, and be so located as to be convenient for
attachment of the utility's service wires.
   (J)   Location of service entrance. The service entrance shall be located at
a point on the building most convenient for connection to the utility's service
pole, with due regard for the desirability of avoiding the crossing of adjacent
property. Either before or during the construction of the customer's building,
the utility will, upon request, advise the customer, without charge, as to the
proper location of his service entrance. In cases where the service entrance is
not located in accordance with the above, the utility reserves the right to
refuse to install the service wires until such time as the service entrance has
been properly located. Such proper location shall be determined by the utility.
   (K)   Supports for service wires. On stucco, tile, brick or stone veneer, or
other masonry walls, where it is necessary to install supports for service span
wires of the utility, the customer must install one galvanized bolt for each
contemplated service wire. If possible, such bolts should be installed during
the construction of the supporting wall; in any case, the bolts should be
securely fastened to supporting wall or framework. The threaded ends of the
bolts should protrude from the outside wall a distance of not more than one (1)
inch. Bolts shall be one-half inch in diameter and shall be spaced
approximately eight (8) inches apart and all in the same plane.
   (L)   Underground residential distribution facilities; exceptions.
      (1)   For the construction of an underground electric distribution
system, (15,000 volts or less to ground for wye connected systems) in a new
residential subdivision or an existing residential subdivision in which
electric distribution facilities have not already been constructed, the owner
or developer of such subdivision(s) shall be required to make a non-refundable
contribution in aid of construction to the Company in an amount equal to the
work order trench-foot measurement multiplied by one dollar fifty cents
($1.50), which amount shall be considered to be the difference in cost between
overhead and direct burial underground facilities. Such contribution in aid of
construction shall be paid to the Company by the owner or developer prior to
actual construction of the underground system.
      (2)   The construction provided for in the $1.50 per trench-foot
contribution in aid of construction includes the extension of electric
distribution facilities to the lot line of each lot in the underground service
area of the subdivision.
      (3)   The developer or owner of any residential subdivision which is to
be served by underground electric distribution facilities shall furnish to the
company free of charge to the Company drawings and prints and at least one
“sepia” print or equivalent which indicates plotted lots, sewers, water lines,
and any other pertinent data relative to the construction of underground
electric distribution facilities by the Company. The developer or owner shall
furnish free of charge to the Company all necessary easements required by the
Company for the construction and maintenance of an underground electric
distribution facility.
      (4)   For complete underground electric primary and secondary service in
a plot of ground there shall be a minimum of eight (8) plotted lots.
      (5)   The Company shall install, own and maintain the electric
distribution system.
   (M)   Compliance with safety requirements. Service entrance, switch boxes,
service cabinets, switches, fuse blocks, meter bases or sockets, conduit,
wiring, connections and other equipment, and the installation thereof for the
reception and control of electrical energy delivered to customer, shall be of
types approved by the utility and shall meet the requirements of the National
Board of Fire Underwriters, and comply with the state and municipal codes
insofar as they apply in accordance with the requirements of the National Board
of Fire Underwriters, meter service boxes, meter sockets, conduits and wiring
on customer's premises shall be permanently grounded for the customer's
protection.
   (N)   Maintenance and replacement of appurtenances. Utility will maintain
and replace when necessary all appurtenances furnished by it to serve the
customer, including the metering equipment and service wires to customer's
entrance. Customer shall maintain and replace when necessary all services and
appurtenances furnished by customer for reception and use of electricity
supplied, and maintain them at all times in condition to render satisfactory
service.
   (O)   Outside metering installations.
      (1)   If electric metering installations are to be made on premises not
heretofore supplied with electricity by utility, customers shall furnish and
install, at their expense, outside metering installations in accordance with
utility's plan therefor
      (2)   If heavy duty household appliances that require added capacity for
facilities supplying electric service, such as electric range or electric water
heater, are to be installed on premises heretofore supplied with electric
service by utility, outside metering installation shall be provided at
customer's expense and in accordance with utility's standards.
      (3)   If changes that involve the replacement or relocation of entrance
wires, entrance switches, etc., are to be made in existing installations,
outside metering shall be provided at customer's expense in accordance with
utility's standards.
      (4)   If customers elect to change the location of meters from inside to
outside, the cost of installing outside meter shall be borne by customer and
shall be done in accordance with utility's standards.
      (5)   If interference with the proper registration of electric meters has
been definitely established, outside installation of meters shall be made at
customer's expense and in accordance with utility's standards therefor. In all
cases meter receptacles will be furnished without charge by utility.
   (P)   Inside metering installations.
      (1)   If, in the judgement of utility's representative, it is physically
impracticable in some instances to install outdoor metering in accordance with
provisions herein, and if all of customer's wiring preceding the meters is in
solid metal conduit, and if entrance switches, fuses, and all other
appurtenances follow the meters in proper sequence, then indoor metering
installations may continue to be used, if meters are installed in receptacles
furnished without charge by utility.
      (2)   Inside metering equipment hereafter installed shall be located on
the first floor as near as possible to the service entrance and in a clean,
dry, safe place not subject to wide variations in temperature. No meter shall
be placed in any location where its accuracy may be affected by vibration or
exposure to the elements or in any location where the visits of the meter
reader or tester shall cause annoyance to the customer.
      (3)   No meter shall be located at a height of less than five (5) feet or
more than six (6) feet from the floor or ground level.
   (Q)   Multi-metering installations. Where buildings or premises are occupied
by more than one customer, utility will set as many meters as there are
separate applications for service, and will connect the meters to one set of
service wires, providing the wires are sufficiently large to furnish an ample
supply for all customers. Building wiring shall be so arranged as to permit the
installation of utility's meters adjacent to each other.
   (R)   Highly fluctuating or intermittent loads. Service to equipment having
highly fluctuating or intermittent characteristics and having a combined input
capacity in excess of three (3) kw will not be permitted under a residential or
commercial rate. customers having x-ray or welding machines having a total
input capacity in excess of three (3) kw can secure service for such equipment
under the applicable power rates.
   (S)   Neon or fluorescent equipment and radio interference. Any commercial
customer installing neon, fluorescent or other types of gaseous tube lighting,
including signs, will be required to furnish, install and maintain, at his own
expense, corrective apparatus so that the power factor at all times, of each
unit or each group of units controlled through one switch, will not be less
than ninety (90) percent lagging. Any customer operating equipment such as
above mentioned, or other apparatus that is found to be causing radio
interference will be requested to discontinue the use of such equipment or
apparatus or to install corrective equipment to eliminate the radio
interference. Failure or refusal, to comply with the above shall be sufficient
cause to discontinue service. In such a case, the service shall not be
reconnected until the utility is assured that the radio interference creating
device has been corrected or eliminated.
   (T)   Service to multiple units.
      (1)   The utility's rates are based upon the supply of service being
rendered separately for each premises and for the ultimate usage in or on such
separate premises. The combining of service through one meter, or two or more
separate classifications, or of two or more premises, or of two or more
separate living quarters of the same premises, will not be permitted except as
specifically provided for under the rate schedules, and these rules and
regulations, or supplements thereto. An outlying or adjacent building of the
customer, if located on the same may be served from the supply to the main
building, provided the use of such supply to the adjacent building is
supplemental and similar to the usage in the main building.
      (2)   “Premises,” as used herein, shall mean the main residence or living
quarters for the use of single family, or main building of a commercial
customer, and shall include the outlying or adjacent buildings used by the same
provided the use of service in the outlying buildings is supplemental and
similar to the service used in the main residence of building.
   (U)   Extension of rural lines and facilities. Unless otherwise provided in
the utility's rate schedules or rules and regulations, the utility will extend
its lines and facilities in accordance with the Terms and Conditions Governing
Extension of Rural Lines, as most recently approved by the Public Service
Commission of Indiana. A contract for service may be required containing such
terms as may be required to reasonably assure utility that the revenue to be
received from the proposed extension or facility will equal the utility's cost.
   (V)   Service extensions to trailer courts.
      (1)   The utility shall furnish without charge, a primary, alternating
current, sixty cycle, single phase circuit not to exceed three (300') hundred
feet in length from its main line pole, and a three wire, single phase, 110/
220,volts, overhead secondary circuit of adequate size and not to exceed two
(200') hundred feet in length to serve all new trailer courts adjacent to its
system in any township or incorporated political subdivision in which the
utility has a franchise or permit for the distribution and sale of electric
service. The cost of the primary circuit extension in excess of three (300')
hundred feet and the cost of the overhead secondary circuit in excess of two
(200') hundred feet shall be the customer's expense. The primary and secondary
circuit extensions shall include the cost of all materials, and labor required
for the installation of poles, wires, crossarms, line hardware, insulators,
switching and protective devices, and transformers. The cost of clearing the
right-of-way, surveys, easements, permits and other rights shall be the
customer's expense. The customer shall provide and maintain at his expense, an
overhead secondary wiring system as required in excess of two (200') hundred
feet. The secondary wiring system shall be of adequate size to allow a maximum
load of thirty (30) amperes for each trailer served without causing an
objectionable drop in voltage. The customer shall furnish meter loops and
provide suitable locations for meters with easy access for reading and
inspection at all times.
      (2)   All wiring and equipment connected with the utility's service,
owned or controlled by the customer, shall be suitable for the purpose intended
and shall be installed in accordance with the requirements of the latest
edition of the National Electrical Code, and the National Board of Fire
Underwriters, and shall be further governed by the Utility's standard rules,
regulations and conditions applying to electric service.
      (3)   All individual underground services to trailers shall be provided
with over-load protection on the load side of meter. All service entrance wires
for meter loops serving two trailers shall be not less than two #6AWG, and one
#8AWG, in rigid iron conduit or standard service entrance cable of equivalent
size. The utility shall make final connections of meter loop wires to overhead
secondary supply conductors on all new installations. Meter receptacles will be
furnished without charge by the utility.
      (4)   Each trailer in an established trailer court shall be metered
through a separate service connection and billed monthly by the utility. In
accordance with the requirements of the National Board of Fire Underwriters,
the customer shall provide, at his expense, and for his protection, an adequate
grounding system for all service boxes, conduits and wiring on customer's
premises. The point of delivery and location of poles on customer's premises
shall be defined by mutual agreement between utility and customer. The utility
reserves the right to repair, replace and maintain the meter, as well as remove
it upon discontinuance of service. The utility shall not be held liable for
damages to the customer or third persons, resulting from the use of the service
on the customer's premises, or from the presence of the utility's appliances or
equipment on the customer's premises.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.25 CHARACTER OF SERVICE.
   (A)   (1)   The utility shall provide alternating current, 60 cycles, 220,
and 7,200/2,400 volts, three phase, for general power use to all customers
adjacent to its distribution system within the corporate limits of the City of
Boonville. Service available for domestic use to residential customers using
standard electric service or individual apartment shall be alternating current,
120/240 volts, 60 cycles, single phase.
      (2)   Service available for use to all commercial and industrial
customers using standard electric service in any building, other than a
resident or individual apartment shall be alternating current 120/240 volts, 60
cycles, single phase, or 120/240 and 7,200/2,400 volts three phase.
      (3)   Motors of one-half (½) horsepower or more shall be 220 volts. All
motors of five (5) horsepower or less shall be single phase, unless permitted
by the utility. All motors of more than five (5) horsepower shall be three
phase where service can be obtained by direct connections with the utility's
existing three-phase system, or where only a reasonable extension of the
existing three-phase system is necessary. For all motors in excess of five (5)
horsepower, the utility may require an approved device for properly limiting
the starting current.
   (B)   Meters installed temporarily for construction work or similar purposes
shall be placed in boxes or other temporary enclosures that will properly
protect the meter from damage. Such installations must be made in a manner so
that unauthorized persons cannot remove meter or interfere with its proper
registration. Charges for electric service furnished through a temporary
service connection shall be at the established rates for other customers. The
customer shall be required to pay all costs for labor and materials incidental
to supplying and removing of service facilities. The utility may require a
suitable deposit to insure payment of the service installation and removal
costs. The costs shall be determined as stipulated herein. Temporary service
applies to circuses, carnivals, fairs, temporary construction, and the like.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.26 FREE SERVICE.
   No free service shall be furnished by the utility to the city or to any
person, firm or corporation, public or private, or to any public agency, or any
organization. Charges for electric services furnished by the utility shall be
billed and collected monthly.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.27 METERS.
   (A)   Grouping. In apartment houses, stores, and other similar
installations; having two, three or four meters served from a single service
entrance, a main entrance switch need not be used, if the meter service
switches are grouped at the point of service entrance. Installations involving
five (5) or more meters served from a single service entrance, a safety-type,
main service switch with fuses or circuit breakers must be installed to control
the entire load. Each meter shall be deemed as serving one customer and the
billing shall be made accordingly.
   (B)   Meters to be furnished and installed by utility. The electricity shall
be measured as to consumption and, at the option of the utility, as to maximum
demand, by a meter or meters to be furnished and installed by the utility upon
the premises at a point most convenient for the utility's service and upon the
registration of such meter or meters all bills, other than bills for minimum
charges, will be calculated.
   (C)   Creeping meters. Whenever the utility shall find a watthour meter in
its place of service to be creeping, an estimate shall be made of the
registration which the creeping has produced during the six (6) months
preceding the date the creeping was found, or for the time the meter has been
in service at that location if less than six (6) months, and a corresponding
refund shall be made to customer by the utility.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.28 REQUESTED INFORMATION.
   Upon request by any party interested therein, the utility shall furnish
necessary information regarding the location of its distribution pole lines,
service wires or underground wires, and the character of service available at
any location.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.29 MODIFICATIONS OF RULES.
   (A)   No agent, representative, or employee of the utility shall have the
power or authority to amend, modify, alter, or waive rates, terms, conditions,
rules or regulations filed with the Public Service Commission of Indiana; but
the public authorities of the utility shall have the right to amend these
rates, rules, regulations and conditions, or make such additions or changes as
it may deem necessary from time to time, subject to their approval by the
Commission.
   (B)   All rules, regulations, conditions and rates, and parts thereof,
insofar as the same may be in conflict herewith, are hereby canceled.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
**** CLASS OF SERVICE; RATES ****
§ 55.40 RESIDENTIAL ELECTRIC SERVICE.
   (A)   Availability.
      (1)   Available for single phase residential services, including
residential lighting, household appliances, refrigeration, cooking appliances,
water heating, space heating and cooling, and small motors not exceeding (3)
three horsepower individual capacity.
      (2)   Applicant must be located on the Utility's distribution lines
suitable for supplying the service requested.
   (B)   Character of service. Alternating current, sixty-cycle, single phase,
at a voltage of approximate: 120 volts two wire, or 120/240 volts three
   (C)   Rates.
      (1)   Summer energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
June through September, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 5.95 cents per
kwh.
      (2)   Winter energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
October through May, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 4.61 cents per kwh.
      (3)   Customer service charge. In addition to the energy charge, each
customer shall be charged a service charge of $2.70 per month, per meter. The
customer's minimum service charge under this rate shall be the monthly customer
service charge of ($2.70).
      (4)   Rate adjustment. The above rates are subject to a Purchased Power
Cost Adjustment Tracking Factor, in accordance with the order of the Public
Service Commission of Indiana approved December 17, 1976, in Cause No. 34614.
The Purchased Power Cost Adjustment Tracking Factor stated in §§
55.45 and
55.46.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.41 COMMERCIAL ELECTRIC SERVICE.
   (A)   Availability. Available for commercial service including commercial
lighting, miscellaneous small appliances, refrigeration, cooking, water
heating, space heating and cooling, and incidental motors not exceeding three
(3) horsepower individual capacity. Applicant must be located on the Utility's
distribution lines suitable for supplying the service requested.
   (B)   Character of service. Alternating current, sixty-cycle, single phase,
120 volts two wire, 120/240 volts three wire, or three phase, 220 volts three
wire.
   (C)   Rates.
      (1)   Summer energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
June through September, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 6.40 cents per
kwh.
      (2)   Winter energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
October through May, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 4.83 cents per kwh.
      (3)   Customer service charge. In addition to the energy charge, each
customer shall be charged a service charge of $5.25 per month, per meter.
      (4)   Minimum charge. The customer's minimum service charge under this
rate shall be the monthly customer service charge ($5.25).
   (D)   Rate adjustment. The above rates are subject to a Purchased Power Cost
Adjustment Tracking Factor, in accordance with the order of the Public Service
Commission of Indiana approved December 17, 1976, in Cause No. 34614. The
Purchased Power Cost Adjustment Tracking Factor stated in §§
55.45 and
55.46.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.42 GENERAL INDUSTRIAL AND POWER SERVICE.
   (A)   Availability.
      (1)   Available for any customer having an installed capacity of 500 KVA
or greater served through a single meter, except as hereinafter provided.
      (2)   Applicant must be located adjacent to an electric transmission or
distribution line of the Utility, within the limits of the City of Boonville or
elsewhere within an area in which the Utility has a franchise or permit for the
distribution and sale of electricity, that is adequate and suitable for
supplying the service requested.
   (B)   Character of service. Alternating current, sixty-cycle, 220 or 7,200/
2,400 volts, three wire, three phase and metered through one meter.
   (C)   Rates.
      (1)   Summer energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
June through September, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 5.06 cents per
kwh.
      (2)   Winter energy charge. For all kwh used during the billing months of
October through May, inclusive, the rate per month shall be 4.28 cents per kwh.
      (3)   Customer service charge. In addition to the energy charge, each
customer shall be charged a service charge of $42.00 per month, per meter.
      (4)   Minimum charge. The customer's minimum service charge under this
rate shall be the monthly customer service charge ($42.00).
   (D)   Rate adjustment. The above rates are subject to a Purchased Power Cost
Adjustment Tracking Factor, in accordance with the order of the Public Service
Commission of Indiana approved December 17, 1976, in Cause No. 34614. The
Purchased Power Cost Adjustment Tracking Factor stated in §§
55.45 and
55.46.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.43 MUNICIPAL LIGHTING SERVICE.
   (A)   Availability. Available to the City of Boonville for street lighting
services within the corporate limits and for traffic signals on federal, state
and county highways, and on city streets within the corporate limits of the
City of Boonville.
   (B)   Character of service. Alternating current, sixty-cycle, single phase,
at a voltage of approximately 120 volts two-wire, or 120/240 volts three-wire.
   (C)   Rates. All energy consumed shall be 7.55 per kwh.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.44 OUTDOOR LIGHTING SERVICE.
   (A)   Availability. Available only for continuous year-round service for
outdoor lighting to any residential, farm, commercial or industrial customer
located adjacent to an electric distribution line of Utility that is adequate
and suitable for supplying the service requested.
   (B)   Character of service. Dusk-to-dawn outdoor lighting service using
lamps available under this schedule.
   (C)   Rate.
      (1)   175 watt, 7,000 lumen: $7.50;
      (2)   400 watt, 20,000 lumen: $10.95;
      (3)   400 watt, 20,000 lumen (directional): $11.90;
      (4)   1,000 watt, 50,000 lumen (directional): $21.95.
   (D)   Hours of lighting. All lamps shall burn approximately one-half hour
after sunset until approximately one-half hour before sunrise each day in the
year, approximately four thousand (4,000) hours per annum.
   (E)   Ownership of system.
      (1)   All facilities installed by the Utility for service hereunder
including fixtures, controls, poles, transformers, secondary lines, lamps and
other appurtenances shall be owned and maintained by the Utility. All service
and necessary maintenance will be performed only during regularly scheduled
working hours of the Utility. Non-operative lamps will normally be restored to
service within 48 hours after notification by the customer.
      (2)   When the customer requests that a lamp be mounted on the customer's
pole or building, the customer waives any claim for damages caused by the
installation and/or removal of secondary and lamp support.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.45 RATE ADJUSTMENT.
   (A)   This section is applicable to §§
55.40 through
55.42.
   (B)   The rate adjustment shall be on the basis of a Purchased Power Cost
Adjustment Tracking Factor, occasioned solely by changes in the cost of
purchased power, in accordance with the order of the Public Service Commission
of Indiana, approved December 17, 1976, in Cause No. 34614 as follows: the rate
adjustment applicable to the above listed rate schedules shall be $.000000 per
kwh used per month.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)
§ 55.46 FUEL COST ADJUSTMENT.
   (A)   This section is applicable to §§
55.40 through
55.42.
   (B)   (1)   The fuel cost adjustment in dollars per kilowatt hour shall be
the same as that most recently billed to the Boonville Municipal Electric
Utility by its purchased power supplier less $.000000 per kilowatt hour.
      (2)   Such difference to be divided by one (1) minus the total energy
losses of the Utility for the preceding calendar year expressed as a decimal
fraction of the total energy purchased for the same year.
(Ord. 1980-35, passed 12-18-80)

§ 70.01 OPERATION AT CITY LAKES.
   It shall be unlawful for any motor vehicles, motorcycles or motor bicycles
or in any manner, to be operated on the levee or dam at city lakes.
(Ord. 1955-7, passed 5-24-55)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 70.02 OPERATION OF TRACTORS AND TRAILERS.
   (A)   This shall be unlawful or any person, firm or corporation to propel,
haul or draw, or cause to be propelled, hauled or drawn any portable traction
engine, tractor or other heavy conveyance or machine on wheels having cleats,
lugs, spikes or other devices projecting therefrom, upon, over, across or along
any improved street in the corporate limits of the City of Boonville, Indiana;
provided, however, that it shall be lawful for any person, firm or corporation
having charge of any such portable traction engine, tractor, heavy conveyance
or machine, on wheels of the kind described, to cross any such street at any of
its intersections if such person, firm or corporation shall first place or
cause to be placed on the surface of such improved street wooden boards of at
least two inches in thickness and of a width equal the width of the wheels
which to propel of the conveyance over haul or draw such engine, tractor,
conveyance or machine, such boards to be provided and laid at the expense of
such person, firm or corporation and to be removed immediately after being used
as herein provided.
   (B)   No commercial vehicle on wheels having steel tires of less than three
(3) inches in width shall hereafter be licensed to haul freight or merchandise
of any kind whatsoever, upon, over, across or along any of the improved streets
within the corporate limits of the City of Boonville, Indiana.
(Ord. 1992, passed 9-19-22)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 70.03 FUNERAL PROCESSIONS.
   It shall be unlawful for any driver or person in charge of any vehicle, in
or upon any of the streets, alleys or highways within the corporate limits of
the City of Boonville, Indiana, to break or drive through, drive between or in
front of the vehicles of, or interfere with or in any manner disturb, any
funeral procession proceeding in or along and over the streets, alleys or
highways of the City of Boonville to any cemetery or burial place located
either within or without the corporate limits of the City of Boonville.
(Ord. 1924, passed 1-8-24)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 70.04 TRUCKS PROHIBITED ON CITY STREETS.
   All trucks weighing in excess of thirteen thousand (13,000) pounds gross
weight are prohibited from all City streets; except the following:
   (A)   North 3rd Street, from the intersection of Main and Locust to the
Northern most point;
   (B)   North 4th Street to the City limits;
   (C)   South 2rd Street, and North 5th Street to Division Street;
   (D)   All trucks making deliveries to specified areas in the exercise of
commerce.
(Ord. 2003-6, passed 9-4-03)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 70.05 GOLF CARTS PROHIBITED ON CITY STREETS.
   (A)   Golf carts are for pleasure and use on golf courses.
   (B)   Golf carts are not subject to registration and licensing in the State
of Indiana.
   (C)   Golf carts cannot be insured for road use in the State of Indiana and
the City of Boonville.
   (D)   The City of Boonville, for the health and safety of its citizens and
users of public roadways, prohibits the use of golf carts on public streets
and/or accepted right-of-ways within the City of Boonville.
   (E)   The penalty for a violation of this section shall be $50 for the
violation, plus any applicable cost, including reasonable attorney fees.
(Ord. 2004-26, passed 12-22-04)
§ 70.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this title for which no specific
penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99.
   (B)   The penalty for a violation of
Chapter 73, traffic schedule IV: no parking zones shall be $25 for the
violation, plus any applicable cost, including reasonable attorney fees. The
registered owner of the vehicle is responsible for any civil penalty, which is
imposed.
   (C)   The penalty for a violation of
Chapter 73, traffic schedule I: commercial and passenger loading and unloading
zones shall be $25 for the violation, plus any applicable cost, including
reasonable attorney fees. The registered owner of the vehicle is responsible
for any civil penalty, which is imposed.
(Am. Ord. 2004-15, passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2011-4, passed 5-5-11)
CHAPTER 71: PARKING RULES
Section
   
71.01   Definition
   
71.02   Prohibited parking areas
   
71.03   Meters; installation and maintenance
   
71.04   Administration
   
71.05   Unlicensed or damaged vehicles
   
71.06   Off-street parking facilities
   
71.07   Parking lights
   
71.08   Oversized and overweight vehicles
§ 71.01 DEFINITION.
   The word “vehicle,” as used herein, shall mean any device by which any
person or property may be transported upon a highway.
(Ord. 1999-6, passed 7-20-99)
§ 71.02 PROHIBITED PARKING AREAS.
   (A)   General prohibitions. It shall be unlawful to permit any vehicle to
stand in any of the following places except when necessary to avoid conflict
with other traffic or in compliance with the directions of a policeman or
traffic control device:
      (1)   In any intersection.
      (2)   In a crosswalk.
      (3)   Within twenty (20) feet of a crosswalk at an intersection.
      (4)   At any place where the vehicle would block the use of a driveway.
      (5)   At any place where official signs prohibit parking.
      (6)   No vehicle shall be parked next to a curb, which has been painted
yellow.
      (7)   No vehicle shall be parked in a space designated as handicapped
parking other than vehicles that display a handicapped license plate or
handicapped permit.
      (8)   On a sidewalk.
      (9)   Within fifteen (15) feet of a fire hydrant.
   (B)   Cab stands, bus stands. No vehicle other than a licensed taxicab shall
be parked in any area designated as a cab stand. No vehicle other than a bus
shall be parked in a place designated as a bus-loading zone.
   (C)   Loading zone. It shall be unlawful for the driver of a vehicle to
stand a passenger vehicle for a period of time longer than is necessary for the
loading or unloading of passengers, not to exceed three (3) minutes and for the
driver to stand any freight carrying vehicles for a period of time longer than
is necessary to load, unload and deliver materials not to exceed thirty (30)
minutes in any place officially designated as a loading zone and marked as
such.
   (D)   Parking at curb. On all streets except where angle parking is
permitted by ordinance, a Vehicle stopped or parked upon a roadway where there
is adjacent curb shall be stopped or parked within the right-hand wheels of the
vehicle parallel with and of the right hand curb.
(Ord. 1999-6, passed 7-20-99; Am. Ord. 2000-8, passed 6-15-00)
Penalty, see § 70.99
Cross-reference:
   Commercial and passenger loading and unloading zones, see
Parking Schedule I
   No parking zones, see
Parking Schedule IV
§ 71.03 METERS; INSTALLATION AND MAINTENANCE.
   (A)   Installation and maintenance of meters. The Mayor and Board of Public
Works and Safety shall provide for installation of meters and the regulation
and operation thereof and shall cause said meters to be maintained. Meters
shall be placed upon the curb next to individual parking places and meters
shall be so constructed as to display a signal showing legal parking upon
deposit therein of the proper coin or coins of the United States as indicated
by instructions on said meter for a period of time not prohibited by ordinance.
The signal shall remain in evidence until expiration of the said parking period
at which time a change of signal or some other mechanical operation shall
indicate expiration of the parking period allowed by the parking meter.
   (B)   Vehicle parking and payment of charges. When any vehicle shall be
parked in a parking meter zone the operation of said vehicle shall park within
the area designated by the curb or street-marking lines as indicated for
parallel or diagonal parking. One or more coins of the United States as
indicated by instructions on the meter shall immediately be deposited for the
period of time allocated for the coin or coins deposited. It shall be unlawful
for any person to fail to park within the designated area or fail or neglect to
deposit the proper coin or coins between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 4:00 p.m.
every Monday through Friday except on those days which have been designated as
legal holidays.
   (C)   Time limitations. The parking space used by any vehicle may then be
used by such vehicle during the legal parking limit provided by the meter. The
parking limit shall be no longer than a three-hour period in the same parking
space. Parking beyond the legal time limit within a meter space between the
hours of 8:00 a.m. and 4:00 p.m. every Monday through Friday except on those
days which have been designated as legal holidays shall be considered to be a
violation of this section punishable by the penalties provided hereafter for
overtime parking.
   (D)   Owner in control. It shall be considered that the registered owner of
a vehicle was in control of the vehicle at the time of such parking. It shall
be unlawful for any person to permit any vehicle registered in his name to be
unlawfully parked as set out in this section.
   (E)   Unlawful objects. It shall be unlawful to deposit or cause to be
deposited in any parking meter any slug, device or other substitute for a coin
of the United States.
   (F)   Unlawful acts. It shall be unlawful for any person to tamper with,
open, willfully damage or destroy any parking meter.
(Ord. 1999-6, passed 7-20-99)
Penalty, see § 70.99
Cross-reference:
   Parking meter zones, see
Parking Schedule III
§ 71.04 ADMINISTRATION.
   (A)   Traffic Safety Officer. In addition to the powers granted to the
police officers of the City of Boonville, Indiana, for the enforcement of the
parking regulation of the City, the Mayor of the City may appoint, in
cooperation with but not subject to the recommendation of the Chief of Police,
a Traffic Safety Officer to issue parking meter violation tickets in compliance
with this section. Any person so appointed shall have the same powers and
duties as a police officer in the enforcement of parking meter violations of
the City of Boonville short of the power to arrest, a right reserved for sworn
officers.
   (B)   Citation of violations. Any Police Officer or Traffic Safety Officer
issuing a citation to a vehicle parked in violation of the provisions in this
chapter shall securely place the citation on such vehicle in a manner
reasonably calculated to attract the attention of the person in control
thereof. The notice of such violation shall set forth the vehicle license plate
number, the make and color of such vehicle, the parking meter number, if
applicable and the location, date and time of such violation.
   (C)   Parking violation; payment of fine.
      (1)   Any person who receives written notice that a vehicle is parked in
such manner or place or for such a time as to violate the provisions of any of
the sections of this chapter relating to the parking of vehicles shall pay to
the City Clerk-Treasurer as a penalty for such violation the following sum:
         (a)   Overtime parking, if paid within thirty (30) days from time of
the violation as indicated on the notice, five dollars ($5.00).
         (b)   Overtime parking, if not paid within thirty (30) days from time
of the violation as indicated on the notice, ten dollars ($10.00).
         (c)   Handicapped parking violation for the first offense if paid
within thirty (30) days from the violation as indicated on the notice, fifty
dollars ($50.00).
         (d)   Handicapped parking violation if not paid within thirty (30)
days from the time of the violation indicated on the notice, one hundred
dollars ($100.00).
         (e)   Handicapped parking violation following the first offense, one
hundred dollars ($100.00).
         (f)   Any other standing or parking violation, twenty dollars ($20.00)
plus any court costs.
      (2)   The penalty shall be deemed paid as of the time of the postmark on
the envelope if mailed or when actually delivered to City or when picked up
from a fine box.
   (D)   Parking violation procedures. In the event that payment for a parking
violation has not been received within thirty (30) days from the time of such
violation, written notice of such violation and of the failure to pay the
penalty therefore shall be forwarded by regular mail to the address of the
registered owner of such vehicle as shown by the most recent list of license
plate registrations compiled and furnished to the City by the Indiana Bureau of
Motor Vehicles. In the event payment of a parking violation is not received by
the City within ten (10) days from the date of the mailing of the notice the
police department of jurisdiction shall file the information of the violation
to the Clerk of the Warrick Superior Court for the enforcement of this
ordinance with all costs including reasonable attorney fees to be paid by the
violator.
   (E)   Purpose of fees. The fee required to be deposited in said meters is
hereby levied as a city regulation and inspection fee to cover the cost of
providing parking spaces, parking meters and the maintenance of meters, the
cost of regulation and inspection, operation and use of the parking meter
spaces and zones created herein and for the regulation and control of traffic
and for the costs of any parking administration expenses.
   (F)   Parking violations; conflict with other penalty provisions. The
provisions of division (C) above shall not affect the penalty provisions of any
other ordinance.
   (G)   Exemptions. There is expressly exempted from the provisions of this
chapter as it relates to parking meter violations any vehicle displaying a
placard, special registration plate or decal issued by the Bureau of Motor
Vehicles indicating the owner thereof to be a disabled veteran or a physically
handicapped person.
(Ord. 1999-6, passed 7-20-99; Am. Ord. 2000-1, passed 1-27-00; Am. Ord. 2001-
16, passed 10-11-01)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 71.05 UNLICENSED OR DAMAGED VEHICLES.
   (A)   No vehicle, as defined by I.C. 9-13-2-196, which does not have issued
to it and on display, a current license plate issued by a State of the United
States, or an agency of government, shall be parked or allowed to set upon any
public right-of-way in the City of Boonville.
   (B)   No vehicle, as defined by I.C. 9-13-2-196, which is damaged so as to
render it non-functional or which does not have issued to it and on display a
current license plate issued by a State of the United States, or an agency of
government, shall be stored or left setting on private property if such vehicle
may be seen by the public from a public right-of-way.
   (C)   Owners of recreation/competition vehicles such as demolition cars,
race cars, mud bogging trucks, and the like, stored on private property and
visible to the public from a public right-of-way must keep a vehicle cover over
the vehicle.
   (D)   The prohibition of division (B) above shall not apply to a business
operating on properly zoned real estate, engaged in the sale of new or used
vehicles so long as those vehicles are not damaged and are capable of being
driven on the public right-of-way.
(Ord. 1990-9, passed 1-15-90; Am. Ord. 2005-10, passed 10-12-05)
Penalty, see § 70.99
Cross-reference:
   Abandoned Vehicles, see
Chapter 90
§ 71.06 OFF-STREET PARKING FACILITIES.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person to enter and remain upon any off-
street parking facility which is posted as hereinafter set forth between the
hours of 6:00 p.m. and 6:00 a.m., except where the establishment which provides
such parking facilities is then open for the transaction of business.
   (B)   Where any business establishment for which an off-street parking
facility is maintained is open past the hour of 6:00 p.m., it shall be unlawful
for any person to enter and remain upon such facility after such business
establishment has closed its operations for the day, provided, that such
facility is posted as hereinafter set forth and such posting specifies the
hours during which parking is prohibited.
   (C)   When any motor vehicle is left unattended upon any such off-street
parking facility during the hours proscribed by divisions (A) and (B) above, it
shall be deemed a violation of this section and its removal by a towing service
may be authorized by order of any police officer of this City. The cost of such
towing and the storage of such vehicle shall be assessed against the owner
thereof.
   (D)   A posted off-street parking facility is one upon which there is placed
a sign in a conspicuous place stating in letters upon which not less than two
(2) inches in height, the hours during which parking is prohibited, that
violators will be prosecuted under the terms of this section and that
unattended vehicles left on the premises during these hours may be towed away
at the cost of the owner.
(Ord. 1980-12, passed 6-5-80; Am. Ord. 1981-2, passed 1-15-81)
Penalty, see § 70.99
§ 71.07 PARKING LIGHTS.
   No lights need be exhibited upon any vehicle on any street of the City of
Boonville from a half-hour after sunset until a half-hour before sunrise if
said vehicle is otherwise parked in accordance with the parking regulations of
the City of Boonville and where there is sufficient lights to reveal any person
or object within a distance of Five Hundred (500) feet on a roadway where said
vehicle is parked.
(Ord. 1954-1, passed 2-16-54)
§ 71.08 OVERSIZED AND OVERWEIGHT VEHICLES.
   (A)   No person shall park any motor vehicle which, in overall dimension, is
more than:
      (1)   Twenty-one (21') feet long;
      (2)   Eight (8') feet wide;
      (3)   Nine (9') feet high;
      (4)   Eleven thousand (11,000) pounds;
      on any street for more than sixty (60) minutes, per day, without advance
written permission of the City Code Enforcement Officer, City Administrator, or
their designee. This provision shall not apply to personal passenger vehicles.
   (B)   No person shall park any commercial, non-motorized, wheeled device,
including, by way of example, and not limitation, trailers (including tractor
and semi), wagons, carts, storage units and wheeled machinery, on any street
for more than sixty (60) minutes, without the advanced written permission of
the City Code Enforcement Officer, the City Administrator, or their designee.
   (C)   The City Code Enforcement Officer, City Administrator, or their
designee are authorized to issue permits outlined above, which shall only be
issued upon a determination that the requested parking will not create a
hazard, present a sight distance hazard, or obstruction of traffic.
   (D)   A violation of this section, upon ticketing or other actual notice of
violation, shall be subject to a $100.00 per day of violation, penalty, for
each day the violation occurs. In addition, the City shall be entitled to
collect costs to enforce the collection of this penalty, including court costs,
post judgment interest, and attorney fees.
(Ord. 2008-7, passed 7-3-08)

SCHEDULE III PARKING METER ZONES.
   It shall be unlawful to park any vehicle or to permit any vehicle to remain
parked in any parking zone for a period longer than designated on the meter on
Friday between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 8:30 p.m. and on all other days
except Sunday and holidays between the hours of 8:00 a.m. and 6:00 p.m. or to
park a vehicle in any such zone without paying the fee as set forth by Common
Council or to remain parked longer in any zone for which the fee was paid. No
person shall park any vehicle in any individual parking place in any parking
zone for a period longer than two hours in any three-hour period on any day.
