CHAPTER 74: OFF ROAD VEHICLES
Section
   
74.01   Definitions
   
74.02   Policies
   
74.03   Restricted streets
   
74.04   Exceptions
   
74.05   Registration
 
   
74.99   Penalty
§ 74.01 DEFINITIONS.
   CITY STREET. Any roadway maintained by the City of Boonville, Indiana, to
include streets, alleys, and some unimproved rights-of-way within the corporate
limits of the city. This does not include any U.S. Highway or State route that
may go by a local street name.
   FINANCIAL RESPONSIBILITY. A recreational off-road vehicle owner's ability to
pay for damages or injuries that may result from a crash.
   OPERATOR. The person in physical control of the vehicle.
   RECREATIONAL OFF-ROAD VEHICLE. As defined by I.C. 14-8-2-233.5 which
provides as follows:
      (1)   For purposes of I.C. 14-8-2-185, means a motorized, off-highway
vehicle that:
         (a)   Is sixty-five (65) inches or less in width;
         (b)   Has a dry weight of two thousand (2,000) pounds or less;
         (c)   Is designed for travel on at least four (4) nonhighway or off-
highway tires; and
         (d)   Is designed for recreational use by one (1) or more individuals.
      (2)   In addition to the above statutory definition for purposes of this
ordinance such vehicles must:
         (a)   Have a nonstraddle seat or saddle; and
         (b)   Have a steering wheel for steering control.
(Ord. 2021-10, passed 5-11-21)
§ 74.02 POLICIES.
   (A)   Recreational off-road vehicles may be operated on city streets by
persons who have a valid motor vehicle operator's license.
   (B)   A person operating a recreational off-road vehicle has all the rights
and duties concerning traffic regulations that are applicable to a person who
drives a motor vehicle.
   (C)   A person operating a recreational off-road vehicle must be able to
provide proof of financial responsibility.
   (D)   A person operating a recreational off-road vehicle must be in
compliance with all Indiana statutes concerning such, including but not limited
to I.C. 14-16-1 et seq. (off road vehicles).
(Ord. 2021-10, passed 5-11-21)
§ 74.03 RESTRICTED STREETS.
   The operator of a recreational off-road vehicle may not operate such vehicle
upon or within the following streets or areas:
   (A)   State Routes 62 and 61, except to cross them at an angle of 90
degrees, or at times allowed by the Board of Public Works and Safety.
   (B)   Any street or highway posted by order of the city as not to be used by
recreational off- road vehicles.
   (C)   On any private property without permission of the owner.
(Ord. 2021-10, passed 5-11-21)
§ 74.04 EXCEPTIONS.
   This chapter shall not be construed to prohibit:
   (A)   Any police officer, firefighter, or other emergency worker from-
operating a recreational off-road vehicle in their official capacity.
   (B)   Persons that are operating a recreational off-road vehicle solely on
private property.
(Ord. 2021-10, passed 5-11-21)
§ 74.05 REGISTRATION.
   Registration shall be required as outlined in this chapter.
(Ord. 2021-10, passed 5-11-21)
§ 74.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person who violates any provision of this chapter shall be deemed
guilty of a violation and, upon conviction, shall be fined pursuant to the
following schedule:
      (1)   First offense in a calendar year: $50;
      (2)   Second offense in a calendar year: $ 100; and
      (3)   Third offense in a calendar year: $ 150.
   (B)   Each day that a violation occurs constitutes a separate offense.
(Ord. 2014-2, passed 8-5-14)
CHAPTER 75: GOLF CARTS
Section
   
75.01   Prohibited operation
   
75.02   Registration
   
75.03   Operators
   
75.04   Financial responsibility
   
75.05   Equipment
   
75.06   Permissible streets
   
75.07   Restrictions
   
75.08   Number and seating of occupants
   
75.09   Traffic rules
   
75.10   Responsibility of owner or lessee
   
75.11   Compliance
   
75.12   Enforcement
 
   
75.99   Penalty
Cross-reference:
   Use of golf carts, see §
70.05
§ 75.01 PROHIBITED OPERATION.
    Except as hereinafter otherwise provided, the operation of vehicles
originally designed for operation on a golf course, whether powered by battery,
fuel engine or otherwise, and commonly referred to as "golf carts", on the city
streets is and shall be prohibited, except insofar as such golf carts are
registered, equipped and operated in full compliance with this chapter.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.02 REGISTRATION.
    The owner or lessee of a golf cart desiring to operate it on the streets in
the City of Boonville, must register the golf cart annually with the City of
Boonville on or before May 1, after May 1, monthly prorated charges will occur
and an annual registration fee of $50.00 must be paid to the Boonville Police
Department and said fee, unless otherwise dedicated, shall be deposited into
the Police Continuing Education Fund. After inspection by the Boonville Police
Department verifying the golf cart is insured for a period of one (1) year and
equipped as required by §§
75.04 and
75.05, respectively, of this chapter, shall issue a Certificate of Registration
("permit") and numerical tags which shall be prominently attached to and
displayed on the front and rear of the golf cart. The permit must be in the
golf cart or in the possession of the person operating the golf cart at all
times such golf cart is in operation on the streets in the city.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.03 OPERATORS.
    Only persons holding a valid motor vehicle driver's license may operate a
golf cart within the city.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.04 FINANCIAL RESPONSIBILITY.
    A golf cart operating on the streets in the city shall be insured by
liability coverage insurance coverage in an amount not less than that provided
by Indiana statutes for motor vehicles operated on public thoroughfares in the
State of Indiana. Proof of such liability coverage, for a minimum of one (1)
year at a time, must be available on the golf cart or carried by the operator
at all times the golf cart is operated on the streets in the city.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.05 EQUIPMENT.
    A golf cart operating on the street in the city shall be equipped with two
operating headlights (one on each side of the front of the golf cart), tail
lights (one on each side of the rear of the golf cart), visible from a distance
of 500 feet, brakes adequate to control the movement of and to stop and hold
the golf cart, brake lights, a rear view mirror, and a slow moving vehicle
placard shall be prominently displayed on the rear of the golf cart.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.06 PERMISSIBLE STREETS.
    Golf carts may be operated on streets within the city, at speeds consistent
with posted speed limits, not to exceed thirty (30) miles per hour where
appropriate.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.07 RESTRICTIONS.
    Golf carts may only cross State Highway 61 and 62 for the sole purpose of a
ninety (90) degree crossing to a City street. Otherwise, said operation of golf
carts on State Highway 61 and 62 shall be prohibited.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.08 NUMBER AND SEATING OF OCCUPANTS.
    Occupants of a golf cart in operation on the City streets or thoroughfares
shall be limited to the number of persons for whom factory seating is installed
on the cart. The number of occupants of the golf cart, including the operator,
shall not exceed two (2) people per seat. Occupants of the golf cart, including
the operator, shall be seated in the cart and no part of the body of the
operator or an occupant shall extend outside the perimeter of the golf cart
while in operation.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.09 TRAFFIC RULES.
    All golf carts and the operation thereof shall be subject to all traffic
rules, in addition to the requirements set forth herein, as required by Indiana
statutes governing the operation of motor vehicles.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.10 RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNER OR LESSEE.
    The owner or lessee of a golf cart who applies for a permit to operate the
golf cart within the city as herein above provided in this chapter shall agree
to be responsible, jointly and severally, with any person who is permitted to
operate the golf cart, for any injury or damages the operator causes by reason
on the operation of the golf cart if such operator is liable for such damages.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.11 COMPLIANCE.
   It shall be the responsibility of the owner or lessee of the golf cart to
comply with the provisions of §§
75.01,
75.02,
75.03,
75.04, and
75.09 of this chapter. The failure to comply with those requirements of §§
75.01,
75.02,
75.03,
75.04, and
75.09 of this chapter shall constitute a violation of this chapter by the owner
or lessee of the golf cart. It shall be the responsibility of the operator of
the golf cart to comply with the provisions of §§
75.04,
75.05,
75.06,
75.07 and
75.08 of this chapter (including specifically, responsibility for the actions
of all occupants) as set forth in §
75.07. The failure to comply with the requirements set forth in §§
75.04,
75.05,
75.06,
75.07 and
75.08 of this chapter shall constitute a violation of the chapter by the
operator.
(Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.12 ENFORCEMENT.
   All law enforcement officers in the City of Boonville shall have the power
and it shall be their duty to enforce the provisions of this chapter unless
otherwise prevented by state statues regarding the enforcement of state laws.
(Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)
§ 75.99 PENALTY.
   (A)    Any person who violates any provision of this chapter shall be
subject to a penalty of $125.00 for the first violation in any twelve (12)
month period for each violation.
   (B)   Any person who violates any provision of this chapter a second time in
any twelve (12) month period shall be subject to a penalty of $250.00 for each
violation.
   (C)   In addition, the permit as herein above provided may be subject to
suspension or revocation. Provided however, in the event that two or more
offenses constituting violations of this chapter occur within one (1) year, the
operator's permit of the golf cart shall be revoked and may not be reissued for
a period of one (1) year.
   (D)   If the person found responsible for violation of this chapter is a
minor, the custodial parent or parents, or the guardian(s) of the minor, shall
be responsible for the actions of such minor and shall be liable for payment of
the fine or fines imposed hereunder.
   (E)   Additionally, a court may order compliance with the provisions of this
chapter.
   (F)   All fines and penalties shall be payable to the city. The Clerk-
Treasurer of the City of Boonville is hereby authorized to accept the payment
of monetary penalties into the general fund of the City. The Boonville Police
Department Continuing Education Fund shall receive all payment of penalties
under this chapter for which the violator desires to waive trial and admit the
violation.
   (G)   All cases wherein persons cited for a violation do not waive trial and
admit the violation or default in appearance before the City of Boonville
Clerk-Treasurer shall be referred to the City Attorney for filing with the
appropriate Court in Warrick County, Indiana.
(Ord. 2014-9, passed 9-4-14; Am. Ord. 2018-13, passed 8-2-18)

CHAPTER 90: ABANDONED VEHICLES
Section
   
90.01   Definitions
   
90.02   Adoption of state regulations
   
90.03   Responsibility of owner
Cross-reference:
   Health and Sanitation, see
Chapter 94
   Unlicensed or damaged vehicles, see §
71.05
§ 90.01 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   The definitions of the terms used in this chapter shall be same as the
terms are defined in I.C. 9-22-1 and I.C. 9-13-2 and as those terms may be
amended.
   (B)   In addition to the definitions provided above, the following words
shall have the following meanings:
   ABANDONED VEHICLE. In addition to the definitions provided above for
purposes of this chapter, ABANDONED VEHICLE means:
      (1)   A vehicle located on public property illegally.
      (2)   A vehicle left on public property continuously without being moved
for three days.
      (3)   A vehicle located on public property in such a manner as to
continue a hazard or obstruction to the movement of pedestrian or vehicle
traffic on a public right-of-way.
      (4)   A vehicle that has remained on private property without the consent
of the owner, or person in control of that property, for more than 48 hours.
      (5)   Vehicle parts and/or a vehicle from which there has been removed
the engine transmission, or differential or that is otherwise partially
dismantled or inoperable and left on public property.
      (6)   A vehicle that has been removed by a towing service or a public
agency upon request of an officer enforcing a statute or ordinance other than
this chapter, if the vehicle once impounded is not claimed or redeemed by the
owner or his agent within 15 days of its removal.
      (7)   Vehicle parts and/or a vehicle that is mechanically inoperable and
is left on private property continuously in a location visible from public
property for more than 30 days. Vehicle parts and/or a vehicle that is covered
by a tarpaulin, canvas, plastic, etc., shall still be visible pursuant to this
chapter unless said covering is custom made to cover the vehicle.
   AUTHORIZED TOWING SERVICE. A person who has been authorized by the Board of
Works to move, remove, store or impound vehicles and parts pursuant to this
chapter.
   PUBLIC AGENCY. The Board of Works of the City of Boonville.
(Ord. 1995-8, passed 6-8-95)
§ 90.02 ADOPTION OF STATE REGULATIONS.
   (A)   It is the purpose of this chapter to provide for the removal and
disposal of abandoned vehicles. The City of Boonville hereby in all ways adopts
the powers and procedures for the removal, storage, disposal and impoundment of
vehicles and parts as set forth in I.C. 9-22-1 and as those provisions may
hereafter be amended.
   (B)   The City of Boonville hereby finds that abandoned vehicles and parts
are a public nuisance and a safety and health hazard.
(Ord. 1995-8, passed 6-8-95)
§ 90.03 RESPONSIBILITY OF OWNER.
   The owner of an abandoned vehicle or parts is responsible for the
abandonment and is liable, to the extent of the market value of the vehicle,
for all costs incidental to the removal, storage and disposal of the vehicle or
parts.
(Ord. 1995-8, passed 6-8-95)
CHAPTER 91: FAIR HOUSING
Section
   
91.01   Policy
   
91.02   Definitions
   
91.03   Unlawful practice
   
91.04   Discrimination in sales or rentals
   
91.05   Discrimination in real estate transactions
   
91.06   Discrimination in brokerage services
   
91.07   Interference, coercion or intimidation
   
91.08   Prevention of intimidation
   
91.09   Equal access to housing in HUD programs
   
91.10   Exemptions
   
91.11   Administration and enforcement
§ 91.01 POLICY.
   It shall be the policy of the City of Boonville to provide, within
constitutional limitation, for fair housing throughout its corporate limits as
provided for under the federal Civil Rights Act of 1968, as amended, the
federal Housing and Community Development Act of 1974, as amended, and I.C. 22-
9.5-1 et seq.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
§ 91.02 DEFINITIONS.
   The definitions set forth in this section shall apply throughout this
chapter:
   AGGRIEVED PERSON. Includes any person who:
      (1)   Claims to have been injured by a discriminatory housing practice;
or
      (2)   Believes that such person will be injured by a discriminatory
housing practice that is about to occur.
(I.C. 22-9.5-2-2)
   COMMISSION. The Indiana Civil Rights Commission created pursuant to I.C. 22-
9-1-4, et seq.
(I.C. 22-9.5-2-3)
   COMPLAINANT. A person, including the commission, who files a complaint under
I.C. 22-9.5-6.
(I.C. 22-9.5-2-4)
   DISCRIMINATORY HOUSING PRACTICE. An act that is unlawful under §§
91.04 through 91.08 of this chapter or I.C. 22-9.5-5.
   DWELLING. Any building, structure, or part of a building or structure that
is occupied as, or designed or intended for occupancy as, a residence by one
(1) or more families; or any vacant land which is offered for sale or lease for
the construction or location of a building, structure, or part of a building or
structure that is occupied as, or designed or intended for occupancy as a
residence by one (1) or more families. (I.C. 22-9.5-2-8)
   FAMILIAL STATUS.
      (1)   One or more individuals who have not attained the age of eighteen
(18) years being domiciled with a parent or another person having legal custody
of such individual or the written permission of such parent or other person.
      (2)   The protections afforded against discrimination on the basis of
familial status shall apply to any person who is pregnant or is in the process
of securing legal custody of any individual who has not attained the age of
eighteen (18) years.
   FAMILY. Includes a single individual, as defined in I.C. 22-9.5-2-9,with the
status of such family being further defined in FAMILIAL STATUS of this section.
   HANDICAP.
      (1)   With respect to a person:
         (a)   A physical or mental impairment which substantially limits one
or more of such person's major life activities;
         (b)   A record of having such an impairment;
         (c)   Being regarded as having such an impairment;
         (e)   An impairment described or defined pursuant to the federal
Americans with Disabilities Act of 1990; and
         (f)   Any other impairment defined in 910 IAC 2-3.
      (2)   The term HANDICAP shall not include current illegal use of or
addictions to a controlled substance as defined in Section 802 of Title 21 of
the United States Code 910 IAC 2-3-2(14); nor does the term HANDICAP include an
individual solely because that individual is a transvestite 910 IAC 2-3-2(14).
   PERSON. Includes one (1) or more individuals, corporations, partnerships,
associations, labor organizations, legal representatives, mutual companies,
joint-stock companies, trusts, non-incorporated organizations, trustees,
trustees in cases under Title 11 of the United States Code, receivers, and
fiduciaries. (I.C. 22-9.5-2-11)
   TO RENT. Includes to lease, to sublease, to let and otherwise to grant for a
consideration the rights to occupy the premises owned by the occupant. (I.C.
22-9.5-2-13)
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2012-15, passed 9-6-12; Am. Ord. 2014-5,
passed 7-3-14)
Editor’s note:
   This section makes reference to I.C. 22-9.5-2-10. This statute was repealed
in 2007.
§ 91.03 UNLAWFUL PRACTICE.
   Subject to the provisions of division (B) of this section, §
91.09 and I.C. 22-9.5-3, the prohibitions against discrimination in the sale or
rental of housing set forth in I.C. 22-9.5-5-1 and in §
91.04 shall apply to:
   (A)   All dwellings except as exempted by division (B) and I.C. 22-9.5-3.
   (B)   Other than the provisions of division (C) of this section, nothing in
§
91.04 shall apply to:
      (1)   Any single-family house sold or rented by an owner where the
private individual owner does not own more than three such single-family houses
at any one time; provided that in the sale of such single family house by a
private individual owner not residing in the house at the time of sale or
exemption shall apply only to one such sale within any twenty-four (24) month
period. The private individual owner may not own any interest in, nor have
owned or reserved on his behalf, title to or any right to all or a portion of
the proceeds from the sale or rental of more than three such single-family
houses at any one time. The sale or rental of any such single family house
shall be exempted from application of this section only if such house is sold
or rented:
         (a)   Without the use in any manner of the sales or rental facilities
or services of any real estate broker, agent or salesman, or any person in the
business of selling or renting dwellings, or of any employee or agent of any
such broker, agent or salesman, or person; and
         (b)   without the publication, posting or mailing, after notice of
advertisement or written notice in violation of §
91.04(C), but noting in this provision shall prohibit the use of attorneys,
escrow agents, abstracters, title companies and other such professional
assistance as necessary to perfect or transfer this title; or
      (2)   Rooms or units in dwellings containing living quarters occupied or
intended to be occupied by no more than four (4) families living independently
of each other, if the owner actually maintains and occupies one of such living
quarters as his residence.
   (C)   For the purposes of division (B), a person shall be deemed to be in
the business of selling or renting dwellings if:
      (1)   They have, within the preceding twelve (12) months, participated as
principal in three (3) or more transactions involving the sale or rental of any
dwelling or any interest therein;
      (2)   They have, within the preceding twelve (12) months, participated as
agent, other than in the sale of his own personal residence, in providing sales
or rental facilities or services in two (2) or more transactions involving the
sale or rental of any dwelling or any interest therein; or
      (3)   They are the owner of any dwelling unit designed or intended for
occupancy by, or occupied by five (5) or more families.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
§ 91.04 DISCRIMINATION IN SALES OR RENTALS.
   As made applicable by §
91.03 and except as exempted by §
91.03(B) and §
91.09, it shall be unlawful:
   (A)   To refuse to sell or rent after the making of a bona fide offer, or to
refuse to negotiate for the sale or rental of, or otherwise make unavailable or
deny, a dwelling to any person because of race, color, religion, sex, handicap,
familial status or national origin.
   (B)   To discriminate against any person in the terms, conditions, or
privileges of sale or rental of a dwelling or in the provision of services of
facilities in connection therewith, because of race, color, religion, sex,
handicap, familial status or national origin.
   (C)   To make, print, or publish, or cause to be made, printed, or published
any notice, statement or advertisement, with respect to the sale or rental of a
dwelling that indicates any preference, limitation, or discrimination based on
race, color, religion, sex, handicap, familial status or national origin, or an
intention to make any such preference, limitation or discrimination.
   (D)   To represent to any person because of race, color, religion, sex,
handicap, familial status or national origin that any dwelling is not available
for inspection, sale or rental when such dwelling is in fact so available.
   (E)   For profit, to induce or attempt to induct any person to sell or rent
any dwelling by representations regarding the entry or perspective entry into
the neighborhood of a person or persons of a particular race, color, religion,
sex, handicap, familial status or national origin.
   (F)   To discriminate in the sale or rental, or to otherwise make
unavailable or deny, a dwelling to any buyer or renter because of a handicap
of:
      (1)   That buyer or renter;
      (2)   A person residing in or intending to reside in that dwelling after
it is so sold, rented, or made available; or
      (3)   Any person associated with that person.
   (G)   To discriminate against any person in the terms, conditions, or
privileges of sale or rental of a dwelling, or in the provision of services or
facilities in connection with such dwelling, because of a handicap of:
      (1)   That person;
      (2)   A person residing in or intending to reside in that dwelling after
it is so sold, rented, or made available; or
      (3)   Any person associated with that person.
   (H)   For purposes of this division, discrimination includes:
      (1)   A refusal to permit, at the expense of the handicapped person,
reasonable modifications of existing premises occupied or to be occupied by
such person if such modifications may be necessary to afford such person full
enjoyment of the premises except that, in the case of a rental, the landlord
may where it is reasonable to do so condition permission for a modification on
the renter agreeing to restore the interior of the premises to the condition
that existed before the modifications, reasonable wear and tear excepted;
      (2)   A refusal to make reasonable accommodations in rules, policies,
practices, or services when such accommodations may be necessary to afford such
person equal opportunity to use and enjoy a dwelling; or
      (3)   In connection with the design and construction of covered multi-
family dwellings for first occupancy after the date that is 30 months after
September 13, 1998, a failure to design and construct those dwellings in such a
manner that:
         (a)   The public use and common use portions of such dwellings are
readily accessible to and usable by handicapped persons;
         (b)   All the doors designed to allow passage into and within all
premises within such dwellings are sufficiently wide to allow passage by
handicapped persons in wheelchairs; and
         (c)   All premises within such dwellings contain the following
features of adaptive design:
            1.   An accessible route into and through the dwelling;
            2.   Light, switches, electrical outlets, thermostats, and other
environmental controls in accessible locations;
            3.   Reinforcements in bathrooms such that an individual in a
wheelchair can maneuver about the space.
   (I)   Compliance with the appropriate requirement Americans with
Disabilities Act of 1990 and of the American National Standard for Buildings
and Facilities providing accessibility an usability for physically handicapped
people (commonly cited as ANSIA117.1") suffices to satisfy the requirements of
division (H)(3)(c)(3.).
   (J)   Nothing in this section requires that a dwelling be made available to
an individual whose tenancy would constitute a direct threat to the health of
safety of other individuals of whose tenancy would result in substantial
physical damage to the property of others.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 91.05 DISCRIMINATION IN REAL ESTATE TRANSACTIONS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person or other entity whose business
includes engaging in residential real estate-related transactions to
discriminate against any person in making available such a transaction, or in
the terms or conditions of such a transaction, because of race, color,
religion, sex, handicap, familial status, or national origin.
   (B)   As used in this section, the term residential real estate-related
transaction means any of the following:
      (1)   The making or purchasing of loans or providing other financial
assistance:
         (a)   For purchasing, constructing, improving, repairing, or
maintaining a dwelling; or
         (b)   Secured by residential real estate.
      (2)   The selling, brokering, or appraising of residential real property.
   (C)   Nothing in this chapter prohibits a person engaged in the business of
furnishing appraisals of real property to take into consideration factors other
than race, color, religion, national origin, sex, handicap, or familial status.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 91.06 DISCRIMINATION IN BROKERAGE SERVICES.
   It shall be unlawful to deny any person access to or membership or
participation in any multiple-listing service, real estate brokers'
organization or other service, organization, or facility relating to the
business of selling or renting dwellings, or to discriminate against him in the
terms or conditions of such access, membership, or participation, on account of
race, color, religion, sex, handicap, familial status or national origin.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 91.07 INTERFERENCE, COERCION, OR INTIMIDATION.
   It shall be unlawful to coerce, intimidate, threaten, or interfere with any
person in the exercise or enjoyment of, or on account of his having exercised
or enjoyed, or on account of his having aided or encouraged any other person in
the exercise or enjoyment of, any right granted or protected by §§
91.03 through
91.06.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 91.08 PREVENTION OF INTIMIDATION.
   Whoever, whether or not acting under code or law, by force or threat of
force willfully injures, intimidates or interferes with, or attempt to injure,
intimidate or interfere with:
   (A)   Any person because of his race, color, religion, sex, handicap,
familial status, or national origin and because he is or has been selling,
purchasing, renting, financing, occupying, or contracting or negotiating for
the sale, purchase, rental, financing or occupation of any dwelling, or
applying for or participating in any service, organization, or facility
relating to the business of selling or renting dwellings; or
   (B)   Any person because he is or has been, or in order to intimidate such
person or any other person or any class of persons from:
      (1)   Participating, without discrimination on account of race, coior,
religion, sex, handicap, familial status, or national origin, in any of the
activities, services, organizations or facilities described in division (A); or
      (2)   Affording another person or class of persons opportunity or
protection so to participate; or
   (C)   Any citizen because he is or has been, or in order to discourage such
citizen or any other citizen from lawfully aiding or encouraging other persons
to participate, without discrimination on account of race, color, religion,
sex, handicap, familial status, or national origin, in any of the activities,
services, organizations or facilities described in subsection (A), or
participating lawfully in speech or peaceful assembly opposing any denial of
the opportunity to participate shall be fined according to local, state and
federal law; and if bodily injury results shall be fined not more than $10,000
or imprisoned not more than ten (10) years, or both; and if death results shall
be subject to imprisonment for any term of years or for life.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
§ 91.09 EQUAL ACCESS TO HOUSING IN HUD PROGRAMS.
   Pursuant to 24 CFR Part 5.403 and 24 CFR Part 574.3 the definition of
"family" is revised to include families regardless of the actual or perceived
sexual orientation, gender identity, or marital status of its members.
(Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
§ 91.10 EXEMPTIONS.
   (A)   Exemptions defined or set forth under I.C. 22-9.5-3 et seq. shall be
exempt from the provisions of this chapter to include those activities or
organizations set forth under divisions (B) and (C) of this section.
   (B)   Nothing in this chapter shall prohibit a religious organization,
association, or society, or any nonprofit institution or organization operated,
supervised or controlled by or in conjunction with a religious organization,
association, or society, from limiting the sale, rental or occupancy of
dwellings which it owns or operates for other than a commercial purpose to
persons of the same religion, or from giving preference to such persons, unless
membership in such religion is restricted on account of race, color or national
origin. Nor shall anything in this chapter prohibit a private club not in fact
open to the public, which as an incident to its primary purpose or purposes
provides lodgings which it owns or operates for other than a commercial
purpose, from limiting the rental or occupancy of such lodging to its members
or from giving preference to its members.
   (C)   Nothing in this chapter regarding familial status shall apply with
respect to housing for older persons. As used in this section, 'housing for
older persons' means housing:
      (1)   Provided under any state or federal program that the Secretary of
the Federal Department of Housing and Urban Development or the state civil
rights commission determines is specifically designed and operated to assist
elderly person (as defined in the state or federal program);
      (2)   Intended for, and solely occupied by, person 62 years of age or
older; or
      (3)   Intended and operated for occupancy by at least one person 55 years
of age or older per unit.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
§ 91.11 ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   The authority and responsibility for properly administering this
chapter and referral of complaints hereunder to the Commissioner as set forth
in division (B) hereof shall be vested in the Chief Elected Official of the
City of Boonville, Indiana.
   (B)   Notwithstanding the provisions of I.C. 22-9.5-4-8, the city, because
of lack of financial and other resources necessary to fully administer
enforcement proceedings and possible civil actions under the chapter, herein
elects to refer all formal complaints of violation of the articles of this
chapter by complainants to the Indiana Civil Rights Commission for
administrative enforcement actions pursuant to I.C. 22-9.5-6 and the Chief
Elected Official of the Town of Americana, Indiana, shall refer all said
complaints to the Commission as provided for under division (A) of this section
to said Commission for purposes of investigation, resolution and appropriate
relief as provided for under I.C. 22-9.5-6.
   (C)   All executive departments and agencies of the City of Boonville,
Indiana shall administer their departments, programs and activities relating to
housing and urban development in a manner affirmatively to further the purposes
of this chapter and shall cooperate with the Chief Elected Official and the
Commission to further such purposes.
   (D)   The Chief Elected Official of the City of Boonville, Indiana, or the
Chief Elected Official's designee, shall provide information on remedies
available to any aggrieved person or complainant requesting such information.
(Ord. 1994-1, passed 1-27-94; Am. Ord. 2014-5, passed 7-3-14)
CHAPTER 92: STREETS AND SIDEWALKS
Section
   
92.01   Occupation of public rights-of-way
   
92.02   Sidewalk rehabilitation cost sharing program
   
92.03   Snow and ice removal on sidewalks and curb ramps in public rights-of-
way
§ 92.01 OCCUPATION OF PUBLIC RIGHTS-OF-WAY.
   (A)   This section applies to all activities and work performed by any
person, partnership, corporation or other entity, including departments,
divisions, agencies or boards, which are hereinafter referred to as
"registrant", in, on, under and over public rights-of-way under the
jurisdiction of the city. Registrants desiring to work in, on, under or over
the public right-of-way shall obtain all applicable permits as required by the
city. In performing such work, the registrant shall be bound by the standards,
regulations, procedures, and guidelines set forth by this section and any and
all federal, state, and local regulations.
   (B)   The Board of Works shall be responsible for controlling all activities
and work performed by Registrants and for enforcing compliance with the
provisions of regulations adopted by the Board of Works.
   (C)   Facilities located in public right-of-way prior to implementation of
this section shall file a registration statement to the Board of Works. The
registration statement of each utility, entity, or person having any facility
within the public rights-of-way shall contain the following:
      (1)   The identity and legal status of the registrant, including any
affiliates who own or operate any facilities in the public rights-of-way.
      (2)   The name, address, and telephone number of the officer, agent, or
employee responsible for the accuracy of the registration statement.
      (3)   A general description of services that the registrant provides or
intends to provide or offer to entities or persons within the city through the
utilization of facilities in the public right-of-way.
      (4)   Such other information as the Board of Works may reasonably
require.
   (D)   The Board of Works shall adopt regulations which apply only to work
performed in, on, under and/or over public rights-of-way and to the permits,
procedures, requirements, standards and fees which are associated with such
work. Those regulations may include, but are not limited to, regulations
establishing the following:
      (1)   Standards for traffic controls for construction and maintenance
operations or activities in, on, under and over the public rights-of-way, which
standards shall be designed to protect members of the public using such public
rights-of-way and to be consistent, to the extent practicable, with the
"Indiana Manual of Uniform Traffic Control Devices," the "Indiana Department of
Transportation Standard Specifications, 1995 edition," and all other state and
federal statutes, recognizing, however, that traffic controls for projects
within public rights-of-way subject to the jurisdiction of the department may
be less stringent than the traffic controls required in connection with work on
state or federal highways where traffic will be traveling at much greater
speeds;
      (2)   Restrictions with respect to when and how work should be performed
in public rights-of-way in certain geographical areas, including but not
limited to the Downtown Square area, schools, hospitals and health care
facilities, and emergency services;
      (3)   Time periods when work in, under, on or over public rights-of-way
should, except in cases of emergencies, be prohibited or limited;
      (4)   Minimum standards for compaction or deflection testing or both;
      (5)   Bonding or insurance requirements;
      (6)   Enforcement procedures;
      (7)   Standards for right-of-way excavations;
      (8)   Standards for restoration of the public right-of-way;
      (9)   Inspection and testing procedures;
      (10)   Applicability of standards adopted by the Board;
      (11)   Variance procedure;
      (12)   Appeal procedure;
      (13)   Form and content of the right-of-way excavation permit;
      (14)   Kinds and categories of permits;
      (15)   Permit fees;
      (16)   Permit issuance procedures;
      (17)   Indemnification provisions;
      (18)   Emergency permit notification procedures; and
      (19)   Permit verification procedures.
   (E)   (1)   It shall be unlawful for the registrant to perform any work,
including, but not limited to, cutting, drilling, digging or excavating in, on,
over or under a public right-of-way without first having obtained a permit from
the Board of Works. A violation of this section is subject to the enforcement
procedures and penalties; provided, however, the fine imposed for such
violation shall not be less than one hundred dollars ($100.00), and each day
that an offense continues shall constitute a separate violation. Any fines
collected under this section to be deposited into an account for the use and
benefit of the Board of Works.
      (2)   In the event an emergency arises that affects the health and safety
of the public or requires the restoration of a utility service and such an
event occurs at a time other than normal business hours of the Board of Works,
work may be performed in, on, over or under the public right-of-way without
first obtaining a permit. If such event were to occur, the registrant
performing such work must file for a permit from the Board of Works on the
first business day following the commencement or performance of the work.
   (F)   All work including, but not limited to, the restoration of pavement,
sidewalks or grass areas, shall be done by the registrant at their expense,
unless the city shall elect to do the work and change the cost thereof to the
utility, entity, or person. Such work shall be subject to the supervision and
approval of the Board of Works.
(Ord. 2020-5, passed 7-30-20)
§ 92.02 SIDEWALK REHABILITATION COST SHARING PROGRAM.
   (A)   The City of Boonville Sidewalk Rehabilitation and Repair Cost Sharing
Program (the "Program") is hereby established in the city.
   (B)   A landowner within the city owning property over or through which a
sidewalk runs may apply to be part of the program ("applicant"). Applications
will be prioritized by the city according to need and available funding.
Applicant must submit the city's sidewalk rehabilitation and repair cost
sharing program application form to City Hall to initiate the program.
   (C)   In order to be eligible, the applicant must covenant and agree to hold
harmless the city, and any representative, employee, and agent of the city from
any cost and or liability thereof resulting from said work.
   (D)   The City Engineer shall develop standard drawings for sidewalks. Any
work completed pursuant to the program shall be completed in accordance with
the city's standard drawings.
   (E)   Once an applicant is approved by the city for the program, the
applicant must obtain three (3) written quotes for the sidewalk rehabilitation
and/or repair work ("work"). The quotes for the work must be obtained from a
list of contractors preapproved by the city. Any contractor can request to be
preapproved by the city so long as said contractor is qualified, adequately
insured, and maintains a performance bond with the city for the cost of
completion of any work. Once three (3) written quotes are obtained, a summary
of quotes should be provided on the application form for final approval. The
chosen contractor shall provide the city with a certificate of insurance which
shall include liability coverage in the amount of $500,000. Contractor must
supply the Clerk-Treasurer with a current IRS Form W-9.
   (F)   The applicant shall provide the city with a copy of each quote
received for the work. If the specific quote and work is approved by the city,
the applicant may then proceed with the work by a preapproved contractor. The
applicant shall be responsible for the work. Contractor and owner are
responsible for cleanup of all construction work including, but not limited to,
forms, spilled material, and other debris generated by the project. Upon
completion of the work, the city will pay either the applicant or the
contractor for one-half of the cost of the work, not to exceed one-half of the
city-approved low quote.
   (G)   The city may, in its discretion, determine whether any particular
sidewalk is in need of repair or rehabilitation. In the event that a landowner
applies for the program, but the city does not agree that the sidewalk is in
need of repair or rehabilitation, then the city may determine that said
landowner is not eligible for the program.
   (H)   Nothing in this section or the program shall obligate the city to
repair or rehabilitate any sidewalks. Further, nothing in this section or the
program shall make the city liable or otherwise responsible for any sidewalk,
any of the work, any continuing maintenance, or any other costs.
   (I)   If the applicant defaults in its obligation to pay the contractor, the
city shall not be liable for paying the contractor the 50% of contractor's bill
owed by the applicant, and the applicant covenants and agrees to hold city
harmless against any attempts by contractor to collect said bill.
   (J)   Construction shall commence within sixty (60) days of signed
agreement. Should the applicant fail to begin construction within sixty (60)
days from the signed agreement, the city may extend additional time. The city
may elect to terminate the cost sharing agreement with written notice provided
to the applicant.
   (K)   The city will earmark a set amount of funds to be utilized by the
sidewalk replacement program. Upon depletion of the allocated funds for a
calendar year, a waiting list may be initiated for such time as funding is
available. Priority will be given on a first come, first serve basis as
permitted by funding. Upon receipt of multiple requests, the city will consider
walkways with the greatest public good and may prioritize funding. The vote of
the Common Council shall be final in determining priority projects. At its
discretion, the city may suspend this program due to funding constraints.
(Ord. 2018-9, passed 8-2-18; Am. Ord. 2019-1, passed 2-13-19)
§ 92.03 SNOW AND ICE REMOVAL ON SIDEWALKS AND CURB RAMPS IN PUBLIC RIGHTS-OF-
WAY.
   (A)   The city places a ban on the use of any salt product, products
containing chlorides, or products containing corrosive chemicals for the
removal of snow and ice on sidewalks and curb ramps within public right-of-way.
   (B)   In lieu of the ban on the use of salt products, products containing
chlorides, or products containing corrosive chemicals, the city recommends the
use of the following:
      (1)   Natural de-icers such as sand;
      (2)   UREA 46-0-0;
      (3)   Liquid anti-icing or de-icing agents not containing corrosive
chemicals or chlorides.
   (C)   Any person found to be in violation of this section shall be subject
to a fine of fifty dollars ($50.00). Each subsequent violation shall be subject
to an additional fine of fifty dollars ($50.00) per occurrence. Fines shall be
cumulative.
(Ord. 2020-8, passed 8-11-20)

§ 93.01 AUTHORITY.
   The Common Council of the City of Boonville, hereby re-vests itself with
full power and authority to establish, maintain and equip playgrounds and
recreation centers and for other purposes on the new waterworks lake property.
(Ord. 1950-1, passed 6-6-50)
§ 93.02 PROHIBITED ACTIVITY AND BEHAVIOR.
   (A)   No person in a public park shall:
      (1)   Willfully mark deface, disfigure, injure, tamper with or displace
or remove, any building, bridges, tables, benches, fireplaces, railings, paving
or paving material, waterlines or other public utilities or parts or
appurtenances thereof, signs, notices or placards whether temporary or
permanent, monuments, stakes, posts or other boundary markers, or other
structures or equipment, facilities or park property or appurtenances
whatsoever, either real or personal;
      (2)   Fail to co-operate in maintaining rest rooms and washrooms in a
neat and sanitary condition. No person over the age of five (5) years shall use
the rest rooms and washrooms designated for the opposite sex;
      (3)   Throw, discharge, or otherwise place or cause to be placed in the
waters of any fountain, pond, lake, stream, bay, spray park or other body of
water in or adjacent to any park or any tributary, stream, storm sewer, or
drain flowing into such waters, any substance, matter or things, liquid or
solid, which will or may result in the pollution of said waters; or
      (4)   Have brought in or shall dump, deposit or leave any bottles, broken
glass, ashes, paper, boxes, cans, dirt, rubbish, waste, garbage, or refuse; or
other trash. No such refuse or trash shall be placed in any waters in or
contiguous to any park, or left anywhere on the grounds thereof, but shall be
placed in the proper receptacles are not so provided, all such rubbish or waste
shall be carried away from the park by the person responsible for its presence,
and property disposed of elsewhere.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful for any person to smoke inside the pool area and
spray park area of the public park.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful to collect, gather or be a member of any
disorderly crowd, or any crowd gathered for any lawful purpose.
   (D)   It shall be unlawful for any animal to be in the pool and spray park
area. All animals in park must be leashed. Owners responsible for removal of
animal’s waste. Owner is responsible for animal’s behavior.
   (E)   It shall be unlawful for any person to have in his or her possession,
custody or control any alcoholic beverage, (including beer), drugs, dope,
narcotics, marijuana, opium or heroin, of any kind whatsoever.
   (F)   No person in a public park shall:
      (1)   Expose or offer for sale any article or thing, nor shall he station
or place any stand, cart or vehicle for the transportation, sale or display of
any such article or thing. Exception is here made as to any regularly licensed
concessionaire acting by and under the authority and regulation of the City of
Boonville.
      (2)   Announce, advertise or call the public attention in any way to any
article or service for sale or hire.
      (3)   Paste, glue, tack or otherwise post any sign, placard,
advertisement or inscription whatever, nor shall any person erect or cause to
be erected any sign whatever on any public lands or highways or roads adjacent
to a park.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 93.03 HOURS OF OPERATION.
   (A)   Hours. The public park shall be opened daily. Any section, or part of
the park, may be declared closed to the public by the Park Director or Aquatic
Director at any time and for any interval of time, either temporarily or at
regular or stated intervals.
   (B)   Hours for swimming pool. Hours for public swimming and Spray Park
shall be posted annually. Directors may close the swimming pool and spray park
during inclement weather, where unsanitary water conditions exist, or for any
special events.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10)
§ 93.04 GROUP ACTIVITY.
   (A)   Whenever any group, association or organization desires to use the
park facilities for a particular purpose, such as picnics, parties or
theatrical or entertainment performances, a representative of the group,
association or organization shall first obtain a permit from the City of
Boonville for such purposes.
   (B)   The City shall grant the application if it appears that the group,
association or organization will not interfere with the general use of the park
by the individual members of the public and if the group, association or
organization meets all other conditions in the application, the application may
contain a requirement for an indemnity bond to protect the city from any
liability of any kind or character and to protect city property from damage,
and shall contain such a provision if the swimming pool or spray park is to be
utilized by the group.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10)
§ 93.05 PICNIC AREAS.
   No person in a public park shall:
   (A)   Picnic or lunch in a place other than those designated for that
purpose. Attendants shall have the authority to regulate the activities in such
areas when necessary to prevent congestion and to secure the maximum use for
the comfort and convenience of all. Visitors shall comply with any directions
given to achieve this end; or
   (B)   Leave a picnic area before the fire is completely extinguished and
before all trash in the nature of boxes, papers, cans, bottles, garbage and
other refuse is placed in the disposal receptacles where provided. If no such
trash receptacles are available, then refuse and trash shall be carried away
from the park area by the picnicker to be properly disposed of elsewhere.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 93.06 SWIMMING IN CITY LAKES PROHIBITED.
   No person in a public park shall:
   (A)   Swim, bathe, or wade in any waters or waterways in or adjacent to any
park, except in such waters and at such places as are provided therefore, and
in compliance with such regulations as are herein set forth or may be hereafter
adopted.
   (B)   No person shall appear in bathing costume at any place in the parks
except within the limits of designated bathing places or areas, and all bathing
costumes shall conform to commonly accepted standards.
   (C)   Dress or undress on any beach, or in any vehicle, toilet or other
place, except in such bathing houses or structures as may be provided for that
purpose.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 93.07 MOTOR VEHICLES.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to operate a motor vehicle in any area
in the public park in excess of posted speed limit, and no motor vehicle shall
be operated any place in the park except upon roadways or parking areas
provided by for motor vehicles.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10) Penalty, see §
10.99
§ 93.08 FISHING IN LAKES; LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   The Common Council of the City of Boonville, Indiana, does hereby
permit fishing in the lakes owned by the City of Boonville, Indiana, under
license, said license to be granted only to persons over 14 years of age,
applying for the same under the following conditions and regulations.
   (B)   Any person over 14 years of age desiring to fish in said lakes may
obtain a license to do so by making a written application to the Clerk-
Treasurer of the City of Boonville, Indiana, if it shall therein appear that
said applicant is a resident of the city of Boonville, said applicant shall pay
a fee of One ($1.00) Dollar per annum therefor; and if said application shows
said applicant is a non-resident of the City of Boonville said applicant shall
pay a fee of Five ($5.00) Dollars per annum. Upon receipt of such fee said
Clerk-Treasurer of the City of Boonville shall issue to such applicant a
fishing license.
   (C)   All fishing licenses herein issued shall expire December 31st of the
year in which the same is issued.
   (D)   All proceeds collected by the Clerk-Treasurer from the sale of fishing
license shall be applied to a special fund for the Boonville Park and
Recreation Board for the use of such Boonville Park and Recreation Board in
beautifying and improving said lakes and the grounds thereof.
   (E)   It shall be unlawful for anyone of the age of 14 years or over to fish
in said lakes without having first obtained said fishing license.
(Ord. 1963-3, passed 4-16-63)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 93.09 ADMINISTRATION; PERMITS.
   (A)   The Parks Director and Aquatics Director will both report to the Park
and Recreation Board ("Board"). The Board will not micro-manage, but all hires
(names submitted for approval) and policy decisions will come before the Board.
   (B)   The Park Director and Aquatic Director and any member of the Park and
Recreation Board or any member of the City Police Department, shall enforce the
provisions of this article and any other ordinance relating to the use of the
public park.
(Ord. 1970-10, passed 10-1-70; Am. Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10)
§ 93.10 CONTROL OF SANITARY CONDITIONS AT CITY LAKE PARK.
   (A)   There shall be no feeding of waterfowl at City Lake Park.
   (B)   All dogs at City Lake Park must be restrained on leashes.
   (C)   Any animal defecation must be removed immediately by owner.
(Ord. 2004-7, passed 4-1-04)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 93.99 PENALTY.
   Any person, firm or corporation violating any of the provisions of this
chapter by failing, neglecting or refusing to comply with the provisions of any
notice herein provided for, within ten (10) days after the service thereof, or,
who shall resist or obstruct in carrying out the provisions of this ordinance
shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable by a fine as set by the City of
Boonville for each offense. A separate offense shall be deemed committed on
each day during or on which a violation occurs or continues.
(Ord. 2010-2, passed 5-6-10)

**** WEEDS ****
§ 94.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purposes of this chapter the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning:
   CONTINUAL ABATEMENT NOTICE.  A statement included on the initial notice
informing the property owner if there are further violations of the weed
ordinance throughout the year and the city mows the weeds on the owner's
behalf, the property owner will receive notice and be invoiced at the property
instead of by certified mail. This notice can be delivered at the time of the
mowing.
   DEPARTMENT. The Board of Works, which shall have the responsibility of
administering the City of Boonville's Weed and Rank Vegetation Ordinance.
   INITIAL NOTICE. The first weed abatement notice of the calendar year sent
through certified mail, return receipt requested, or an equivalent service
permitted under I.C. 1 -1 -7-1.
   OWNER. The owner of record as found in the records of the Auditor of Warrick
County.
   OWNER'S ADDRESS. The most recent mailing address of the owner and being that
address to which the County Treasurer sends tax duplicates.
   RANK VEGETATION. Any plant growth which is or may be harmful to the senses,
health or well-being of citizens of the jurisdiction.
   WEEDS.  Any growth of vegetation, other than trees, bushes, shrubs,
ornamental plants, or plants cultivated in an orderly manner for the purpose of
producing food. Trees, bushes or shrubs which have sprouted as volunteers and
are growing along fence line(s), structures and public right-of-ways, shall be
considered to be WEEDS .
(Ord. 1970-5, passed 6-4-70; Am. Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2004-18,
passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2014-1, passed 4-3-14) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.02 PROHIBITIONS.
   (A)   No owner of real property shall allow any growth of weeds in excess of
eight (8) inches in height upon his property.
   (B)   No owner of real property shall allow the growth of rank vegetation
upon his property.
(Ord. 1970-5, passed 6-4-70; Am. Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2004-18,
passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2004-19, passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2014-1, passed 4-3-14)
Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.03 NOTICE TO REMOVE.
   (A)   Notice to owner - content.
      (1)   For any property found to be in violation of §
94.02, the Department shall issue a notice to remove weeds or rank vegetation
to the owner giving the owner five (5) calendar days notice to remove the
vegetation. The notice shall also state that if the vegetation is not removed
within the five (5) days, the Department will have the vegetation removed,
either by its own workers and equipment or by a contractor hired by the
Department for this purpose. This notice must also state that if the vegetation
is removed by the Department or its agent, the owner will be liable for the
cost incurred by the Department in removing the vegetation.
      (2)   Subsequent notice. One certified mail with return receipt notice
will be sent per property per calendar year hereinafter referred to as the
'initial notice'. Any additional weed violations in the same calendar year will
be handled by a 'continual abatement notice'. The continual abatement notice
will be included on the initial notice which allows the city to mow the
property on the owner's behalf, receiving notice at the property instead of by
certified mail and can be delivered at the time of the mowing.
   (B)   Manner of legal services. Any initial notice to be given hereunder
shall be deemed sufficiently given when placed in an envelope directed to the
person to be notified at the owner’s address as defined in §
94.01 and deposited in a United States Post Office mailbox postage prepaid.
Such postal notices shall be by certified mail, return receipt requested.
Should the return show that the person to be notified has refused delivery of
such certified letter or that such certified letter was unclaimed by the
addressee or unable to be forwarded to the addressee, this refusal or failure
to claim such letter shall constitute sufficient service hereunder.
(Ord. 1970-5, passed 6-4-70; Am. Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2014-1,
passed 4-3-14)
§ 94.04 REMOVAL OF VEGETATION BY DEPARTMENT.
   The Department shall inspect each property for which a notice to remove
weeds and rank vegetation has been issued at least five (5) calendar days from
the date of delivery as noted on the return receipt or if service is by
publication, at least five (5) calendar days from the date of the second
publication. If the vegetation has not been removed by the owner at the time of
this inspection, the Department may have it removed utilizing its own employees
and equipment or a contractor engaged by the Department pursuant to a public
bidding process. In the event that the vegetation is located in an enclosed
area, the entry to which is limited by a locked gate, the Department or its
contractor shall have the authority to remove the lock and enter onto the
property for the purpose of removing vegetation that is in violation of the
ordinance.
(Ord. 1970-5, passed 6-4-70; Am. Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2014-1,
passed 4-3-14)
§ 94.05 LIABILITY FOR COSTS FOR REMOVAL OF VEGETATION.
   (A)   When rank vegetation is removed by action of the Department or its
contractor under §
94.04, the owner(s) of record at the time the notice to remove rank vegetation
was sent are jointly and severally liable for the following costs:
      (1)   The actual cost of the work performed by the department or the bid
price of work accomplished by a contractor under §
94.04; and
      (2)   An amount that represents a reasonable forecast of the average
processing expense that will be incurred by the Department in taking the
technical, administrative and legal actions concerning rank vegetation
violations that are necessary to remove rank vegetation may be performed by the
Department or by a contractor under §
94.04. In calculating the amount of the average processing expense, the
following costs may be considered:
         (a)   The cost of obtaining reliable information about the identity
and location of the owner;
         (b)   The cost of preparing and mailing notices;
         (c)   The cost of publishing notices;
         (d)   The salaries and fringe benefits of employees;
         (e)   The pro-rated cost of motor vehicle operating costs for
Department owned vehicles or the cost of mileage for use of employees' private
vehicles; and
         (f)   The cost of supplies, equipment and office space.
   (B)   (1)   When grass and/or weeds are removed by action of the Department
or its contractor under §
94.04, the owners(s) of record at the time the notice to remove grass and/or
weeds was sent are jointly and severally liable for the costs of removal.
      (2)   The costs of typical grass and/or weed removal by the City will be
$200.00 whether the City has to mow or trim any portion of any property,
premises, lot or sidewalk within the City of Boonville.
      (3)   The costs of atypical grass and/or weed removal by the City will
be:
         (a)   The actual cost of the work performed by the department or the
bid price of work accomplished by a contractor under §
94.04; and
         (b)   An amount that represents a reasonable forecast of the average
processing expense that will be incurred by the Department in taking the
technical, administrative and legal actions concerning grass and/or weed
violations that are necessary to remove weeds and/or grass may be performed by
the Department or by a contractor under §
94.04. In calculating the amount of the average processing expense, the
following costs may be considered:
            1.   The cost of obtaining reliable information about the identity
and location of the owner;
            2.   The cost of preparing and mailing notices;
            3.   The cost of publishing notices;
            4.   The salaries and fringe benefits of employees;
            5.   The pro-rated cost of motor vehicle operating costs for
department owned vehicles or the cost of mileage for use of employees' private
vehicles; and
            6.   The cost of supplies, equipment and office space.
(Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2004-19, passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2014-1,
passed 4-3-14)
§ 94.06 NOTICE OF COSTS INCURRED IN REMOVAL OF VEGETATION.
   (A)   Upon the initial ordinance violation, the Department shall send a
notice of cost of removal of vegetation to owner after completion of the
removal and calculation by the Department of its costs incurred in removing the
vegetation or upon receipt by the Department of an invoice from the contractor
which removed the vegetation. The notice shall include the property address
from which the vegetation was removed, the date on which the vegetation was
removed, the cost of the actual removal of vegetation.
   (B)   Upon subsequent ordinance violations, the invoice will be included
with the continual abatement notice and payable within 15 days. Unpaid invoices
plus associated recording, certified mail and lien release fees will be filed
as a lien with the Warrick County Auditor and Recorded in the office of the
Recorder of Warrick County, Indiana.
(Ord. 1995-3, passed 3-9-95; Am. Ord. 2014-1, passed 4-3-14)
§ 94.99 PENALTY.
   The penalties for violation of this chapter shall be one hundred dollars ($
100.00) for each violation. Unpaid fines/citations shall be filed as a lien
with the Warrick County Auditor and recorded in the Recorder’s Office of
Warrick County, Indiana.
(Ord. 2004-18, passed 8-5-04; Am. Ord. 2014-1, passed 4-3-14)
CHAPTER 95: ANIMALS
Section

**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 95.01 CRUELTY PROHIBITED.
   No person shall cruelly treat any animal in the City in any way; any person
who inhumanely beats, under feeds, overloads or abandons any animal shall be
deemed guilty of a violation of this section.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.02 DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to permit any dangerous animal or vicious animal
of any kind to run at large within the City; exhibitions or parades of animals
which are feral naturae in the eyes of the law may be conducted only upon
securing a permit from the chief of police.
   (B)   The members of the police department or any other person in the City,
are authorized to kill any dangerous animals of any kind when it is necessary
for the protection of any person or property.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
§ 95.03 NOISES AND ODORS.
   It shall be unlawful to harbor or keep any animals which disturb the peace
by loud noises at any time of the day or night, or which produces odors which
disturb the peace, health, comfort or sanitary conditions of the neighborhood.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.04 STRAY ANIMALS.
   It shall be unlawful to permit any cattle, horse, swine, sheep, goats or
poultry to run at large in the City; any such animal running at large in any
public place in the City shall be impounded as provided by ordinance. It shall
further be unlawful to picket or tie any such animal in any of the streets of
the City for the purpose of grazing or feeding.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.05 DISEASED ANIMALS.
   (A)   No domestic animal afflicted with a contagious or infectious disease
shall be allowed to run at large, or to be exposed in any public place whereby
the health of man or beast may be affected; nor shall such diseased animal be
shipped or removed from the premises of the owner thereof, except under the
supervision of the chief of police or the health officer.
   (B)   It is hereby made the duty of the health officer to secure such
disposition of any diseased animal and such treatment of affected premises as
to prevent the communication and spread of the contagion or infection, except
in cases where the state veterinarian is empowered to act.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.06 HOUSING; SHELTER REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   No person shall cause or allow any stable or place where any animal is
or may be kept to become unclean, or unwholesome, and it shall be unlawful to
keep any live swine or pigs in the City.
   (B)   It shall be unlawful to keep or have any goats anywhere in the City,
or any cattle, horses, sheep or chickens within one hundred fifty (150) feet of
any residence, other than the residence of the person so keeping or having such
animals anywhere in the City for a period of more than Twenty Four (24) hours.
   (C)   Every person maintaining a poultry or animal yard shall keep the same
clean and sanitary, and free from all refuse. Such yards shall be thoroughly
swept at least once every twenty-four hours and it shall be unlawful to permit
any decaying food, or any refuse of any kind, to remain in such yard. Refuse
from such yards shall, when swept up or collected, be kept in airtight
containers until disposed of in accordance with the ordinances of the City, and
it shall be unlawful to permit any such refuse to remain uncovered.
   (D)   Every poultry or animal yard shall be adequately enclosed so as to be
impenetrable against rats or other rodents. The presence of any rats in any
poultry or animal yard shall be prima facie evidence that such yard is
maintained in violation of the provisions of this section.
(Ord. 1967-12, passed 7-6-67)
Penalty, see § 10.99
**** DOG AND CAT REGULATIONS; LICENSING ****
§ 95.20 RUNNING AT LARGE.
   No dog nor cat shall be permitted to run at large in the city at any time.
(Ord. 1980-1, passed 2-14-80)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.21 REGISTRATION; LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   No dog shall be permitted to be or remain in the city without being
licensed as hereinafter provided.
   (B)   (1)   All dogs kept in the city shall be registered as to sex, breed,
name and address of the owner and name of dog. At the time of such registration
such owner shall obtain a license for such dog and shall pay a fee of One
($1.00) Dollar for each male and spayed female dog and a fee of Two ($2.00)
Dollars for each unspayed female dog.
      (2)   It shall be the duty of said owner to cause such license tag to be
securely attached around the dog's neck and kept there at all times during the
license period.
   (C)   No person owning any dog or cat shall suffer or permit such dog to
disturb the peace and quiet of the neighborhood by making loud or unusual
noises.
   (D)   Any dog or cat running at large in the city and any dog found in the
city without a license is hereby declared a nuisance and shall be impounded as
hereinafter provided.
(Ord. 1980-1, passed 2-14-80)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 95.22 POUNDKEEPER; IMPOUNDMENT PROCEDURE.
   (A)   The poundkeeper is hereby charged with the duty of impounding all dogs
and cats running at large in violation of the provisions of this chapter. He
shall be special police officer and shall be and is hereby authorized to make
all necessary arrests in carrying out the provisions of this chapter.
   (B)   The owner or owners of any dogs or cats impounded hereunder may redeem
the same by paying the sum of Two ($2.00) Dollars for each day said dog or cat
has been impounded up to the time of making the redemption, and when the same
are paid to said poundkeeper it shall be his duty and he shall release the dog,
or cat from said pound and deliver it to the owner thereof.
   (C)   (1)   Immediately after impounding any dog or cat hereunder, it shall
be the duty of the poundkeeper to enter upon the records of the pound in a book
to be kept by him for such purposes the date of impounding, a description of
the dog or cat impounded, and a record as to whether or not such dog has been
licensed and tagged as required by the ordinances.
      (2)   Public notice of the impounding of such dog or cat shall be given
by posting one copy of the description of such dog or cat and date of
impounding at the pound and one copy of such notice at the door of the city
hall. Any such dog or cat not redeemed by the owner thereof within five (5)
days after the posting of such notice by the poundkeeper shall be and he is
hereby declared to be a public nuisance. The poundkeeper shall immediately
thereafter kill or destroy such dog or cat.
   (D)   It shall be the duty of the poundkeeper to cause notice to be served
by mail upon the registered owner of any licensed dog impounded under the
provisions of this chapter, such notice to be mailed at least two (2) days
prior to the posting of the notice of impounding hereinbefore provided.
(Ord. 1980-1, passed 2-14-80)
§ 95.23 BITING DOGS; QUARANTINE.
   Whenever, any dog bites a person, the owner of the said dog shall
immediately notify the chief of police who shall order the dog held on the
owner's premises or shall have it impounded for a period of two (2) weeks. The
dog shall be examined immediately after it has bitten anyone and again at the
end of the two (2) week period. If at the end of two (2) weeks a veterinarian
is convinced that the dog is then free from rabies the dog shall be released
from quarantine or from the pound as the case may be. If the dog dies in the
meanwhile its head shall be sent to the state department of health for
examination of rabies.
(Ord. 1980-1, passed 2-14-80)

TITLE XI: BUSINESS REGULATIONS
Chapter

CHAPTER 110: GENERAL BUSINESS PROVISIONS
Section
   
110.01   Taxicabs; transportation for hire
   
110.02   Junkyards
   
110.03   Cable television systems
Cross-reference:
   Solid Waste Collection, see §§
51.25 through
51.31
§ 110.01 TAXICABS; TRANSPORTATION FOR HIRE.
   (A)   No taxicab or motor vehicle used for the transportation of passengers
for hire upon the streets in the City of Boonville shall operate within the
corporate limits of the City of Boonville until its owner or operator shall
first obtain a license from the Common Council of the City of Boonville.
   (B)   (1)   As a condition precedent to the issuance of any license for the
operation of a taxicab or motor vehicle for the transportation of passengers
for hire, the applicant therefor must obtain first a certificate of public
convenience and necessity for each motor vehicle used for the transportation of
passengers for hire issued by the Board of Public Works and Safety of said
City. The Common Council of the City of Boonville shall, in its first meeting
of each new calendar year determine how many of such certificates of public
conveniences and necessity shall be issued by the Board of Public Works and
Safety during that calendar year, and no more than the number determined by the
Common Council shall be issued by the Board of Public Works and Safety; that
for the calendar year 1957, the Board of Public Works and Safety may issue up
to and including five (5) certificates of public convenience and necessity.
      (2)   The Board of Public Works and Safety may issue a certificate of
public convenience and necessity to the applicant for each motor vehicle used
in the transportation of passengers for hire after first ascertaining the
applicant's ability to meet the requirements as set forth in this section and
after an affirmative finding for the need of the service offered by said
applicant.
   (C)   Certificates of public convenience and necessity shall be deemed
renewable, each year, for a period of five (5) years, by the holder of the
annual license and registration fees. At the end of the fifth year for any
issued certificate of public convenience and necessity any certificate holder
must make new application to the City for the issuance of a new certificate.
   (D)   Before obtaining a license from the Common Council of said City for
the operation of a taxicab or motor vehicle for the transportation of
passengers for hire in said City, the applicant shall first place on file in
the City Hall an indemnity insurance policy conditioned to protect passengers
of members of the public generally who may be injured as a result of the
operation of any taxicab or motor vehicle for the transportation of passengers
for hire which may be owned or operated by said applicant and conditioned
further to protect against property damage occasioned by the operation of such
vehicles; such insurance policy shall have indemnity limits at least equal to
those required by the State of Indiana for such vehicles, and said indemnity
amounts may be fixed by the Common Council of the City of Boonville in amounts
higher than is required by the State of Indiana generally.
   (E)   No license shall be issued on any certificate of public convenience
and necessity to any operator of any taxicab or motor vehicle for the
transportation of passengers for hire who is under the age of twenty-one (21)
years or to one who is not of good moral character.
   (F)   Each applicant for a license for the operation of a taxicab or motor
vehicle for the transportation of passengers for hire shall present a schedule
of proposed rates and charges for the transportation of passengers within the
city for the approval of the Common Council. No rates or charges so approved
shall be changed until such licensee shall first present any proposed change in
the schedule of rates and charges to the Common Council and have the same
approved.
   (G)   (1)   Each holder of a certificate of public convenience and necessity
for any motor vehicles for the transportation of passengers for hire shall pay
to the City of Boonville, at the City Hall, an annual license fee in the amount
of $25.00 together with an annual registration fee in the amount of $5.00 for
each taxicab or motor vehicle used for the transportation of passengers for
hire within said City. Such license and registration fees shall be payable on
or before the 31st day of January of each year or upon the issuance thereof.
      (2)   The Clerk-Treasurer, upon the payment of said license fee together
with the registration fee, and upon the presentation of a certificate of public
convenience and necessity for each motor vehicle used for the transportation of
passengers for hire shall issue to said certificate holder a license, together
with city registration plates for each taxicab or motor vehicle used by said
certificate holder for the transportation of passengers for hire, which
registration plate shall be affixed to each taxicab or motor vehicle used for
such transportation of passengers for hire. No motor vehicle for the
transportation of passengers for hire shall be operated within said city
without having had registration plates issued therefor and attached thereto.
   (H)   The City of Boonville may revoke or suspend any such license issued
for good cause, by giving written notice to such license holder and after
giving such license holder an opportunity for a hearing, if so demanded.
   (I)   (1)   The vehicle or vehicles, equipment and premises of each license
holder shall be inspected semi-annually from the date of issuance of such
license by an Inspection Commission, consisting of the Chief of Police of said
City, the City Mechanic and a third person nominated by the Mayor
      (2)   Such Inspection Commission shall have the authority to issue orders
requiring any license holder to correct and deficiency in any equipment of the
condition of the premises which may be detrimental to the public welfare and
safety.
   (J)   No license or certificate of public convenience and necessity shall be
deemed saleable or transferable unless the holder of such license or
certificate of public convenience and necessity first obtain the consent of the
Common Council of the City of Boonville to such sale or transfer, and the
Common Council approves the transferee as the future holder of such license or
certificate of public convenience and necessity.
(Ord. 1956-10, passed 1-1-57)
Penalty, see § 10.99
Cross-reference:

system operating in the City, notwithstanding any different or inconsistent
provisions in the franchise; and
   (B)   In connection with such regulation, the City will ensure a reasonable

   (C)   The Mayor or his designee, is authorized to execute on behalf of the
City and file with the FCC such certification forms or other instruments as are

the City to regulate basic service rates and charges.
