**** GENERAL PROVISIONS ****
§ 111.01 BUCKET SHAKES PROHIBITED.
   (A)   No person shall engage in the collection of funds while standing on a
street or highway in the City of Boonville (generally known as “Bucket Shake”)
without first having obtained written permission issued therefor as provided in
this section.
   (B)   The word “person” shall mean any human being, partnership, association
or corporation.
   (C)   All persons collecting funds as herein provided shall be permitted to
collect funds only on the corner of Main Street, intersection of Second and
Locust streets and Third Street on Saturday between the hours of 8:00 a.m. to
12:00 noon.
   (D)   All persons collecting funds must have a permit issued by the State of
Indiana to collect funds while standing upon a State Highway. The Indiana State
permit must be shown to Police Chief before permission for “bucket shake” is
granted.
   (E)   All persons collecting funds as herein provided shall present proof to
Police Chief that the collection of funds is to be used solely by a not-for-
profit organization.
   (F)   All persons collecting funds in the designated streets must be
eighteen (18) years of age or older. Said persons must be wearing an orange
item or apparel while collecting funds.
   (G)   All persons shall identify the purpose and/or organization while
collecting funds.
(Ord. 1994-20, passed 12-8-94)
Penalty, see § 10.99
**** PEDDLING AND SOLICITING ****
§ 111.15 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly indicates or requires a different meaning.
   GOODS, WARES, MERCHANDISE and SERVICES. Includes, but is not limited to
magazines, periodicals, printed matter and advertising space in any
publication.
   REGISTRATION. Not to be construed to be a permit to enter privately-policed
areas.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.16 PEDDLERS AND HAWKERS LICENSE REQUIRED.
   (A)   No person shall engage in the business of hawking or peddling, with or
without a vehicle or handcart, on or about the streets, alleys, hotels,
business houses, private dwellings, or any public or private place in the City,
or offer for sale from house to house or door to door, any jewelry, cutlery,
clocks, medicine, goods, wares, or merchandise of any kind, or any meats, fish,
fruits, produce, ice-cream, or other articles or things, or take orders for
such goods, wares, or other articles or things for immediate or future
delivery, without first having obtained a license therefor as provided in this
title.
   (B)   Nothing in this section shall be construed to apply to any farmer,
gardener, or fruit or vegetable grower engaged in selling the produce of his
own farm, orchard, or vineyard, located in Warrick County, or such a person who
sells or takes orders for goods to be sold and delivered to merchants, traders,
or dealers who purchase the goods for the purpose of resale.
   (C)   Nothing contained in this section shall be construed to apply to news
carriers. No person under the full age of fourteen (14) years of age shall
engage in the business of hawking or peddling under any circumstances, with the
exception of news carriers.
   (D)   No license shall be transferable or shall authorize any other person
than the one named therein to sell thereunder.
   (E)   The word "person" shall mean any human being, partnership, association
or corporation.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.17 BADGE AND VEHICLE SIGN: DISPLAY.
   When any license is issued pursuant to this section, the person to whom the
license is issued shall be supplied by the Zoning Administrator, without
additional charge, with a badge stamped or marked "Licensed Peddler" and it
shall be numbered. The peddler shall keep the badge plainly displayed on the
outside of his/her coat or clothes when engaged in peddling and shall surrender
it on the expiration of the license.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.18 SALES MADE FROM VEHICLE OR HANDCRAFT.
   No person engaged in the business of hawking or peddling, as herein defined,
shall conduct the business from a vehicle or handcraft which is standing in one
location, whether occupied or not, except temporarily and then only for the
purpose of and while actually engaged in the transaction of a sale. Any
structure remaining over five (5) days shall be connected to City water and
sewer.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 111.19 REGISTRATION REQUIREMENT.
   No person over fourteen (14) years of age shall solicit orders from door to
door or on any street or public place within the City for the future delivery
of any goods, wares, merchandise or services, without first registering as
provided in this chapter.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 111.20 APPLICATION FOR PERMISSION TO SOLICIT.
   (A)   An applicant for permission to solicit order hereunder shall complete
an application blank provided by the Zoning Administrator. The application
blank shall contain the following information.
      (1)   The name, home address, and local address, if any, of the
applicant. The applicant must provide the Zoning Administrator with a copy of a
valid merchant certificate issued by the Indiana Department of Revenue to
applicant.
      (2)   A physical description of the applicant, setting forth the
applicant's age, height, weight, and color of hair and eyes. (Example: Driver's
License and/or other picture I.D.)
      (3)   The name and address of the person, firm, or corporation for whom
or through whom orders are to be solicited or cleared, including both the home
address and the address of the office or branch out of which the applicant
works.
      (4)   The nature of the goods, wares, merchandise, or services for which
orders are to be solicited.
      (5)   Written permission from the property owner of the location where
peddler will be set up.
      (6)   A statement as to the period during which the applicant intends to
solicit orders, which shall be not longer than until December 31st of the
calendar year during which the application is made.
      (7)   A certificate of insurance in the sum of five hundred thousand
dollars ($500,000.00) shall be provided to the Zoning Administrator.
   (B)   The applicant, at the time of executing the application blank, shall
also submit identification satisfactory to the Zoning Administrator, which
shall contain a specimen of the applicant's signature and a recent photograph
of the applicant small enough to be attached to his/her application blank or
card.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.21 FEES; APPROVAL.
   (A)   The Zoning Administrator shall issue a license for peddling and
soliciting upon the payment of fees as follows:
      (1)   The applicant has tendered to the Zoning Administrator the sum of
thirty dollars ($30.00) for a period not to exceed one (1) week-end from Friday
through Sunday; or
      (2)   The applicant has tendered to the Zoning Administrator the sum of
one hundred dollars ($100.00) for a period not to exceed one (1) month; or
      (3)   The applicant has tendered to the Zoning Administrator the sum of
one thousand dollars ($1,000.00) for a period not to exceed one (1) year; or
      (4)   All licenses shall terminate on December 31st at 12:00 A.M. in the
year of issue.
   (B)   Upon compliance by the applicant with the provisions of this section,
the Zoning Administrator shall stamp approval on the application blank
submitted by the applicant.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.22 STAMPED CREDENTIALS; NON-TRANSFERABLE.
   Stamped credentials shall be non-transferable and shall entitle the holder
thereof, for the period indicated therein, unless revoked, to solicit orders
within the City for the purchase of the merchandise specified in his/her
application; however, the holder shall have his/her stamped credentials in his/
her possession at all times while soliciting orders and shall exhibit them at
any time upon request by any law enforcement officer of the City or any
purchaser or prospective purchaser. All food vendors are required to have
approval and permit from the Warrick County Health Department.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.23 EXCEPTIONS.
   This subchapter shall not apply to:
   (A)   Salesmen selling goods to retail or wholesale stores for resale.
   (B)   Any person who has, and has had for the preceding twelve (12) months a
regular place of business within the City.
   (C)   Any person employed by or soliciting for any person who has, or has
had for the preceding twelve (12) months, a regular place of business within
the City.
   (D)   Solicitation for or on behalf of churches, schools, or Indiana not-
for-profit corporations.
   (E)   Mail order and catalog sales.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
§ 111.24 REVOCATION OF LICENSE.
   A license issued pursuant to this subchapter may be revoked by the Zoning
Administration after notice and a hearing before the Board of Zoning
Administration, for any of the following causes:
   (A)   Any fraud, misrepresentation, or false statements contained in the
application for the license;
   (B)   Any fraud, misrepresentation, or false statement made in connection
with the selling of goods, wares, or merchandise; or
   (C)   Any violation of this subchapter.
   (D)   Conducting the business licensed under this subchapter in an unlawful
manner or in such a manner as to constitute a breach of the peace or to
constitute a menace to the health, safety, or general welfare of the public.
(Ord. 1994-19, passed 12-22-94; Am. Ord. 2004-3, passed 3-4-04)
Penalty, see § 10.99
CHAPTER 112: AMUSEMENTS
Section
   
112.01   Parades, circuses and the like
§ 112.01 PARADES, CIRCUSES AND THE LIKE.
   (A)   Application for all licenses and permits required by ordinances shall
be made in writing to the City Clerk-Treasurer. Each application shall state
the name of the applicants, the permit or license desired, the location to be
used, if any, the time covered and the fee to be paid; and each application
shall contain such additional information as may be needed for the proper
guidance of the city officials in the issuing of the permit or license applied
for
   (B)   Whenever in this code a license is required for the maintenance,
operation or conduct of any business or establishment, or for doing business or
engaging in any activity or occupations any person or corporation shall be
subject to the requirement if by himself or through an agent's employee or
partner, he holds himself forth as being engaged in the business or occupation,
or solicits patronage therefor, actively or passively, or performs or attempts
to perform any part of such business or occupation in the city or intends to
use the public streets of the city for parades, marches, assemblages of masses
of people, or property for the promotion of projects, ideas or beliefs.
   (C)   Forms for all licenses and permits and applications therefor, shall be
prepared and kept in file by the City Clerk-Treasurer.
   (D)   Each license or permit issued shall bear the signatures of the Mayor
and the City Clerk-Treasurer in the absence of any provision to the contrary.
   (E)   Upon the receipt of any application for a license or permit where
ordinances of the city necessitate an inspection or investigation before the
issuance of such permit or license, the City Clerk-Treasurer shall refer such
application to the proper officer for making such investigation within forty-
eight (48) hours of the time of such investigation or inspection shall make a
report thereon, favorable or otherwise, within ten (10) days after receiving
the application or a copy thereof. The health officer shall make or cause to be
made an inspection in regard to such licenses in the connection of the care or
handling of food and the preventing of nuisances and the spread of disease for
the protection of health; the building inspector shall make or cause to be made
any such inspections relative to the construction of buildings or other
structures. All other investigations except where otherwise provided, shall be
made by the Chief of Police or by some other officer designated by the Mayor
   (F)   In the absence of provision to the contrary, all fees and charges for
licenses or permits shall be paid in advance at the time application therefor
is made to the City Clerk-Treasurer. When an applicant has not engaged in the
business until after the expiration of part of the current license year, the
license fee shall be prorated by quarters and the fee paid for each quarter or
fraction thereof during which the business has been or will be conducted.
Except as otherwise provided, all license fees shall become a part of the
corporate fund.
   (G)   All annual licenses shall terminate on the last day of the fiscal year
of the city where they are issued where no provision to the contrary is made.
   (H)   The location of any licensed business or occupation, or of any
permitted act, may be changed, provided ten (10) days notice is given to the
City Clerk-Treasurer, in the absence of any provision to the contrary,
provided, that the building, zoning and frontage consent requirements of the
ordinances are complied with.
   (I)   No business, licensed or not, shall be so conducted or operated as to
amount to a nuisance in fact.
   (J)   (1)   Whenever inspections of the premises used for or in connection
with the operation of a licensed business or occupation are provided for or
required by ordinance, or are reasonably necessary to secure compliance with
any ordinance provision or to detect violations thereof, it shall be the duty
of the licensee, or the person in charge of the premises to be inspected to
admit thereto for the purpose of making the inspection, any officer or employee
of the city who is authorized or directed to make such inspection at any
reasonable time that admission is requested.
      (2)   Whenever an analysis of any commodity or material is reasonably
necessary to secure conformance with any ordinance provision or to detect
violations thereof, it shall be the duty of the licensee of the municipality
whose business is governed by such provision to give to any authorized officer
or employee of the city requesting the same sufficient samples of such material
or commodity for such analysis upon request. In addition to any other penalty
which may be provided, the Mayor may revoke the license of any licensed
proprietor of any licensed business in the city who refuses to permit any such
officer or employee who is authorized to make such inspection or take such
sample to make the inspection, or take an adequate sample of the said
commodity, or who interferes with such officer or employee while in the
performance of his duty in making such inspection. Provided, that no license
shall be revoked for such cause unless written demand is made upon the licensee
or person in charge of the premises, in the name of the city, stating that such
inspection or sample is desired at the time it is sought to make the inspection
or obtain the sample.
   (K)   It shall be unlawful to conduct any parade, march, assemblages of
masses of people or property for the promotion of projects, ideas or beliefs in
or on any public street, alley or other public way or place in the city, unless
a permit therefor is first obtained from the Chief of Police and the Clerk-
Treasurer of the City as herein provided. Such permit shall specify the route
to be followed, and shall be accompanied by a bond in the sum of Ten Thousand
Dollars ($10,000.00), conditioned to indemnify the city for any loss, damage or
liability incurred or caused by the conduct of such parade, march, assemblage
of masses of people or property.
   (L)   Assemblage of masses of people or property is hereby defined to be an
organized group of people of five (5) or more persons which tends to or is
calculated to cause or promote attention, gathering of spectators or people, to
create a disturbance or a riot.
   (M)   Any license or permit for a limited time may be revoked by the Mayor
at any time during the life of such license or permit for any violation by the
licensee the license or permit or to the premises occupied; such revocation be
in addition to any fine imposed.
(Ord. 1971-10, passed 5-20-71)
Penalty, see § 10.99

CHAPTER 130: GENERAL OFFENSES
Section

130.99   Penalty
§ 130.01 UNLAWFUL NOISE.
   (A)   No person shall make or cause to be made any distinctly and loudly
audible noise that unreasonably annoys, disturbs, injures or endangers the
comfort, repose, health, peace, safety or welfare of any person or precludes
their enjoyment of property or affects their property's value. This general
prohibition is not limited by the specific restrictions of the following
subdivisions.
   (B)   No person shall sound any audible signaling device on any vehicle
except as a warning of danger in the conformance with the traffic laws of the
City.
   (C)   No person shall discharge the exhaust or permit the discharge of the
exhaust of any internal combustion engine except through a muffler or other
device that effectively prevents loud or explosive noises therefrom and
complies with all applicable City or State laws and regulations.
   (D)   No person shall use any vehicle so out of repair or so loaded as to
create loud and unnecessary grating, grinding rattling or other noise.
   (E)   No person shall create loud and excessive noise in loading, unloading
or unpacking any vehicles.
   (F)   No person operating or occupying a motor vehicle on a street, highway,
alley, parking lot or driveway, whether public or private property, shall
operate or permit the operation of any sound amplification system from within
the vehicle so that the sound is plainly audible at a distance of 50 or more
feet from the vehicle.
      (1)   Sound amplification system means any radio, tape player, compact
disc, loud speaker or other electronic device used for the amplification of
sound.
      (2)   Plainly audible means any sound produced by a sound amplification
system from a vehicle, which clearly can be heard at a distance of 50 feet or
more. Measurement standards shall be by the auditory senses, based on the
direct line of sight. Words or phrases need not be discernible and bass
reverberations are included. The motor vehicle may be stopped, standing, parked
or moving on a street, highway, alley, parking lot or driveway on either public
or private property.
      (3)   It is an affirmative defense to a charge under this section that
the operator was not otherwise prohibited by law from operating the sound
amplification system and that any of the following apply:
         (a)   The system was being operated to request medical or vehicular
assistance or to warn of a hazardous road condition;
         (b)   The vehicle was an emergency public safety vehicle; and
         (c)   The system or vehicle was used in an authorized public activity
such as a parade, fireworks, sporting events, musical production or other
activities which have the approval of the governing body or a department of the
City authorized to grant such approval.
(Ord. 1998-20, passed 11-23-98)
Penalty, see § 10.99
Cross-reference:
   Traffic Regulations, see
Chapter 70
§ 130.02 SIGNAGE ON UTILITY POLES.
   (A)   No person, firm, association, business or corporation shall permit or
cause to attach to any utility pole, City property or City right-of-way of the
City of Boonville, Indiana, any posters, signs, notices, papers and
advertisements.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of the chief of police to serve or cause to be
served a notice upon the person, firm, association, business or corporation who
has permitted such posters signs, notices, papers and advertisements or who has
caused said posters, signs, notices, papers or advertisements to be placed or
attached upon the utility pole or poles, City property or City right of way of
the City in violation of the provisions of this section and to demand the
abatement of said posters, signs, notices, papers or advertisements and nails,
tacks or staples immediately on same day of placement.
   (C)   If the person so served does not abate the nuisance of said posters,
signs, notices, papers and advertisements on the same day of placement the
chief of police may proceed to abate such nuisance, keeping an account of the
expense of the abatement, and such expense shall be charged and paid by such
owner or occupant.
(Ord. 1972-2, passed 5-4-72; Am. Ord. 1998-5, passed 5-14-98)
Penalty, see § 10.99
Cross-reference:
   Business Regulations, see
Title XI
§ 130.03 POSSESSION OF ALCOHOL ON PUBLIC PROPERTY.
   (A)   The possession or use of beer, wine or liquor while within the
confines of any property identified in division (B) is prohibited.
   (B)   Those properties subject to division (A) are all properties owned by
the City or any of its utilities, departments or boards, except City Hall.
   (C)   The City has acquired Quail Crossing Golf Club, which is now a City
owned and operated venture. It is appropriate for the City to sell and allow
the responsible use of alcohol on its property. Ordinance 1990-12 previously
prohibited the possession, sale and use of alcohol on all City properties.
Accordingly Ordinance 1990-12 is amended as specified herein.
(Ord. 1990-12, passed 5-24-90; Am. Ord. 2017-21, passed 11-2-17)   
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 130.04 FIREARMS; DISCHARGE AND POSSESSION.
   No minor under the age of sixteen (16) years shall handle, use or have in
his possession, or under his control, in any public, place within the corporate
limits of the City of Boonville, Indiana, except while accompanied by or under
the immediate charge of his parent or legal guardian, any firearm of any kind,
including air rifles and bee bee guns.
(Ord. 1966-1, passed 2-3-66)
Penalty, see § 10.99
§ 130.05 CURFEW.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly indicates or requires a
different meaning.
      CHILD.  Has the same meaning as JUVENILE , and vice versa.
      CURFEW VIOLATION. The presence of:
         (a)   A child/juvenile fifteen (15), sixteen (16), or seventeen (17)
years of age in a public place: (i) between 1:00 a.m. and 5:00 a.m. on Saturday
or Sunday; (ii) after 11:00 p.m. on Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday,
Thursday, or (iii) before 5:00 am on Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday or
Friday without Parent (Custodian), Guardian or responsible adult.
         (b)   A child/juvenile under fifteen (15) years of age in a public
place: (i) after 11:00 p.m. on before 5:00 a.m. on any day.
      All items being prevailing local time for City of Boonville, Warrick
County, Indiana.
      CUSTODIAN. A parent or other person who has legal or physical custody
over a child/juvenile, whether by parentage, court order, practice, or custom.
      EMERGENCY. An unforeseen combination of circumstances or the resulting
state that calls for immediate action. The term includes, but is not limited
to, a fire, natural disaster, or automobile accident or any situation requiring
immediate action to prevent serious bodily injury or loss of life.
      ESTABLISHMENT. Any privately-owned place of business operated for a
profit to which the public is invited, including but not limited to any place
of amusement or entertainment.
      GUARDIAN.  
         (a)   A person who, under court order, is the guardian of the person
of a child/juvenile; or
         (b)   A public or private agency with whom a child/juvenile has been
placed by a court.
      JUVENILE. Any person less than 18 years of age, or the status or
circumstance of being under 18 years of age.
      OPERATOR. Any individual, firm, association, partnership, company, or
corporation operating, managing, or conducting any establishment. The term
includes the manager of managing partners of an association or partnership or
company and the officers of a corporation.
      PARENT. A person who is:
         (a)   A natural parent, adoptive parent, or step-parent of a child/
juvenile; and
         (b)   At least 18 years of age and authorized by law to have the care
and custody of a child/ juvenile.
      PUBLIC PLACE. Any place to which the public or a substantial group of the
public has access and includes, but is not limited to, streets, highways and
the common areas of schools, hospitals, apartment houses, office buildings,
transport facilities, shops and businesses.
      REMAIN. To:
         (a)   Linger or stay; or
         (b)   Fail to leave the public place or establishment when requested
to do so by a law enforcement officer of the owner, operator, or other person
in control of an establishment.
      SERIOUS BODILY INJURY. Bodily injury that creates a substantial risk of
death or that causes death, serious permanent disfigurement, or protracted loss
or impairment of the function of any bodily member or organ.
   (B)   Offenses/violations/prohibited acts. Each violation of a provision of
this section shall be deemed a separate offense, subject to sanction,
enforcement, and penalties as herein prescribed. Offenses under this section
are defined as follows:
      (1)   A child/juvenile commits an offense/violation under this section,
by virtue of a curfew violation, as defined in division (A) of this section.
      (2)   It is a defense to a violation of this section if:
         (a)   The child/juvenile engaged in the prohibited conduct while:
            1.   Accompanied by his or her parent, guardian, or custodian, or
responsible adult; or
            2.   Accompanied by an adult specified by his or her parent,
guardian, or custodian; or
         (b)   The child/juvenile was emancipated:
            1.   Under I.C. 31-37-19-27 or I.C. 31-6-4-15.7 (before its
repeal);
            2.   By virtue of having married; or
            3.   In accordance with the laws of another state or jurisdiction.
      (3)   A parent, guardian, or custodian of a child/juvenile commits an
offense/violation under this section if he or she knowingly permits, or by
insufficient control allows, the child/juvenile to commit a curfew violation by
remaining in any public place or on the premises of any establishment within
the incorporated areas of Boonville City, Indiana in violation of division (B)
(1) of this section.
   (C)   Enforcement. Before taking any enforcement action under this section,
a law enforcement officer, to include officers of the Boonville Police
Department or other law enforcement officers, shall determine the apparent
offending child's/juvenile's age and reason for being in the public place or at
an establishment if reasonably possible. The law enforcement officer shall not
issue a citation or make an arrest under this section unless the law
enforcement officer reasonably believes that an offense has occurred and that,
based on any response and other circumstances, none of the factors referenced
as parts of division (B)(2) of this Section are present.
   (D)   Continuing education fund. All monies collected as fines assessed as
described in §
130.99 shall be deposited to and used for the purposes of continuing education
for the Boonville Police Department, unless such shall be prohibited by law, in
which circumstance such shall be deposited to and used for the purposes
permitted by the General Fund of Boonville City Government.
(Ord. 2012-17, passed 10-4-12) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.06 SYNTHETIC CANNABINOIDS.
   (A)   This section is adopted pursuant to I.C. 36-1-3-1.
   (B)   Products containing synthetic cannabinoids ("Products"), such as K2 or
Spice, or similar products which contain one or more of the following chemical
compounds:
      (1)   (6aR, 10aR)-9-(hydroxymethyl)-6, 6-dimethyl-3-(2memyloctan-2-yl)-
6a,7, 10, 10a- tetrahydrobenzo[c]chromen-1-ol, also known as HU-210;
      (2)   l-Pentyl-3-(l-naphthoyl)indole, also known as JWH-018;
      (3)   l-Butyl-3-(l-napthoyl)indoIe, also known as JWH-073; or
      (4)   All parts of the plant presently classified botanically as Salvia
divinorum, whether growing or not, any extract from any part of such plant, and
every compound, manufacture, salts derivative, mixture or preparation of such
plant, its seeds or its extracts;
      (5)   TFMPP, a substance also known as l-(3-[trifluoromethylphenyl])
piperazine;
      (6)   Cannabicyclohexanol, a substance also know as 2-[(lR,3S)-3-
hydroxycyclohexyl]- 5-(methylnonan-2-yl)phenol; or
      (7)   Any other relevant compound or derivative
      Shall not be sold, marketed, or offered for sale within Boonville,
Indiana; and
   (C)   Products containing synthetic camiabinoids ("Products") may not be
burned, incinerated, or ignited in any public place or on any property owned,
leased or controlled by the City of Boonville.
   (D)   It is unlawful for any business or retail establishment to sell, to
publicly display for sale, or to attempt to sale, give or barter synthetic
marijuana or synthetic equivalents of substances contained in the plant
Cannabis, or in the resinous extractives of Cannabis and/or synthetic
substances, derivatives, and their isomers with similar chemical structure and
pharmacological activity or any substance which claims such properties even if
in actuality they do not. Since nomenclature of these substances is no
internationally standardized, compounds of these structures, regardless of
numerical designation of atomic positions, are similarly prohibited.
   (E)   Persons violating the provisions of this section shall be subject to
the penalty imposed for violating a Class B infraction, as set forth in I.C.
34-28-5-4, and shall be subject to a minimum fine of $500.00, plus court costs.
Each day of the existence of any violation of this section shall be considered
a separate offense.
   (F)   Entities, including businesses and retail establishments, violating
the provisions of this section shall be subject to the penalty imposed for
violating a Class B infraction, as set forth in I.C. 34-28-5-4, and shall be
subject to a minimum fine of $2,500.00, plus court costs. Each day of the
existence of any violation of this section shall be considered a separate
offense.
   (G)   Any person found to be in possession of the aforementioned substances
shall be subject to the penalty imposed for violating a Class C infraction, as
set forth in I.C. 34-28-5-4, and shall be subject to a minimum fine of $250.00,
plus court costs. Further, if any of the aforementioned substances are found in
the possession of any person, they may be confiscated and destroyed by any law
enforcement official as provided by law. Each day of the existence of any
violation of this section shall be considered a separate offense.
   (H)   This section shall be enforced by the provisions of I.C. 34-28-5.
   (I)   Any fines collected under this section shall be placed in the City
General Fund.
(Ord. 2010-8, passed 10-7-10)
§ 130.07 LIVING IN A RECREATIONAL VEHICLE.
   (A)   RECREATIONAL VEHICLE means a vehicle with or without motive power
equipped exclusively for living quarters for persons traveling upon the
highways. RECREATIONAL VEHICLE does not include a mobile structure as defined
in I.C. 22-12-1-17.
   (B)   A person may not live in a recreational vehicle or a non-permanent
structure for a period in excess of fourteen (14) days within a calendar year
in the City of Boonville, Indiana.
   (C)   The Board of Zoning Appeals is given authority to grant a variance and
except a person from the enforcement of this section. If the Board of Zoning
Appeals grants a variance, then the Board shall determine the granting of a
variance is in the best interest of the citizens of Boonville, Indiana.
(Ord. 2008-8, passed 8-7-08; Am. Ord. 2023-5, passed 4-20-23)  Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   The custodial parent or legal guardian of any child who commits a
curfew violation, as defined in §
130.05, violates §
130.05 and shall be subject to the following fines:
      (1)   Twenty-five dollars ($25.00) for a first offense;
      (2)   One hundred dollars ($100.00) for a second offense, plus court
cost; or
      (3)   Two hundred dollars ($200.00) for a third offense and every
subsequent offense plus court costs.
   (B)   Violation of §
130.07 may be punishable by a civil penalty not exceeding two thousand five
hundred dollars ($2,500.00) as prescribed in §
10.99 granted under I.C. 36-1-3-8(a)(10) and each event shall be deemed a
separate violation.
(Ord. 2008-8, passed 8-7-08; Am. Ord. 2012-17, passed 10-4-12; Am. Ord. 2023-5,
passed 4-20-23)
